Within each band prove: uncentered range, centered core, reflection to all lengths, then extra punctures by \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:D.5}{(69)}. Comparison uses the already proved uncentered range with positive margin; other children use strictly smaller completed bands.

Make every terminal loss (floor, diagonal, exceptional) \(<\epsilon/4\) by choosing \(\rho,\sigma\). Choose padding and local divisor/Schwartz losses \(\ll\epsilon/D\). Triangle takes the maximum of child exponents after counting mass; Cauchy averages them. Each branch incurs one terminal loss plus edge losses.

Measure powers before dyadic rounding. Use joint log boxes, selecting whole frequency dyads from the outer box's scale supremum with padding \(Z^{\xi/2}\), independently of live columns. Discarded genuine Poisson arguments are \(\ge Z^{\xi/4}\). Bounds \(|\mathfrak g(a,h)|\le\sqrt{q_a}\), \(|F(u,v;j)|\le q_uq_v\) and polynomial counts make tails arbitrarily small by Schwartz decay \textbf{before} separation.

Retained \(\widehat\Phi(R\exp L(x))\) has uniform Euler derivatives for \(R>0\) and a common Fourier measure of finite weighted \(L^1\) norm. Remove central powers before inverse-root separation. Finite-depth support enlargements cost \(O(1/\log Z)\); rescale subunit annuli only in positive child norms, preserving formal centered scales. Formulas \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:D.10}{(74)}, \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:A.16}{(12)}, \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:D.22}{(86)} preserve character/common-mask closure with polynomial norms. Choose seminorm and height orders backwards, then thresholds and external tail orders. This proves \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:D.3}{(67)}. \(\square\)

No moving twist and two identical profiles give \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:3.3}{(96)}; restricting the positive row sum to sixth-power-free rows decreases it. Conjugation gives the inverse orientation.

\hypertarget{cf:moment-corollaries}{}

\label{cf:moment-corollaries}

\hypertarget{coefficient-specific-corollaries-and-sixth-power-amplification}{%
\paragraph{5.8 Coefficient-specific corollaries and sixth-power amplification}\label{coefficient-specific-corollaries-and-sixth-power-amplification}}

For a fixed finite-ray character \(\nu\), let \(\varphi_u=\nu\psi_u^{\pm1}\), with its original zeros, and define

\hypertarget{cf:eq:3.1}{}

\label{cf:eq:3.1}

\[
M_u(D;W)=D^{-1/2}\sum_n\mu(n)\varphi_u(n)W(q_n/D),\qquad
 S_u(N;W)=N^{-1/2}\sum_n\varphi_u(n)W(q_n/N).
\qquad\text{(94)}
\]

The preceding theorems imply the following two coefficient-specific estimates:

\hypertarget{cf:eq:3.2}{}

\label{cf:eq:3.2}

\[
\sum_{0<q_u\le H}|M_u(D;W)|^2\ll H(HD)^\varepsilon\|W\|_{C^J}^2,
 \qquad H\ge D^{1+\vartheta},\quad \vartheta>0;
\qquad\text{(95)}
\]

\hypertarget{cf:eq:3.3}{}

\label{cf:eq:3.3}

\[
\sum_{\substack{q_u\asymp U\text{ sixth-power-free}\\\varphi_u^*\ne1}}
 |S_u(U^m;W)|^4\ll U^{1+\varepsilon}\|W\|_{C^J}^4.
\qquad\text{(96)}
\]

Here \(m\ge0\) is bounded and \(\varphi_u^*\) is the primitive inducing character. Equations \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:3.2}{(95)}, \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:3.3}{(96)} follow from Theorems 4.1 and 5.1, with §\protect\hyperlink{cf:sec:1.2}{1.3}'s uniformity and no zero-free assumption; \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:3.2}{(95)} includes sixth-power rows.

Sixth-power amplification extends \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:3.2}{(95)} to longer inverse polynomials. The exact reconstruction identity is

\hypertarget{cf:eq:3.4}{}

\label{cf:eq:3.4}

\[
M_u(D;W)=\sum_{d\mid\operatorname{rad}(a)}
 \mu(d)\varphi_u(d)q_d^{-1/2}M_{ua^6}(D/q_d;W).
\qquad\text{(97)}
\]

Separate squarefree column primes dividing \(a\) to prove \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:3.4}{(97)}, retaining shared \(u,a\) zeros. Its coefficient mass is \(\ll q_a^\varepsilon\). Log-scale differentiation sends \(W\) to \(-W/2-yW'\), so Sobolev and \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:3.2}{(95)} give

\hypertarget{cf:eq:3.5}{}

\label{cf:eq:3.5}

\[
\sum_{0<q_v\ll H}\sup_{0<E\le D}|M_v(E;W)|^2\ll H(HD)^\varepsilon,
 \qquad H\ge D^{1+\vartheta}.
\qquad\text{(98)}
\]

Only \(O(\log(2+D))\) log intervals occur; subunit scales have boundedly many columns and eventually none.

For sixth-power-free \(q_u\asymp U\), put \(H=\max(2U,D^{1+\vartheta})\) and average \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:3.4}{(97)} over the \(\gg_S(H/U)^{1/6}\) primary ideals \(q_a\le(H/U)^{1/6}\) prime to \(S\). The map \((u,a)\mapsto ua^6\) is injective: valuations modulo six recover \(u\), primary generators its unit. Equation \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:3.5}{(98)} gives \(\sum^{(6)}|M_u(D)|^2\ll U^{1/6}H^{5/6}(UD)^\varepsilon\). For bounded \(r\), take \(D=U^r\) and small \(\vartheta\):

\hypertarget{cf:eq:3.6}{}

\label{cf:eq:3.6}

\[
\sum_{q_u\asymp U}^{(6)}|M_u(U^r;W)|^2
 \ll U^{e(r)+\varepsilon},\qquad e(r)=\max\{1,(1+5r)/6\}.
\qquad\text{(99)}
\]

\hypertarget{cf:probe-arithmetic}{}

\label{cf:probe-arithmetic}

\hypertarget{the-exact-poisson-series-of-the-common-probe}{%
\subsubsection{6 The exact Poisson series of the common probe}\label{the-exact-poisson-series-of-the-common-probe}}

Here \(X,Y,Z>0\) are independent. This unmarked probe supplies the exact coefficient interface; compensated or affine probes must derive their combinations from it.

Fix primitive \(\eta\) and let \(b_*\) generate the product of \(S\). Choose primitive \(\xi\bmod b_*\), quadratic at odd residue characteristic and cubic at norm four; thus \(\xi^6=1\) on units. Put

\[
\tau=q_{b_*}^{-1/2}g_\xi(b_*,1),\qquad
 \Xi(A)=\xi(A)\chi_A(b_*),\qquad |\tau|=1.
\]

Choose nonnegative nonzero annular weights \(W_0,W_1,w\), and set

\hypertarget{cf:eq:2.2}{}

\label{cf:eq:2.2}

\[
V_G(r)=\frac{e^{-(\log r)^2/4}}{2\sqrt\pi},\qquad \widehat V_G(t)=e^{t^2},
\qquad\text{(100)}
\]

The ideals \(c,n\) may share primes. With \(A=cn^3\), define

\hypertarget{cf:eq:2.3}{}

\label{cf:eq:2.3}

\[
T_{m,a,\eta}(t)=\sum_{c\ {\rm sf},n}
 \gamma_2(c)\overline{\alpha(A)G(A)}\eta(A)\Xi(A)^{-1}R(A,a)
 \chi_A(m)q_c^{-1/2-t}q_n^{-1-3t},
\qquad\text{(101)}
\]

\hypertarget{cf:eq:2.4}{}

\label{cf:eq:2.4}

\[
\begin{aligned}
 I_\eta(X,Y,Z)=\frac1Y\sum_a
 &\frac{W_1(q_a/Y)\chi_a(b_*)}{\tau\xi(a)\sqrt{q_{b_*}q_aX}}
 \sum_m\xi(m)q_a^{-1/2}g_{\chi_a}(a,-m)
 W_0\!\left(\frac{q_m}{q_{b_*}q_aX}\right)\\
 &\times\frac1{2\pi i}\int_{(4)}Z^te^{t^2}T_{m,a,\eta}(t)\,dt.
\end{aligned}
\qquad\text{(102)}
\]

Annular weights make \(a,m\) finite, \(m\ne0\); remaining sums/integrals converge absolutely. Expand \(\overline G\) on its ray group and partition \(a\) into classes \(\sigma\) for \(R(A,a)\). Then \(B_{m,\sigma}(Z)\) is a fixed finite combination of \protect\hyperlink{cf:completed-definition}{(16)} with \(V_G\). Put \(Q_0=q_{b_*}XY\) and fix \(\Omega=1\) on \(\operatorname{supp}W_0\operatorname{supp}W_1\). Mellin inversion gives a finite sum of

\hypertarget{cf:eq:2.6}{}

\label{cf:eq:2.6}

\[
\frac{Q_0^{-1/2}}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbf R}\widehat W_0(iv)
 \sum_{m\ne0}\Omega(q_m/Q_0)\xi(m)(q_m/Q_0)^{-iv}
 A_{m,\sigma,v}(Y)B_{m,\sigma}(Z)\,dv,
\qquad\text{(103)}
\]

where

\hypertarget{cf:eq:2.7}{}

\label{cf:eq:2.7}

\[
A_{m,\sigma,v}(Y)=Y^{-3/2}\sum_{a\in\sigma}
 \frac{\chi_a(b_*)}{\tau\xi(a)}W_1(q_a/Y)(q_a/Y)^{-1+iv}g_{\chi_a}(a,-m).
\qquad\text{(104)}
\]

The normalization follows from \(q_m/(q_{b_*}q_aX)=(q_m/Q_0)/(q_a/Y)\).

Let \(\widetilde W_0(|y|^2)\) be the planar Fourier transform of \(W_0(|z|^2)\), and \(\mathcal M(z)=\int_0^\infty\widetilde W_0(r)r^{z-1}\,dr\), holomorphic for \(\Re z>0\) and rapidly decreasing vertically on compact substrips.

\textbf{Proposition 6.1} (Exact common probe series). \hypertarget{cf:exact-probe-series}{}\label{cf:exact-probe-series}

\hypertarget{cf:eq:2.8}{}

\label{cf:eq:2.8}

\[
\begin{aligned}
 I_\eta(X,Y,Z)=\frac1{(2\pi i)^3}\int_{(3)}\int_{(3)}\int_{(2)}
 &X^{1/2-z}Z^{s+z-1}Y^{w-1}e^{(s+z-1)^2}
 \mathcal M(z)\widehat W_1(w)\\
 &\times\sum_u^{(6)}q_u^{-z}\overline{\xi(u)}
 \frac{\zeta_K^S(6z)L_K^S(w,\psi_u)}{L_K^S(s,\eta\overline{\psi_u})}
 H_{\eta,u}(s,w,z)\,dz\,dw\,ds.
\end{aligned}
\qquad\text{(105)}
\]

\emph{Here \(u\ne0\) is prime to \(S\), including all units.}

Section \protect\hyperlink{cf:sec:A.3}{6.2} derives the local factors \(H_{\mathfrak p}\) and full/prime-divisible series \(P_{\mathfrak p},P_{\mathfrak p}^*\).

\emph{Proof.}

\hypertarget{cf:sec:A.2}{}

\label{cf:sec:A.2}

\hypertarget{the-finite-transform-and-phase-cancellation}{%
\paragraph{6.1 The finite transform and phase cancellation}\label{the-finite-transform-and-phase-cancellation}}

Expanding the Gauss sum in the physical row gives the finite coefficient

\hypertarget{cf:eq:A.3}{}

\label{cf:eq:A.3}

\[
\mathcal F(a,A,h)=
\sum_{\substack{d\bmod a\\h\equiv b_*Ad\pmod a}}
\chi_a(d)\,g_{\xi\chi_A}\!\left(b_*A,\frac{h-b_*Ad}{a}\right).
\qquad\text{(106)}
\]

The finite transform of \(\xi(m)\chi_A(m)q_a^{-1/2}g_{\chi_a}(a,-m)\) modulo full \(ab_*A\) equals \(q_a^{1/2}\mathcal F(a,A,h)\): summing lifts modulo \(b_*A\) imposes the congruence, including common primes of \(a,A\).

Poisson at scale \(q_{b_*}q_aX\) gives factor \(X/q_A\) and argument \(Xq_h/q_A\). Combining \(q_a^{1/2}\) with \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:2.4}{(102)}'s square root leaves \(X^{1/2}/(\sqrt{q_{b_*}}q_A)\) and the character/\(Y^{-1}\) factors. Primitivity at \(b_*\) kills \((h,S)>1\), including \(h=0\). Write uniquely \(h=ur^6\), \(u\) sixth-power-free, \(r\) primary.

Mellin inversion of the two radial weights, followed by \(s=t+1-z\), gives the row Dirichlet series

\hypertarget{cf:eq:A.4}{}

\label{cf:eq:A.4}

\[
\mathcal F_{\eta,u}(s,w,z)=
\sum_{c\ {\rm sf},n,a,r} K_\eta(c,n,a,r;u)
q_c^{-1/2-s}q_n^{-1-3s}q_a^{-w}q_r^{-6z},
\qquad\text{(107)}
\]

where

\hypertarget{cf:eq:A.5}{}

\label{cf:eq:A.5}

\[
K_\eta=
\frac{\mathcal F(a,A,ur^6)\chi_a(b_*)}
{\sqrt{q_{b_*}}\tau\xi(a)\overline{\xi(u)}}
\gamma_2(c)\overline{\alpha(A)G(A)}\eta(A)\Xi(A)^{-1}R(A,a).
\qquad\text{(108)}
\]

The remaining row factor is \(q_u^{-z}\overline{\xi(u)}\), and the global weight is

\[
X^{1/2-z}Z^{s+z-1}Y^{w-1}e^{(s+z-1)^2}\mathcal M(z)\widehat W_1(w).
\]

Interchanges are absolute at \((\Re s,\Re w,\Re z)=(3,3,2)\) by \(|\mathcal F(a,A,h)|\le q_aq_{b_*A}\), counting and Mellin decay. We factor \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:A.4}{(107)}.

At good \(p\), put \(Q=q_p\), \(e_0=v_p(c)\in\{0,1\}\), \(l=v_p(n)\), \(k=v_p(a)\), \(m=v_p(r)\), \(t=e_0+3l\), \(j=v_p(u)\), \(j'=j+6m\), \(\epsilon_p=\chi_p(-1)\). The local convolution is

\hypertarget{cf:eq:A.6}{}

\label{cf:eq:A.6}

\[
C_{\mathfrak p}(t,k,j')=
\sum_{\substack{d\bmod\mathfrak p^k\\\mathfrak p^{j'}\equiv\mathfrak p^td\pmod{\mathfrak p^k}}}
\chi_{\mathfrak p}(d)^k
g_{\chi_{\mathfrak p}^t}\!\left(\mathfrak p^t,
\frac{\mathfrak p^{j'}-\mathfrak p^td}{\mathfrak p^k}\right).
\qquad\text{(109)}
\]

At \(k=0\) the absent outer character is one; at \(t=0\) the Gauss factor is one. Residue lifting gives

\hypertarget{cf:eq:A.7}{}

\label{cf:eq:A.7}

\[
C_p(t,k,j')=
\begin{cases}
1,&t=k=0,\\
\mathbf1_{j'=0},&t=0,\ k>0,\\
g_{\chi_p^t}(p^t,p^{j'}),&t>0,\ k=0,\\
\epsilon_p^{-1}g_{\chi_p}(p,1)g_{\chi_p^{t-1}}(p^t,p^{j'-1}),
&t>0,\ k=1,\ j'\ge1,\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.
\end{cases}
\qquad\text{(110)}
\]

For case four, expanding the Gauss sum and summing \(d\bmod p\) changes exponent \(t\) to \(t-1\). For \(k\ge2\), the final \(d\)-lift preserves its character but has nontrivial additive phase, hence cancels. Principal local characters retain their nonunit zeros.

For unit parts \(h_p=h/p^{j'}\), \(a_p^\circ=a/p^k\), \(A_p^\circ=A/p^t\), CRT and \(d=h_p(b_*A_p^\circ)^{-1}d'\) give

\[
\chi_p(h_p)^{k-t}\chi_p(a_p^\circ)^t
\chi_p(A_p^\circ)^{t-k}\chi_p(b_*)^{t-k}.
\]

Put \(\rho=\chi_p(u/p^j)\). The \(b_*\) Gauss factor is \(\sqrt{q_{b_*}}\tau\xi(A)\overline{\xi(h/a)}\), with \(\overline{\xi(h/a)}=\overline{\xi(u)}\xi(a)\) since \(\xi^6=1\). Thus \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:A.5}{(108)}'s fixed \(\xi\) factors cancel; the preceding \(b_*\) factors cancel \(\chi_a(b_*)\chi_A(b_*)^{-1}\).

For distinct good primes, reciprocity gives

\[
R(A,a)\prod_p\chi_p(a_p^\circ)^{t_p}\chi_p(A_p^\circ)^{-k_p}
=\prod_pR(p,p)^{t_pk_p}.
\]

Use \(\gamma_2(c)=\mu(c)\alpha(c)G(c)/\gamma_1(c)\) and CRT for \(\gamma_1(c)\). At \(p<q\), the remaining exponent is \(t_pt_q-e_pe_q=3(e_pl_q+e_ql_p+3l_pl_q)\); its sign product is

\[
\prod_{p<q}R(p,q)^{e_pl_q+e_ql_p+l_pl_q}
=R(c,n)G(n^3)\prod_p\overline{G(p^3)}^{\,l_p}
R(p,p)^{-e_pl_p-l_p(l_p-1)/2}.
\]

Expand \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:A.2}{(7)} to verify this. Then \(G(c)R(c,n)G(n^3)=G(cn^3)\) cancels the conjugate phase; \(G(p^3)=\gamma_3(p)\) and \(R(p,p)=\epsilon_p\). The coefficient therefore factors at every prime without ray restriction.

\hypertarget{cf:sec:A.3}{}

\label{cf:sec:A.3}

\hypertarget{local-euler-factors}{%
\paragraph{6.2 Local Euler factors}\label{local-euler-factors}}

At \(\mathfrak p\notin S\), put

\[
Q=q_{\mathfrak p},\quad j=v_{\mathfrak p}(u)\in\{0,\ldots,5\},\quad
\rho=\chi_{\mathfrak p}(u/\mathfrak p^j),
\]

\[
a_{\mathfrak p}=\overline{\alpha(\mathfrak p)}^3\eta(\mathfrak p)^3,
\quad b_{\mathfrak p}=\overline{\alpha(\mathfrak p)}^2\eta(\mathfrak p)^2
\overline{\chi_{\mathfrak p}(4)},\quad b_{\mathfrak p}^3=a_{\mathfrak p}^2,
\]

\hypertarget{cf:eq:A.8}{}

\label{cf:eq:A.8}

\[
V=Q^{-6z},\quad R=a_{\mathfrak p}^2Q^{4-6s-6z},\quad
W^\circ=\rho Q^{-w},\quad
W=\chi_{\mathfrak p}(u)Q^{-w},\quad
D=\eta(\mathfrak p)\overline{\chi_{\mathfrak p}(u)}Q^{-s}.
\qquad\text{(111)}
\]

For \(j>0\), \(W=D=0\) while \(W^\circ\) retains its unit phase. The marked and full series are

\[
P_{\mathfrak p}^*=\frac1{1-R}
\left\{\frac{R(1-Q^{-1})-\eta(\mathfrak p)(Q-1)Q^{-s-w}V^{\mathbf1_{j\le1}}}{1-V}+J_j\right\},
\]

\hypertarget{cf:eq:A.9}{}

\label{cf:eq:A.9}

\[
P_{\mathfrak p}=\frac1{1-V}+\mathbf1_{j=0}\frac{W^\circ}{1-W^\circ}+P_{\mathfrak p}^*,
\qquad
H_{\mathfrak p}=P_{\mathfrak p}\frac{(1-V)(1-W)}{1-D},
\quad H_{\eta,u}=\prod_{\mathfrak p\notin S}H_{\mathfrak p},
\qquad\text{(112)}
\]

with

\[
\begin{array}{c|l}
j&J_j\\\hline
0&-\eta(\mathfrak p)\rho^{-1}Q^{-s}+W^\circ R\\
1&\eta(\mathfrak p)Q^{-s-w}\\
2&a_{\mathfrak p}\rho^{-3}Q^{3/2-3s}\\
3&-\eta(\mathfrak p)b_{\mathfrak p}\rho^{-2}Q^{2-3s-w}+b_{\mathfrak p}^2\rho^{-4}Q^{2-4s}\\
4&-\eta(\mathfrak p)a_{\mathfrak p}\rho^{-3}Q^{5/2-4s-w}\\
5&-a_{\mathfrak p}^2Q^{3-6s}
\end{array}
\]

The indicator exponent gives \(V\) for \(j=0,1\), otherwise one.

Equivalently, using \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:A.8}{(111)},

\hypertarget{cf:eq:A.10}{}

\label{cf:eq:A.10}

\[
\begin{aligned}
P_p=\sum_{\substack{e_0=0,1\\l,k,m\ge0}}
&(-1)^{e_0}\gamma_1(p)^{-e_0}\eta(p)^{e_0}
\bigl(a_p\overline{\gamma_3(p)}\bigr)^l
\rho^{k-t}\epsilon_p^{tk-e_0l-l(l-1)/2}\\
&\times C_p(t,k,j+6m)
Q^{-(s+1/2)e_0-(1+3s)l-wk-6zm},\qquad t=e_0+3l.
\end{aligned}
\qquad\text{(113)}
\]

Restricting to \(t>0\) gives the marked series, with families

\hypertarget{cf:eq:A.11}{}

\label{cf:eq:A.11}

\[
\begin{array}{c|cc}
 t&k=0&k=1\\\hline
 6r+6&j'\ge t-1&j'=t\\
 6r+1&j'=t-1&j'\ge t\\
 6r+3&j'=t-1&j'=t\\
 6r+4&j'=t-1&j'=t
\end{array}\qquad r\ge0.
\qquad\text{(114)}
\]

Residue lifting supports a nonprincipal Gauss sum exactly at valuation \(t-1\); a principal one has its negative boundary there and value \(Q^t-Q^{t-1}\) above. In \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:A.7}{(110)}, use exponents \(t\) at \(k=0\) and \(t-1\) at \(k=1\).

Insert \(j'=j+6m\). Increasing \(r\) and first allowed \(m\) by one multiplies by \(R\); further \(m\) increments multiply by \(V\). The strict tails are

\[
\frac{R(1-Q^{-1})}{(1-R)(1-V)},\qquad
 -\frac{\eta(p)(Q-1)Q^{-s-w}V^{\mathbf1_{j\le1}}}{(1-R)(1-V)}.
\]

The second starts at \(m=r+1\) for \(j\le1\), otherwise \(m=r\). Equality cases give \(J_j\) by \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:A.1}{(6)}: e.g.~\(j=2\) from \((t,k,m)=(3,0,0)\) and \(j=3\) from \((3,1,0),(4,0,0)\). The restored cube phase is \(\overline{\gamma_3(p)}\), retaining parity through \(\gamma_3(p)^2=\chi_p(-1)\).

For \(t=0\), \(k=0\) gives \((1-V)^{-1}\); \(k>0\) requires \(j=m=0\) and gives \(W^\circ/(1-W^\circ)\). Thus \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:A.9}{(112)} holds in every valuation, including collisions. Extracting \((1-V)^{-1},(1-W)^{-1},1-D\) gives

\[
\mathcal F_{\eta,u}(s,w,z)=
 \frac{\zeta_K^S(6z)L_K^S(w,\psi_u)}{L_K^S(s,\eta\overline{\psi_u})}
 H_{\eta,u}(s,w,z).
\]

Together with Section~\protect\hyperlink{cf:sec:A.2}{6.1} this proves \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:2.8}{(105)}.~\(\square\)

\hypertarget{cf:interfaces}{}

\label{cf:interfaces}

\hypertarget{interfaces-retained-for-later-chapters}{%
\paragraph{6.3 Interfaces retained for later chapters}\label{interfaces-retained-for-later-chapters}}

The retained contracts are: \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:B.13}{(29)}, arbitrary squarefree frozen \(f\) at cost \(q_f\); \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:C.1}{(45)}, exact Möbius coefficients, common profile and power gap; \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:D.3}{(67)}, two factors with common character, natural zeros, common puncture and row-fixed moving radical paid in \(m+q\). Identities \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:A.12}{(8)}--\protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:A.19}{(15)}, \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:C.3}{(47)} preserve collision masks; \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:2.8}{(105)} retains its quotient and all valuation factors.

Selected-prime, merged inverse--plain, relative-character and incumbent-dependent coefficients require their own contracts. In particular, \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:4.6}{(40)} is only the stated-scale theta energy, not an inverse-times-prime or plain-fourth-times-prime moment.

\hypertarget{mf:chapter}{}

\label{mf:chapter}

\hypertarget{marked-completed-energy-and-the-incumbent-moments}{%
\subsubsection{7 Marked completed energy and the incumbent moments}\label{marked-completed-energy-and-the-incumbent-moments}}

Using §\protect\hyperlink{cf:reflection}{3.1}'s reflection and the finite identities \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:A.12}{(8)}--\protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:A.19}{(15)}, \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:C.3}{(47)}, we prove multi-slot completed energy, inverse-times-prime and natural marked plain moments. Original MF equation labels are retained; identical foundational formulas are aliases. Here \(\beta\) is the actual Hecke zero supremum, and every prime estimate assumes \(2\beta-1\le\kappa\).

The original and refined bootstrap ranges remain distinct. Every \(Z^\epsilon\) means a loss chosen before the scale. Rows are Eisenstein elements with all six units and arbitrary valuations.

\hypertarget{the-completed-reflection-with-product-marks}{}

\label{the-completed-reflection-with-product-marks}

\hypertarget{the-completed-reflection-with-product-marks-heading-2}{%
\paragraph{7.1 The completed reflection with product marks}}

Whole-index marks change conductors at vanishing local frequency. Retain compatible branches until row and mark phases separate, then apply the quadratic--cubic sieve.

\hypertarget{the-completed-family-and-fixed-conventions}{}

\label{the-completed-family-and-fixed-conventions}

\hypertarget{the-completed-family-and-fixed-conventions-heading-2}{%
\subparagraph{7.1.1 The completed family and fixed conventions}}

Use §\protect\hyperlink{cf:sec:1.1}{1.2}'s arithmetic conventions, \(q_a=|a|^2\), \(Z\ge2\), and fixed \(S\) containing primes above \(6\). Primary generators represent ideals prime to \(\lambda\). In particular,

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.1}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.1}

\[
\chi_p(A)^0=1_{p\nmid A}.
\qquad\text{(MF2.1)}
\]

Exponent reduction never deletes this mask.

Fix finitely many finite-ray \(\nu\), zero outside primary \(S\)-units. A squarefree good label \(f\) and squarefree puncture \(r_\rho\) may vary between invocations but are fixed within a row norm:

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.2}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.2}

\[
\rho(A)=1_{(A,r_\rho)=1},\qquad
\Psi_k(A)=\nu(A)\rho(A)\chi_A(k)\chi_A(f)^4.
\qquad\text{(MF2.2)}
\]

The puncture may depend on frozen outer labels, but neither the current row nor a subsequently averaged \(f\).

For a fixed number of pairwise disjoint prime lists \(\mathcal P_i\), with \(q_p\asymp Z^{z_i}\) on the \(i\)-th list and \(|a_i(p)|\le1\), put

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.3}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.3}

\[
d(A)=\sum_{(p_i)\in\prod_i\mathcal P_i}
       \prod_i a_i(p_i)\,1_{\prod_i p_i\mid A},
\qquad z=\sum_i z_i.
\qquad\text{(MF2.3)}
\]

The completed sum at length \(X\) is

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.4}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.4}

\[
\mathcal T_d(X;k;V)=
\sum_{\substack{n\ {\rm sf},\,b\\ (nb,S)=1}}
\frac{\overline{\alpha(n)}\gamma_2(n)\Psi_k(n)\,
      \overline{\alpha(b)}^{\,3}\Psi_k(b)^3}
     {\sqrt{q_n}\,q_b}\,
d(nb^3)V(q_nq_b^3/X).
\qquad\text{(MF2.4)}
\]

Primal \(n,b\) may share primes. Tests are annular smooth or rapidly decreasing with all Euler derivatives at both endpoints; include the central conversion factor \(y^{1/2}\) in \(V\).

A common smooth profile in completed norm and individual prime norms, on fixed logarithmic prime boxes, is allowed inside the tuple sum. Logarithmic Fourier inversion reduces it to an integrable common family of \(V\); bounds use finitely many weighted Euler seminorms, uniformly in lists, bounded coefficients, \(k,f,r_\rho\) and bounded logarithmic lengths. Heights cost fixed polynomials. Product coefficients are required during assignment to extracted factors; afterwards surviving tuple coefficients may be arbitrary bounded functions independent of row and dual indices.

Classical theta and sieve inputs are exactly §\protect\hyperlink{cf:classical-inputs}{1.1}'s unconditional results; moving-conductor and marked reductions follow below.

\hypertarget{exact-reflection-with-the-local-zeros-retained}{}

\label{exact-reflection-with-the-local-zeros-retained}

\hypertarget{exact-reflection-with-the-local-zeros-retained-heading-2}{%
\subparagraph{7.1.2 Exact reflection, with the local zeros retained}}

\hypertarget{the-finite-theta-source}{}

\label{the-finite-theta-source}

The finite theta source

With \(\check e(z)=\exp(2\pi i(z+\bar z))\) and \(e(z)=\check e(z/\lambda)\), the theta input {[}DR, (5.4), (5.7), (5.9), (5.12)--(5.17), Appendix A{]} gives

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.5}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.5}

\[
\Gamma_2=\langle {\rm SL}_2(\mathbb Z),\Gamma(3)\rangle,\qquad
\theta(gw)=\kappa(g)\theta(w),\qquad
\kappa\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\c&d\end{pmatrix}
=\left(\frac ca\right)_3\quad(g\in\Gamma(3),\ c\ne0).
\qquad\text{(MF2.5)}
\]

The multiplier is one on \({\rm SL}_2(\mathbb Z)\) and on the \(c=0\) elements of \(\Gamma(3)\). Let

\[
L(t)=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\t&1\end{pmatrix},\qquad
\Theta_j(w)=\theta(L(t_j)w),\qquad
(t_0,t_1,t_2)=(0,\omega^2,\omega).
\]

Translations by \(\mathbb Z+3\mathcal O\) have multiplier one, so \(L(\pm\lambda)\) give \(\Theta_1,\Theta_2\). For \(u_0=s+t\omega\), \(0\le s,t<3\), \(\theta(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}u_0&-1\\1&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)w)=\Theta_t(w)\).

For each of these three sources define \(d_H\) by

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.6}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.6}

\[
\overline{\theta(H(z,v))}
=C_H(v)+\sum_{\mu\ne0}d_H(\mu)\,vK_{1/3}(4\pi|\mu|v)\check e(\mu z).
\qquad\text{(MF2.6)}
\]

Their support and coefficient bound are

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.7}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.7}

\textbf{(MF2.7)} is the cusp support and bound \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:B.5}{(21)}, with \(m=h\), \(d=d_H\), and \(\sqrt{q_b}=|b|\).

The dual unit \(u\) and primary ideals \(n,b\) retain the full cusp support: \(n,b\) may share primes and meet \(S\setminus\{\lambda\}\), without primal \(S\)-exclusion.

For the precise source check, the ordinary cubic sums

\[
\widetilde g_3(n)=q_n^{-1/2}\sum_{v\bmod n}\left(\frac vn\right)_3\check e(v/n)
\]

have modulus one for primary squarefree \(n\) by finite Gauss and CRT, including the norm-four prime \(-2\) using only its cubic symbol. The three source magnitudes are \(3^{h/6+8/3}|b|\), \(3^{h/6+3}|b|\), and \(9|b|\) at \(h=-4\), each within \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.7}{(MF2.7)}. Valuations uniquely determine \(n,b\). At infinity, \(\lambda^3\mu\equiv1\pmod3\) selects

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.8}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.8}

\[
d_I(\lambda^{-3}nb^3)=3^{5/2}|b|\widetilde g_3(n),\qquad
\widetilde g_3(n)=\chi_n(\lambda)^{-2}\gamma_2(n)\quad((n,S)=1).
\qquad\text{(MF2.8)}
\]

Shared \(n,b\) primes remain allowed.

\hypertarget{all-nonzero-element-rows-use-one-fixed-modulus}{}

\label{all-nonzero-element-rows-use-one-fixed-modulus}

All nonzero element rows use one fixed modulus

Write uniquely \(k=uk_Sk_{\rm good}\) with fixed generators at \(S\) and primary generators elsewhere. On primary \(S\)-units, reciprocity gives

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.9}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.9}

\[
\chi_A(k)=\chi_A(uk_S)R(A,k_{\rm good})
          \prod_{p\mid k_{\rm good}}\chi_p(A)^{v_p(k)}.
\qquad\text{(MF2.9)}
\]

At collisions both sides vanish, including \(6\mid v_p(k)\). Fixing \(u\), \(S\)-valuations modulo six and the reciprocity ray class of \(k_{\rm good}\) puts the first two factors times \(\nu\) in a fixed finite \(S\)-supported family \(\Psi_0\). Supplementary characters have only these finitely many exponents. The fourth-power label adds no sign because \(R(A,f)^4=1\).

Fix \(E\) with support exactly \(S\), divisible by these conductors. Choose \(L\) on the same support, \(18\mid L\), \(E\mid L\), so

\[
\varphi(x)=
\begin{cases}
\chi_x(\lambda)^2\Psi_0(x),&x\equiv1\pmod3,\ (x,E)=1,\\
0,&\text{otherwise}
\end{cases}
\]

is periodic: \((\lambda/x)_3\) has modulus \(9\) by the supplementary law; all other characters and zeros have fixed moduli. Evaluate the zero branch first. Set

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.10}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.10}

\[
\widehat\varphi(h_0)=q_L^{-1}\sum_{x\bmod L}\varphi(x)e(-h_0x/L),
\qquad \mathfrak M=\lambda^{12}L^4.
\qquad\text{(MF2.10)}
\]

Keep every good prime of \(k,f,r_\rho\) in a moving local set \(\mathcal P\), combining exponents modulo six; none enters \(L\). Reflect \(\Psi(A)=\Psi_0(A)\prod_{p\in\mathcal P}\chi_p(A)^{j_p}\), \(0\le j_p<6\).

\hypertarget{the-reflection-identity}{}

\label{the-reflection-identity}

The reflection identity

For \(m\not\equiv0\pmod6\), let

\[
\tau_{p,m}^{\pm}=q_p^{-1/2}\sum_{y\bmod p}\chi_p(y)^m e(\pm y/p),
\qquad |\tau_{p,m}^{\pm}|=1.
\]

A branch chooses \(h_0\bmod L\) and an active subset containing all \(j_p\ne0\). Let \(r\) be its product. Reduce \(\lambda^2h_0/L=a_F/c_F\), making the numerator primary if \(\lambda\mid c_F\), otherwise the denominator. Put \(c=c_Fr\) and, modulo active \(p\), define

\[
\sigma_p=\lambda^2c/p,\qquad
\epsilon_p=-\bigl((\lambda^3c/p)\sigma_p\bigr)^{-1}
          =-\lambda^{-5}(c/p)^{-2},
\]

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.11}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.11}

\[
B_{p,j}(x)=
\begin{cases}
\chi_p(x)^{-j-2},&j\ne0,4,\\
q_p^{-1/2}(-1+q_p1_{p\mid x}),&j=4,\\
q_p^{-1/2}\chi_p(x)^{-2},&j=0,
\end{cases}
\quad
\omega_{p,j}=
\begin{cases}
\tau_{p,j}^-\tau_{p,j+2}^+\chi_p(\epsilon_p)^{-j-2},&j\ne0,4,\\
\tau_{p,4}^-,&j=4,\\
-\tau_{p,2}^+\chi_p(\epsilon_p)^{-2},&j=0.
\end{cases}
\qquad\text{(MF2.11)}
\]

Indices on \(\tau\) are reduced modulo six.

Define

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.12}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.12}

\par\noindent\resizebox{\linewidth}{!}{\begin{minipage}{\dimexpr\linewidth+33.76625pt\relax}\[
K_*=\frac{(2\pi)^4}{27},\qquad
\mathcal R(t)=\prod_\pm\frac{\Gamma(1+t\pm1/6)}{\Gamma(1-t\pm1/6)},\qquad
V^\sharp(x)=\frac1{2\pi i}\int_{(\sigma)}
\widehat V(-t)\mathcal R(t)(K_*x)^{-t}\,dt,\quad \sigma\ge0.
\qquad\text{(MF2.12)}
\]\end{minipage}}\par

The integral is independent of \(\sigma\ge0\). Let \(\mathcal T_1(X;\Psi;V)\) be \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.4}{(MF2.4)} with no mark and character \(\Psi\). Each branch's source \(d\), additive character \(\vartheta\) and unit \(\kappa_F\) depend only on \(h_0,r\bmod\mathfrak M^2\).

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.13}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.13}

\textbf{(MF2.13)} is \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:B.2}{(18)} with scalar \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:B.4}{(20)}, under \(\mathcal T_1=\mathcal C_V(X;\Psi)\), \(\widehat\phi=\widehat\varphi\), \(c=c_Fr\), and the local factors \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.11}{(MF2.11)}. The scalars agree since \(\tau_{p,j}^-=\chi_p(-1)^j\gamma_j(p)\) and \(\tau_{p,j}^+=\gamma_j(p)\).

Dual sums converge absolutely, \(|\zeta|\le1/81\), with \(O_L(2^{|\mathcal P|})\) branches, or \(O_L(4^{|\mathcal P|})\) after splitting \(j=4\). Choices are simultaneous: adding an inactive \(j=0\) prime multiplies by \(1-q_p^{-1}\) while preserving conductor, source, additive character, other phases and kernel.

\textbf{Conductor and phase proof.} The fixed-modulus construction of §\protect\hyperlink{cf:reflection}{3.1} applies; we retain its extra explicit sector checks here. Its local Fourier identity is

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.14}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.14}

\textbf{(MF2.14)} is \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:B.9}{(25)}, with \(q=q_p\), \(j=j_p\) and \(\tau^-_{\mathfrak p,j}=\tau^-_{p,j}\).

Hence the masked theta function is

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.15}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.15}

\[
F(z,v)=\sum_{h_0,(h_p)}
\widehat\varphi(h_0)\prod_pC_{p,j_p}(h_p)\,
\overline{\theta(z+z_h,v)},\qquad
z_h=\lambda^2\left(h_0/L+\sum_ph_p/p\right).
\qquad\text{(MF2.15)}
\]

At infinity \(\lambda^3\mu=nb^3\), the mask first deletes \((nb,E)>1\), leaving multiplier \(\chi_n(\lambda)^2\Psi(n)\Psi(b)^3\), also at shared moving primes. Thus \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.8}{(MF2.8)} identifies the completed direct series.

Fix \(h_0\) and the active frequencies. Then

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.16}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.16}

\[
z_h=a/c,\qquad c=c_Fr,\qquad
a=a_Fr+\lambda^2c_F\sum_{p\mid r}(r/p)h_p.
\qquad\text{(MF2.16)}
\]

The numerator is a unit at \(c_F\) and at \(p\mid r\) (there \(a\equiv\sigma_ph_p\)), so the fraction is reduced even for nonunit \(h_0\). Lift \(h_p\equiv0\pmod{\mathfrak M^2}\) while retaining its \(p\)-class and fix \(r\bmod\mathfrak M^2\); then \(a\bmod\mathfrak Mc_F\) is fixed. Choose by CRT

\[
\begin{aligned}
\delta'&\equiv a^{-1}\pmod{\mathfrak Mc_F}\text{ at primes of }c_F,\\
\delta'&\equiv0\pmod{\mathfrak M}\text{ at other primes of }\mathfrak M,\\
\delta'&\equiv a^{-1}\pmod p\ (p\mid r).
\end{aligned}
\]

Put \(b=(a\delta'-1)/c\), \(g=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\c&\delta'\end{smallmatrix}\right)\). At \(c_F\), \(b\equiv0\pmod{\mathfrak M}\); elsewhere in \(\mathfrak M\), \(b\equiv-c^{-1}\). Thus fixed-prime matrix residues are frequency-independent. Periodic lifts and CRT-modulus shifts of \(\delta'\) avoid zero entries if needed for symbols.

If \(v_\lambda(c)\ge2\), take \(H=I\): \(g\in\Gamma(3)\) and \(\kappa(g)=(c_F/a)_3(a/r)_3\). If \(v_\lambda(c)=1\), take \(H=L(u_0)\), \(u_0\in\{\pm\lambda\}\), \(u_0\equiv c\pmod3\), and \(A=a-u_0b\). Then \(G=gH^{-1}\in\Gamma(3)\), \(a,A\) are coprime primary, and

\[
a(c-u_0\delta')=cA-u_0,\qquad bc\equiv-1\pmod a.
\]

Cubic reciprocity gives

\[
\kappa(G)=
\left(\frac{-u_0}{A}\right)_3\left(\frac aA\right)_3^{-1}
=\left(\frac{-u_0}{A}\right)_3
 \left(\frac{c_F/u_0}{a}\right)_3\left(\frac ar\right)_3.
\]

If \(\lambda\nmid c\), choose \(u_0=s+t\omega\equiv a\pmod3\), \(0\le s,t<3\), and \(H=\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}u_0&-1\\1&0\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)\). Then

\[
G=gH^{-1}=
\begin{pmatrix}-b&a+bu_0\\-\delta'&c+\delta'u_0\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma(3).
\]

Both \(-b,c\) are primary and \(-bc=1-a\delta'\equiv1\pmod9\). For \(\delta'=u\lambda^h\delta_0\), supplementary laws handle unit/\(\lambda\) factors modulo \(9\); reciprocity gives numerator \(1\bmod\delta_0\), so \((\delta'/(-bc))_3=1\). Hence \(\kappa(G)=(a/c_F)_3(a/r)_3\). In all cases,

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.17}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.17}

\[
\kappa(G)=\kappa_F\prod_{p\mid r}\chi_p(a)^2,
\qquad\text{(MF2.17)}
\]

The sector fixes \(\kappa_F,d_H\), without requiring unit-frequency support for \(\widehat\varphi\).

For \(x=\lambda^4\mu\) and \(D_F=\lambda^3c_F\), additive CRT gives

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.18}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.18}

\[
\check e(-\delta'\mu/c)
=\vartheta(x)\prod_{p\mid r}e(\epsilon_ph_p^{-1}x/p),\qquad
\vartheta(x)=e(-\delta_F'r^{-1}x/D_F).
\qquad\text{(MF2.18)}
\]

Fixed congruences determine \(\delta_F'\bmod D_F\), and the sector fixes \(r^{-1}\). Conjugating \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.17}{(MF2.17)} contributes \(\overline{\kappa_F}\prod_p\chi_p(\sigma_p)^{-2}\chi_p(h_p)^{-2}\). Multiply \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.14}{(MF2.14)} and set \(y=h_p^{-1}\): Gauss exponent \(j+2\), or \(-1+q_p1_{p\mid x}\) at \(j=4\), gives exactly \(\omega_{p,j}B_{p,j}(x)\) with zeros. Inactive and absent zero-exponent primes have identical active radical, proving simultaneous compatibility.

\textbf{Proof of the Mellin normalization and continuation.} Put

\[
D(s,\Psi)=\sum_{n\ {\rm sf}}\overline{\alpha(n)}\gamma_2(n)\Psi(n)q_n^{-s},
\quad L_0(s,\Psi)=\sum_b\overline{\alpha(b)}^{\,3}\Psi(b)^3q_b^{-3s+1/2},
\quad T=L_0D.
\]

These primary \(S\)-unit sums converge for \(\Re s>1\). Put \(J(s)=\int_0^\infty\partial_{\bar z}F(0,v)v^{2s-1}\,dv\); the Bessel integral is

\[
\int_0^\infty K_{1/3}(y)y^{w-1}\,dy
=2^{w-2}\Gamma((w-1/3)/2)\Gamma((w+1/3)/2).
\]

Using \(\partial_{\bar z}\check e(\mu z)=2\pi i\bar\mu\check e(\mu z)\) and \(\overline{\alpha(\lambda^{-3})}=-i\),

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.19}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.19}

\textbf{(MF2.19)} is the \protect\hyperlink{cf:direct-mellin-identity}{direct Mellin identity}, with \(\Theta_\Psi=F\), \(\mathcal D_\Psi(s)=T(s,\Psi)\) and left side \(J(s)\).

The inverse cusp action is \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:B.10}{(26)}, with \(\delta=\delta'\).

At \(z=0\) the chain rule gives \(-\overline{\kappa(G)}(cv)^{-2}\partial_z\) at \((-\delta'/c,1/(q_cv))\), deleting every cusp constant. Substitute \(v\mapsto1/(q_cv)\) and the Bessel integral: for \(\Re s<0\), \(J(s)\) equals

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.20}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.20}

\[
-\frac i4(2\pi)^{2s-2}\overline{\alpha(c)}^{\,2}q_c^{1-2s}
\overline{\kappa(G)}
\prod_\pm\Gamma(3/2-s\pm1/6)
\sum_{\mu\ne0}d_H(\mu)\alpha(\mu)\check e(-\delta'\mu/c)q_\mu^{-(1-s)}.
\qquad\text{(MF2.20)}
\]

By \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.7}{(MF2.7)}, this converges absolutely. Direct and inverse-cusp expansions give exponential endpoint decay; log-height integration by parts makes \(J\) entire and rapidly decreasing on fixed strips. Equation \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.19}{(MF2.19)} makes \(T\) entire, bounded right of \(1\) and polynomial left of \(0\) via \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.20}{(MF2.20)}. Its preliminary intervening bound is \((1+|s|)^C e^{\pi|\Im s|}\). Apply the maximum principle to \(T(s)(B+s)^{-C'}e^{\epsilon s^2}\), \(-B\) outside the strip, then let \(\epsilon\downarrow0\), obtaining polynomial strip growth.

Mellin inversion gives \((2\pi i)^{-1}\int_{(a)}\widehat V(s-1/2)X^{s-1/2}T(s,\Psi)\,ds\), \(a>1\). Shift left of zero, use \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.20}{(MF2.20)}/\protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.19}{(MF2.19)} and set \(t=1/2-s\). The gamma quotient is \(\mathcal R(t)\); the remaining factor is

\[
-\frac i{81}\overline{\alpha(c)}^{\,2}q_\mu^{-1/2}
(K_*q_\mu X/q_c^2)^{-t}.
\]

Absolute convergence and Mellin decay justify interchanges and shifts. The finite factors give \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.13}{(MF2.13)}, also for the rapidly decreasing tests admitted here.

\hypertarget{common-profiles-and-whole-series-tails}{}

\label{common-profiles-and-whole-series-tails}

\hypertarget{common-profiles-and-whole-series-tails-heading-2}{%
\subparagraph{7.1.3 Common profiles and whole-series tails}}

For \(A,B\ge0\), with \(B\) an integer, set

\[
\mathcal M_{A,B}(V)=
\sup_{-A\le\eta\le1/4}\int_{\mathbb R}
(1+|t|)^B|\widehat V(\eta+it)|\,dt.
\]

The first negative numerator pole is \(-5/6\) and \(|\mathcal R(\sigma+it)|\ll_A(1+|t|)^{4\sigma}\) for \(-1/4\le\sigma\le A\). Shifts to \(-1/4,0,A\) give

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.21}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.21}

\[
|(x\partial_x)^jV^\sharp(x)|
\ll_{A,j}\min\{x^{1/4},(1+x)^{-A}\}
\mathcal M_{A,\lceil4A\rceil+j+2}(V).
\qquad\text{(MF2.21)}
\]

These are finite seminorms: \(B+2\) integrations by parts bound \(\mathcal M_{A,B}\) by

\[
\ll_{A,B}\int_0^\infty
(1+x^{-A}+x^{1/4})\bigl(|V(x)|+|(x\partial_x)^{B+2}V(x)|\bigr)\frac{dx}{x}.
\]

Replacing \(V\) by \(x^{it_0}V\) costs at most \((1+|t_0|)^B\).

On a fixed compact log box let \(F_Y(\mathbf y)=W(\mathbf y)V^\sharp(Y\prod_i y_i^{a_i})\), \(Y\) fixed per block. Equation \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.21}{(MF2.21)} bounds each fixed derivative by

\[
m_A(Y)=\min\{Y^{1/4},(1+Y)^{-A}\}
\]

times fixed seminorms. Logarithmic Fourier integration by parts gives the same weighted \(L^1\) scalar. For a linear row vector, set \(F_Y=m_A(Y)\widetilde F_Y\) and apply one common Fourier inversion and Minkowski:

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.22}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.22}

\[
\|U(F_Y)\|_{\ell^2(I)}
\le m_A(Y)\int |\widehat{\widetilde F_Y}(\mathbf t)|
\|B(\mathbf t)\|_{\ell^2(I)}\,\frac{d\mathbf t}{(2\pi)^d}.
\qquad\text{(MF2.22)}
\]

Then enlarge the positive row set using the separated definition of \(B\) on added rows, without asserting kernel bounds there. Squaring gives \(m_A(Y)^2\), in particular small-argument energy gain \(Y^{1/2}\). Row-dependent Fourier measures would invalidate this step.

For \(\Lambda=\mathcal O\) or \(\lambda^{-4}\mathcal O\), \(|K(x)|\le C_Ax^{-A}\) (\(x\ge1\), \(A>1\)) gives

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.23}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.23}

\textbf{(MF2.23)} is the shell bound \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:B.25}{(44)}, valid for both lattices with \(a>0\), \(U\ge1\).

Its \(O(1+2^jU/a)\) shell count controls the whole discarded series independently of retained-index bounds.

\hypertarget{the-quadraticcubic-norm-including-both-collision-masks}{}

\label{the-quadraticcubic-norm-including-both-collision-masks}

\hypertarget{the-quadraticcubic-norm-including-both-collision-masks-heading-2}{%
\subparagraph{7.1.4 The quadratic--cubic norm, including both collision masks}}

\hypertarget{imported-sieves-and-their-domains}{}

\label{imported-sieves-and-their-domains}

Imported sieves and their domains

For squarefree primary good ideals, use the quadratic sieve

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.24}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.24}

\textbf{(MF2.24)} is \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:B.11}{(27)} under \((R,n,K,N)=(a,b,U,V)\), with the same row-independent coefficients.

For clarity, the primitive ideal character in §\protect\hyperlink{cf:sec:B.2}{3.2}'s family check is

\[
\vartheta_a((x))=\epsilon_\lambda(x)^{e(a)}(x/a)_2,\qquad
e(a)=(q_a-1)/2\bmod2,
\]

Here \(\epsilon_\lambda\) is the nontrivial character modulo \(\lambda\). Primary adjustment uses square powers of \(\omega\); its sign contributes \(\epsilon_\lambda^{e(a)}\). CRT gives conductor \(a\lambda^{e(a)}\), trivial infinite type, omitting \(\lambda\) when \(e(a)=0\). Fixed primary square classes modulo four make \(e(a)\) equal; coprime product conductors are exactly products, and reciprocity is a fixed-ray bicharacter. These are {[}GL, Definition 1{]}'s three conditions; apply its theorem and transpose duality.

Heath-Brown's cubic sieve gives

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.25}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.25}

\textbf{(MF2.25)} is \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:B.19}{(36)}, since \((b/a)_3=\chi_a(b)^2\), with squarefree primary ideals, lengths \(U,V\) and row-independent \(c_b\).

Neither sieve requires squarefree rational norms or exclusion of rational prime factors. Conjugation/reciprocity changes cubic orientation. Fixed row restrictions decrease the positive norm; fixed column restrictions are zero coefficients. Resolve mixed collision masks first.

\hypertarget{quadratic-reduction-for-a-squarefree-index-times-a-cube}{}

\label{quadratic-reduction-for-a-squarefree-index-times-a-cube}

Quadratic reduction for a squarefree index times a cube

Use §\protect\hyperlink{cf:sec:B.3}{3.3}'s variables: squarefree good \(q_k\ll K\), squarefree primary \(q_n\asymp N\), primary \(q_b\asymp B\), allowing dual \(S\setminus\{\lambda\}\) primes and arbitrary \(k\)-independent \(\beta(n,b)\) with fixed supports.

Write uniquely

\[
b=gt^2,\qquad c=(n,g),\qquad n=cm,\qquad g=ch,
\]

Here \(g\) is squarefree; \(c,m,h\) are pairwise coprime squarefree, allowing \((g,t)>1\). On \(q_c\asymp C,q_g\asymp G,q_t\asymp T\), \(B\asymp GT^2\), and

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.26}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.26}

\[
\chi_k(nb)^3=\chi_k(mh)^3\,1_{(k,ct)=1}.
\qquad\text{(MF2.26)}
\]

Apply the quadratic reduction proved in §\protect\hyperlink{cf:sec:B.3}{3.3}: Cauchy in \(t\), retain \((k,t)=1\), expand \(1_{(k,c)=1}\) completely, and set \(k=rk'\), \(c=rc'\). Keep \((k',r)=(c',r)=1\); \((r,t)>1\) vanishes. No \((k',c')\)-mask is restored termwise.

The same fixed-\(S\) split, squarefree product grouping and Cauchy over \(O(C/q_r)\) choices of \(c'\) give, with \(\mathcal D_r(t)\) retaining the original support,

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.27}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.27}

\textbf{(MF2.27)} is the positive quadratic bound \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:B.17}{(34)}, with \(Q_{C,G,T}=\mathcal Q_{C,G,T}\) and final sum restricted to \(\mathcal D_r(t)\). All its row/column independence and common-prime conventions are unchanged.

This positive form preserves tuple independence. For \(|\beta|\le1\), the triple count is \(O(NG/(q_rC))\); on \(q_r\asymp r_0\), \(O(r_0)\) choices of \(r\) and \(O(T)\) choices of \(t\) give

\[
\ll (KNB)^\epsilon\frac{NB}{r_0}
\left(\frac K{r_0}+\frac{NG}{C^2}\right)
\ll(KNB)^\epsilon NB(K+NB).
\]

Triangle over \(C,G,T\) dyads yields

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.28}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.28}

\textbf{(MF2.28)} is \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:B.18}{(35)}, with the same \(K,N,B\), supports and \(|\beta|\le1\).

Subunit quotients are empty; bounded ranges, including the unit, cost constants.

\hypertarget{hybrid-norm}{}

\label{hybrid-norm}

Hybrid norm

Let \(P\) be squarefree good with \(q_P\asymp L\). Suppose \(|a(P)|,|\beta(n,b)|\le1\), with \(a\) independent of \(k,n,b\) and \(\beta\) independent of \(k,P\). Then

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.29}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.29}

\[
\begin{aligned}
\mathcal H&=
\sum_k\left|
\sum_P\frac{a(P)}{\sqrt{q_P}}1_{(P,k)=1}
\sum_{n,b}\beta(n,b)\chi_k(nb)^3\chi_P(n)^{-2}1_{(P,b)=1}
\right|^2\\
&\ll_\epsilon(KNBL)^\epsilon
(K+NB)B\{N+L+(NL)^{2/3}\}.
\end{aligned}
\qquad\text{(MF2.29)}
\]

Fixed supports and ray sectors may be imposed while preserving both independence conditions.

Expand \(1_{(P,k)=1}\) completely over \(D\mid(P,k)\) and apply divisor Cauchy. Put \(P=DP'\), \(k=Dk'\) and

\[
a_D(P')=a(DP')1_{(D,P')=1},\qquad
\beta_D(n,b)=\beta(n,b)\chi_D(nb)^3\chi_D(n)^{-2}1_{(D,b)=1}.
\]

Energy gains \(q_D^{-1}\), with lengths \(K/q_D,L/q_D\). Do not restore \((P',k')=1\). Apply \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.27}{(MF2.27)} to \(k'\) with column coefficient

\[
\beta_D(n,b)\sum_{P'}a_D(P')q_{P'}^{-1/2}
\chi_{P'}(n)^{-2}1_{(P',b)=1}
\]

as its row-independent coefficient. In its notation the positive output for one \(C,G,T\)-block is bounded by

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.30}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.30}

\[
\begin{aligned}
(KNBL)^\epsilon
\sum_D\frac1{q_D}T\sum_{q_t\asymp T}\sum_r
\left(\frac K{q_Dq_r}+\frac{NG}{C^2}\right)\frac C{q_r}
\sum_{c',m,h}|\beta_D(rc'm,rc'ht^2)|^2\\
\hspace{15mm}\cdot
\left|\sum_{P'}\frac{a_{D,r}(P')}{\sqrt{q_{P'}}}
\chi_{P'}(c'm)^{-2}1_{(P',ht)=1}\right|^2,
\qquad a_{D,r}(P')=a_D(P')\chi_{P'}(r)^{-2}.
\end{aligned}
\qquad\text{(MF2.30)}
\]

The identity used to obtain it, with zeros included, is

\[
\chi_{P'}(rc'm)^{-2}1_{(P',rc'ht^2)=1}
=\chi_{P'}(r)^{-2}\chi_{P'}(c'm)^{-2}1_{(P',ht)=1}.
\]

The character supplies the zeros at \(r,c',m\).

Only in this positive sum discard \(|\beta_D|^2\le1\). For fixed \(h,t,D,r\), coefficients are independent of squarefree primary \(j=c'm=n/r\), with mass \(\sum_{P'}q_{P'}^{-1}|a_{D,r}(P')|^2 1_{(P',ht)=1}\ll(KNBL)^\epsilon\). Group by \(j\), retaining its \(S\)-parts and norm \(O(N/q_r)\); \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.25}{(MF2.25)} and \(O(G/C)\) values of \(h\) give on \(q_D\asymp D_0,q_r\asymp r_0\)

\[
\ll(KNBL)^\epsilon\frac GC
\left\{\frac N{r_0}+\frac L{D_0}+
\left(\frac{NL}{r_0D_0}\right)^{2/3}\right\}.
\]

The \(t\)-sum and \(C/q_r\) give \(T^2(C/r_0)(G/C)\asymp B/r_0\); \(O(r_0)\) values of \(r\) cancel its denominator and \(\sum_{q_D\asymp D_0}q_D^{-1}\ll1\). Replace \(D_0,r_0\) by one in increasing sieve factors, use \(NG/C^2\ll NB\) and sum dyads. This proves \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.29}{(MF2.29)} with all original masks.

\hypertarget{whole-index-marks-and-separation-of-their-phases}{}

\label{whole-index-marks-and-separation-of-their-phases}

\hypertarget{whole-index-marks-and-separation-of-their-phases-heading-2}{%
\subparagraph{7.1.5 Whole-index marks and separation of their phases}}

For marks \(\mathcal Q\) outside the base local set, expand \(\prod_q1_{q\mid A}=\prod_q(1-\chi_q(A)^0)\) and apply compatible \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.13}{(MF2.13)}. Each inactive contribution is

\[
1-(1-q_q^{-1})=q_q^{-1}.
\]

The subtracted active branch contributes \(q_q^{-1/2}\chi_q(\lambda^4\mu)^{-2}\), scalar \(-\omega_{q,0}\). Thus

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.31}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.31}

\par\noindent\resizebox{\linewidth}{!}{\begin{minipage}{\dimexpr\linewidth+39.11569pt\relax}\[
\begin{aligned}
\mathcal T_{\mathcal Q}(X;\Psi;V)
&=\sum_{\mathcal B}\zeta_{\mathcal Q}
\sum_{\mu\ne0}\frac{d(\mu)\alpha(\mu)\vartheta(\lambda^4\mu)}{\sqrt{q_\mu}}
\prod_{p\in\mathcal A_0}B_{p,j_p}(\lambda^4\mu)
\prod_{q\in A_{\mathcal Q}}q_q^{-1/2}\chi_q(\lambda^4\mu)^{-2}
V^\sharp(q_\mu X/q_c^2),\\
\zeta_{\mathcal Q}
&=-\frac i{81}\overline{\alpha(c)}^{\,2}
\widehat\varphi(h_0)\overline{\kappa_F}
\prod_{p\in\mathcal I_0}(1-q_p^{-1})\prod_{q\in I_{\mathcal Q}}q_q^{-1}
\prod_{p\in\mathcal A_0}\chi_p(\sigma_p)^{-2}\omega_{p,j_p}
\prod_{q\in A_{\mathcal Q}}\chi_q(\sigma_q)^{-2}(-\omega_{q,0}).
\end{aligned}
\qquad\text{(MF2.31)}
\]\end{minipage}}\par

Here base sets are \(\mathcal A_0,\mathcal I_0\), marks split as \(\mathcal Q=A_{\mathcal Q}\sqcup I_{\mathcal Q}\), and \(c=c_F\prod_{p\in\mathcal A_0\cup A_{\mathcal Q}}p\). Inactive marks are absent from conductor and kernel. A mark already in the base set conflicts with its primal zero, so delete that term first.

The phase at an active prime has the exact form

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.32}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.32}

\[
\chi_p(\sigma_p)^{-2}\omega_{p,j}
=\xi_{p,j}\chi_p(c/p)^{2j+2},
\qquad |\xi_{p,j}|=1,
\qquad\text{(MF2.32)}
\]

Here \(\xi_{p,j}\) depends only on \(p,j\). Substitute \(\epsilon_p=-\lambda^{-5}(c/p)^{-2}\) in \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.11}{(MF2.11)}, using \(2j+2\equiv-2\pmod6\) at \(j=4\). Distinct active primes therefore give

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.33}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.33}

\[
\chi_p(q)^{2j_p+2}\chi_q(p)^{2j_q+2}
=\left(\frac qp\right)_3^{j_p+j_q+2}.
\qquad\text{(MF2.33)}
\]

Residual row exponent one and mark exponent zero give phase one. Exponent four would retain a cubic row interaction, so freeze every nonresidual base prime first.

Let \(R\) be residual row, \(P\) active mark product, \(F_{\rm act}\) frozen active product. Separately fix \(R,P\bmod\mathfrak M^2\), determining \(d,\vartheta,\kappa_F\) without pair restrictions. Equation \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.33}{(MF2.33)} assigns row--row/frozen-row phases to a row scalar and mark--mark/frozen-mark phases to a tuple scalar; \(\overline{\alpha(c)}^2\) separates. Primal collisions retain \(1_{(P,R)=1}\).

After fixing the dual unit, ramified power, and extracted frozen factors, the remaining dual part is \(nb^3\). Its moving columns are exactly

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.34}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.34}

\[
\chi_R(nb^3)^3=\chi_R(nb)^3,\qquad
\chi_P(nb^3)^{-2}=\chi_P(n)^{-2}1_{(P,b)=1}.
\qquad\text{(MF2.34)}
\]

Source, additive character and frozen columns depend only on the full extracted dual index. Frozen collisions are fixed row, tuple or dual restrictions. Common separation therefore gives tuple coefficients independent of \(R,n,b\), dual coefficients independent of \(R,P\), and exactly \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.29}{(MF2.29)}'s mixed masks.

\hypertarget{reflected-energy-and-its-two-consequences}{}

\label{reflected-energy-and-its-two-consequences}

\hypertarget{reflected-energy-and-its-two-consequences-heading-2}{%
\subparagraph{7.1.6 Reflected energy and its two consequences}}

\hypertarget{block-parameters-and-estimate}{}

\label{block-parameters-and-estimate}

Block parameters and estimate

Fix \(q_f\asymp Z^V\), \(Q=\log_Zq_{r_\rho}\), \(X=Z^{N_*}\), and rows \(0<q_k\ll Z^M\). Split the ideal of \(k\) as

\[
(k)=k_{\rm pow}k_{\rm sup}R.
\]

The maximal powerful part contains valuations \(\ge2\); \(k_{\rm sup}\) contains valuation-one primes in \(S\cup\operatorname{supp}(fr_\rho)\); residual \(R\) is squarefree, with disjoint supports. Freeze both actual parts, unit and sectors. On dyads \(q_{k_{\rm pow}}\asymp Z^O\), \(q_R\asymp Z^H\), retain the actual row cutoff:

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.35}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.35}

\[
0\le H\le M-O+O(1/\log Z).
\qquad\text{(MF2.35)}
\]

Equality need not hold.

Choose the active zero-exponent base primes and split every \(j=4\) factor. For a large \(j=4\) term use the disjoint assignment

\[
1_{p\mid nb^3}=1_{p\mid n}+1_{p\nmid n}1_{p\mid b}.
\]

Let \(A_0\) be active non-slot radical length outside \(R\); \(S_0\) counts small \(j=4\) and active base-zero factors; \(N_0,B_0\) count large factors assigned to squarefree/cube indices. Let \(z_a=\sum_{i\,\mathrm{active}}z_i\le z\), so \(q_P\asymp Z^{z_a}\). All are nonnegative.

For actual frozen products \(F_{\rm act},N_F,B_F\) of logarithmic norms \(A_0,N_0,B_0\), write a dual index as

\[
\mu=u\lambda^hN_Fn(B_Fb)^3,\quad h\ge-4,\quad
q_n\asymp Z^v,\quad q_b\asymp Z^{\ell_b},\quad
e_\lambda=h\log_Z3,
\]

Here \(v,\ell_b\ge0\); retain source restrictions and allowed \(S\)-parts. Define

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.36}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.36}

\[
T_d=2H+2A_0+2z_a-N_*-N_0-3B_0,\qquad
u_h=\min\{v,z_a,(v+z_a)/3\}.
\qquad\text{(MF2.36)}
\]

For any fixed \(\tau_{\rm ref}>0\), all dyads outside

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.37}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.37}

\[
v+3\ell_b+e_\lambda\le T_d+\tau_{\rm ref}
\qquad\text{(MF2.37)}
\]

have arbitrarily small total size. A retained dyad, including the sum over active tuples, satisfies

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.38}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.38}

\[
\begin{aligned}
E_{\rm ref}
={}&\frac O2+\max(H,v+\ell_b)-S_0-B_0+z_a-u_h-\ell_b-\frac23e_\lambda\\
&-\frac12(T_d-v-3\ell_b-e_\lambda)_+.
\end{aligned}
\qquad\text{(MF2.38)}
\]

For each frozen pair, energy over \(R\) is \(\ll Z^{E_{\rm ref}-O/2+\epsilon}\); summing the \(O\)-dyad and local choices gives \(Z^{E_{\rm ref}+\epsilon}\). Uniformity is as in §7.1.1. Row restrictions are independent of active tuples/dual indices except \(1_{(P,R)=1}\); character, puncture and original product mark are independent of subsequently averaged \(f\). Thresholds depend only on bounded log ranges, profiles, fixed moduli, \(\epsilon,\tau_{\rm ref}\); negative \(e_\lambda=O(1/\log Z)\).

\textbf{Proof.} Each inactive slot has mass \(\sum_{p\in\mathcal P_i}q_p^{-1}\ll Z^\epsilon\), including bounded lists. Freeze it by row-space triangle, making collisions fixed row restrictions; active tuples stay inside the norm.

Equation \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.7}{(MF2.7)}, before the local factors, gives

\[
\frac{|d(\mu)|}{\sqrt{q_\mu}}
\le27\,q_{\lambda^h}^{-1/3}q_{N_Fn}^{-1/2}q_{B_Fb}^{-1}.
\]

Small \(j=4\) and active zero factors contribute \(q_p^{-1/2}\). Large \(j=4\) contributes \(q_p^{1/2}\): squarefree assignment cancels its root denominator, cube assignment leaves \(q_p^{-1/2}\). Shared \(N_F,b\) cube occurrences stay in \(b\). Hence the common coefficient per tuple has exponent

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.39}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.39}

\[
-v/2-\ell_b-e_\lambda/3-(S_0+B_0+z_a)/2.
\qquad\text{(MF2.39)}
\]

The denominator is \(c=c_FF_{\rm act}RP\). With actual annular coordinates

\[
y_R=q_R/Z^H,\quad y_n=q_n/Z^v,\quad
y_b=q_b/Z^{\ell_b},\quad y_i=q_{p_i}/Z^{z_i},
\]

the kernel argument is exactly

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.40}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.40}

\[
\frac{q_\mu X}{q_c^2}
=Y\,y_ny_b^3y_R^{-2}\prod_{i\ {\rm active}}y_i^{-2},
\qquad
Y=q_{c_F}^{-2}Z^{v+3\ell_b+e_\lambda-T_d}.
\qquad\text{(MF2.40)}
\]

This remains exact at shared primes. The annular monomial is bounded above/below, so \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.22}{(MF2.22)} extracts

\[
m_{\rm ker}=Z^{-(T_d-v-3\ell_b-e_\lambda)_+/4}
\]

to be factored from the whole linear row vector, with bounded normalized seminorms.

Before separation remove tails: violating \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.37}{(MF2.37)} forces argument \(>Z^{\tau_{\rm ref}/2}\) for large \(Z\). Use \(|d(\mu)|/\sqrt{q_\mu}\le27\,3^{4/3}\) and local factors \(\le q_p^{1/2}\). Rows, tuples, branches and \(1+q_c^2/X\) cost \(Z^{B_{\rm tail}}\), fixed before kernel order. Apply \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.23}{(MF2.23)} at \(a=X/q_c^2\), \(U=Z^{\tau_{\rm ref}/2}\) with

\[
A>1+2(B_{\rm tail}+D)/\tau_{\rm ref}.
\]

This bounds all discarded \(\mu\) by \(O(Z^{-D})\) times a finite seminorm and fixed height power.

Separate each retained \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.40}{(MF2.40)} once, then enlarge the positive row norm to \(q_R\ll Z^H\). Phase separation gives \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.29}{(MF2.29)} with

\[
K=Z^H,\qquad N=Z^v,\qquad B=Z^{\ell_b},\qquad L=Z^{z_a}.
\]

Leave \(q_P^{-1/2}\) inside that norm. The outside squared coefficient is \(Z^{-v-2\ell_b-2e_\lambda/3-S_0-B_0+\epsilon}\). Since

\[
\max\{v,z_a,2(v+z_a)/3\}=v+z_a-u_h,
\]

the hybrid norm gives \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.38}{(MF2.38)}'s first line without \(O/2\); \(m_{\rm ker}^2\) supplies its last term.

The powerful count proved after \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:B.23}{(42)} is \(O(Z^{O/2})\); the supported part divides \(\operatorname{rad}(Sfr_\rho)\), giving \(Z^\epsilon\) choices, as do local branches. Divisor Cauchy and the positive frozen-part sum add \(O/2+\epsilon\) once; active tuples are already included. This proves \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.38}{(MF2.38)}.

\hypertarget{general-label-and-puncture-width}{}

\label{general-label-and-puncture-width}

General label and puncture width

At an active non-slot prime, the coefficient of its log-norm in \(2A_0-N_0-S_0-4B_0\) is

\begingroup\small
\begin{longtable}{@{}p{\linewidth}@{}}
\toprule
\endhead
\bottomrule
\endlastfoot
\textit{Local choice}: \(j\) odd or \(j=2\); \enspace \textit{Coefficient}: \(2\) \\
\addlinespace[3pt]
\textit{Local choice}: \(j=0\), active; \enspace \textit{Coefficient}: \(1\) \\
\addlinespace[3pt]
\textit{Local choice}: \(j=4\), small or assigned to \(n\); \enspace \textit{Coefficient}: \(1\) \\
\addlinespace[3pt]
\textit{Local choice}: \(j=4\), assigned to \(b\); \enspace \textit{Coefficient}: \(-2\) \\
\addlinespace[3pt]
\textit{Local choice}: \(j=0\), inactive; \enspace \textit{Coefficient}: \(0\) \\
\end{longtable}
\endgroup

Residual primes contribute two. Primewise these coefficients are bounded by valuations of \((q_k^2/q_{k_{\rm pow}})q_fq_{r_\rho}\): powerful valuation \(e\ge2\) supplies \(e\), simple rows two, label/puncture-only primes one; overlaps increase availability. Thus

\[
q_R^2 Z^{2A_0-N_0-S_0-4B_0}
\le C_S\,\frac{q_k^2}{q_{k_{\rm pow}}}\,q_fq_{r_\rho},
\]

and therefore

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.41}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.41}

\[
T_d-S_0-B_0
\le2M-O+Q+V-N_*+2z_a+O(1/\log Z).
\qquad\text{(MF2.41)}
\]

This remains uniform in actual \(f,r_\rho\) when completion gives \(N_*=N-3\ell_h\), without replacing \(H\) by \(M-O\).

With no slots, \(z_a=u_h=0\). On a retained dyad, \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.38}{(MF2.38)} gives

\[
E_{\rm ref}\le O/2+\max\{H,T_d-S_0-B_0\}
+O(1/\log Z)+\tau_{\rm ref}.
\]

Drop nonpositive terms for the row branch; for the column branch use \(v\le T_d-3\ell_b-e_\lambda+\tau_{\rm ref}\). Apply \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.35}{(MF2.35)}, \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.41}{(MF2.41)}, choose small buffer/losses and sum dyads:

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.42}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.42}

\[
\sum_{\substack{0<q_k\ll Z^M\\q_{k_{\rm pow}}\asymp Z^O}}
|\mathcal T_1(Z^{N_*};k;V)|^2
\ll_\epsilon
Z^{\,O/2+\max\{M-O,\;2M-O+Q+V-N_*\}+\epsilon}.
\qquad\text{(MF2.42)}
\]

In particular, with \(f=r_\rho=1\), summing \(O\) gives the unmarked completed bound

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.43}{}

\label{mf:eq:B2.43}

\textbf{(MF2.43)} is the unmarked bound \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:4.4}{(33)}, with \((H,N)=(Z^M,Z^{N_*})\) and equivalent power notation \(Z^{\max(M,2M-N_*)+\epsilon}\), for the tests admitted in §7.1.1. The preceding argument extends its annular/Gaussian statement to that test class.

This includes the balanced energy \(Z^{1+\epsilon}\) at \(M=N_*=1\).

\hypertarget{short-marked-energy-without-label-or-puncture}{}

\label{short-marked-energy-without-label-or-puncture}

Short marked energy without label or puncture

When \(f=r_\rho=1\), every active non-slot prime outside \(R\) divides the powerful part. Thus

\[
2A_0\le O+O(1/\log Z),\qquad 0\le N_0\le A_0.
\]

\textbf{Theorem 7.1} (Short marked completed energy). \emph{\hypertarget{mf:marked-energy}{}\label{mf:marked-energy}If \(M+2z\le N_*\), then for every \(\epsilon>0\), \hypertarget{mf:eq:B2.44}{}\label{mf:eq:B2.44}}

\[
\sum_{0<q_k\ll Z^M}|\mathcal T_d(Z^{N_*};k;V)|^2
\ll_\epsilon
Z^{\,M+(N_*+2z-2M)_+/4+\epsilon}.
\qquad\text{(MF2.44)}
\]

\emph{Proof.} Use \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.38}{(MF2.38)}, suppressing \(O(1/\log Z)\) and tiny \(\tau_{\rm ref}\). Since

\[
T_d-H\le M+2z-N_*\le0,
\]

retention gives \(\max(H,v+\ell_b)=H+O(\tau_{\rm ref}+1/\log Z)\). If \(u_h=z_a\), then \(E_{\rm ref}\le M\) up to this error; otherwise \(u_h\ge v/2\). Put \(y_d=v+3\ell_b+e_\lambda\); \(\ell_b\ge0\), \(e_\lambda\ge-O(1/\log Z)\) imply

\[
u_h+\ell_b+\frac23e_\lambda
+\frac12(T_d-y_d)_+
\ge \frac14y_d+\frac12(T_d-y_d)_+-O(1/\log Z)
\ge\frac14T_d-O(1/\log Z).
\]

With \(\Delta_H=M-O-H\ge-O(1/\log Z)\), substitution into \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.38}{(MF2.38)} gives

\[
\begin{aligned}
4(E_{\rm ref}-M)
&\le N_*+2z_a-2M-2\Delta_H
-(2A_0-N_0+B_0+4S_0)+O(\tau_{\rm ref}+1/\log Z)\\
&\le N_*+2z-2M+O(\tau_{\rm ref}+1/\log Z).
\end{aligned}
\]

Restore the row branch, tails and losses to obtain \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.44}{(MF2.44)}, independently of moments or zero-free conclusions. \(\square\)

\hypertarget{the-inverse-and-plain-moment-inductions}{}

\label{the-inverse-and-plain-moment-inductions}

\hypertarget{the-inverse-and-plain-moment-inductions-heading-2}{%
\paragraph{7.2 The inverse and plain moment inductions}}

We prove inverse moments and unconditional zero-slot/canonical common-profile positive-slot plain moments from the finite identities and reflected energy above. Inverse descent terminates at reflection; plain descent is independent of the inverse moment.

\hypertarget{common-conventions-and-analytic-bookkeeping}{}

\label{common-conventions-and-analytic-bookkeeping}

\hypertarget{common-conventions-and-analytic-bookkeeping-heading-2}{%
\subparagraph{7.2.1 Common conventions and analytic bookkeeping}}

Retain §7.1's conventions: primary ideal columns avoid \(S\); all element rows and displayed zeros remain. Fixed ray moduli lie in \(S\), moving punctures are separate. Put

\[
a_0(n)=\overline{\alpha(n)}\gamma_2(n)\qquad(n\text{ squarefree}).
\]

For disjoint prime lists \(\mathcal P_i\), fixed throughout the current row and label averages, define a \textbf{product mark}

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.1}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.1}

\textbf{(MF3.1)} is the product mark \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.3}{(MF2.3)} on slot subset \(I\), with lists and coefficients fixed throughout the current row and label averages.

Slot count is fixed; coefficients need not be multiplicative and include individual smooth weights. Product form, beyond a divisor bound, allows

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.2}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.2}

\[
\mathbf1_{p\mid D_1\cdots D_h n}
=\sum_{j=1}^h\mathbf1_{p\mid D_j}\prod_{a<j}\mathbf1_{p\nmid D_a}
 +\mathbf1_{p\mid n}\prod_{a\le h}\mathbf1_{p\nmid D_a}
\qquad\text{(MF3.2)}
\]

to assign each slot first to one extracted factor or retain it on the residual column. Assigned coefficients are divisor-bounded; survivors retain product form. Allocate the two copies of a squared mark independently.

\textbf{Common-profile lemma.} For finitely many compact normalized-norm profiles, radial Schwartz kernels and fixed-ray Fourier expansions, keep nonsmooth arithmetic restrictions in outer weights. Multiply a coupled \(H(y_1,\ldots,y_d)\) by larger individual cutoffs equal to one on current supports and use

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.3}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.3}

\[
H(y)=(2\pi)^{-d}\int_{\mathbf R^d}\widehat H(t)\prod_jy_j^{it_j}\,dt,
\qquad
\int|\widehat H(t)|\langle t\rangle^Jdt
 \ll_{J,d}\max_{|\beta|\le2r}\|D^\beta H\|_\infty,
\quad 2r>J+d,
\qquad\text{(MF3.3)}
\]

Here \(D_j=y_j\partial_{y_j}\), \(\langle t\rangle=1+|t|\). Define the measure on the ambient box before choosing row or live label; norm relations restrict evaluation points only. Include inverse roots once in the coupled profile; individual column tests get only cutoffs and Fourier powers.

Logarithmic integration by parts with \((1-\Delta)^r\) proves the bound. For \(K(A\prod y_j^{e_j})\), Euler derivatives are fixed multiples of \((x\partial_x)^rK(x)\) uniformly in \(A>0\), so fixed derivative orders add no \(Z\) power; norm heights cost polynomials. Also,

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.4}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.4}

\textbf{(MF3.4)} is \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:B.25}{(44)} on \(\mathcal O\), with \((U,A)=(Y,B)\) and the kernel seminorm absorbed in the constant. It holds for \(a>0\), \(Y\ge1\), \(B>1\).

Discard tails on the genuine expression before coprime extension or absolute Fourier integration. Choose a sector-fixed outer ball whose complement forces kernel argument \(>Z^\tau\), retaining the full kernel inside. If raw factors and \(1+a^{-1}\) cost \(Z^{B_0}\), choose \(B>1+(B_0+T)/\tau\) in \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.4}{(MF3.4)} for \(O(Z^{-T})\). Polynomial scale bounds fix \(B_0\) before \(B\).

For uniformity choose terminal profile/height orders, then propagate backwards through the bounded-depth acyclic graph using \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.3}{(MF3.3)}. Normalize discrete counting measures after extracting their \(Z^b\) mass once. Cauchy averages child exponents; Minkowski takes their maximum after coefficient mass, so numerical loss grows only with path length. Fix quotient/separation cutoffs and bounded clipping families before the final \(Z\) threshold. A nonempty nominal negative-length window has \(1\le Z^{-n}\le B\) (fixed upper endpoint), hence clips to length zero by bounded dilation.

\textbf{Masked primitive Poisson.} For primitive zero-extended \(\psi\bmod\mathfrak m\) and radial Schwartz \(\Phi\), use

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.5}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.5}

\textbf{(MF3.5)} is \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:C.3}{(47)}, under \((H,m,r)=(K,\mathfrak m,R)\), \(\gamma(\psi)=\gamma(\psi;\mathfrak m)\) and \(\widehat\Phi=\mathcal F\Phi\).

The proof is §\protect\hyperlink{cf:sec:C.1}{4.1}'s complete mask expansion and primitive finite transform; Parseval gives \(|\gamma|=1\). For the primitive principal character use modulus one, \(\gamma=1\), including \(h=0\). Its nonzero frequencies cost \(O_\Phi(d(\operatorname{rad}R))\) since \(A\sum_{h\ne0}|\mathcal F\Phi(Aq_h)|\ll1\); restore them with the original outer mask at diagonal cost. Expand fixed rays into characters first, keeping deleted conductor zeros in \(R\).

\hypertarget{a-canonical-marked-energy}{}

\label{a-canonical-marked-energy}

\hypertarget{a-canonical-marked-energy-heading-2}{%
\subparagraph{7.2.2 A canonical marked energy}}

Fix squarefree \(\rho(n)=1_{(n,r_\rho)=1}\) independent of row \(k\) and label \(f\), with \(Q=\log_Zq_{r_\rho}\). Let \(q_f\asymp Z^V\) and label-only \(0\le w(f)\le Cd(f)^C\). For \(F=N+V\), define

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.6}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.6}

\par\noindent\resizebox{\linewidth}{!}{\begin{minipage}{\dimexpr\linewidth+19.25075pt\relax}\[
\mathcal E(N,V,M)=Z^{-V}\sum_f w(f)\sum_{0<q_k\ll Z^M}
 \left|Z^{-N/2}\sum_{n\ {\rm sf}}a_0(n)\nu(n)\rho(n)
       \chi_n(k)\chi_n(f)^4d_I(n)W(q_n/Z^N)\right|^2.
\qquad\text{(MF3.6)}
\]\end{minipage}}\par

Here \(\nu\) is fixed finite-ray, lengths are bounded, slot sum is \(\le z_0\), and no additional \(n\)-coefficient is allowed.

\textbf{Proposition 7.2} (Canonical marked energy). \emph{\hypertarget{mf:canonical-energy}{}\label{mf:canonical-energy} For any fixed \(c_*>0\), \hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.7}{}\label{mf:eq:B3.7}}

\[
F-M-Q-z_0\ge c_*,\qquad4F-3M-6z_0\ge c_*
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\mathcal E(N,V,M)\ll Z^{F+\epsilon}.
\qquad\text{(MF3.7)}
\]

\emph{Uniformity includes moving punctures, the stated label weights and every surviving slot subset, always with the original cap \(z_0\).}

\emph{Proof.} \textbf{Terminal completion.} Choose \(0<d\le c_*/200\). Set \(\Psi_k(n)=\nu(n)\rho(n)\chi_n(k)\chi_n(f)^4\) multiplicatively on all ideals. For \(V_*(y)=y^{1/2}W(y)\) define

\[
T_{d_*}(X;k)=\sum_{n\ {\rm sf},b}
 \frac{a_0(n)\Psi_k(n)\overline{\alpha(b)}^3\Psi_k(b)^3}
      {\sqrt{q_n}\,q_b}\,d_*(nb^3)V_*(q_nq_b^3/X).
\]

The exact cube inversion is

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.8}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.8}

\par\noindent\resizebox{\linewidth}{!}{\begin{minipage}{\dimexpr\linewidth+33.08385pt\relax}\[
Z^{-N/2}\sum_{n\ {\rm sf}}a_0(n)\Psi_k(n)d_I(n)W(q_n/Z^N)
=\sum_h\frac{\mu(h)\overline{\alpha(h)}^3\Psi_k(h)^3}{q_h}
 T_{d_h}(Z^N/q_h^3;k),\quad d_h(A)=d_I(h^3A).
\qquad\text{(MF3.8)}
\]\end{minipage}}\par

Opening the completion and setting \(c=hb\) leaves mark \(d_I(nc^3)\) independent of \(h\); \(\sum_{h\mid c}\mu(h)=1_{c=1}\) verifies the identity, normalization and masks.

Use fixed dyadic windows with unit center zero. If \(V<d\) and the \(h\)-center \(\ell_1<d\), freeze \(h\), assign slots by \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.2}{(MF3.2)} and apply reflected energy (RE) at \(N_*=N-3\log_Zq_h\), surviving cap \(z_a\le z_0\). The factor \(\Psi_k(h)^3\) contracts the joint \((f,k)\) norm and \(\sum_{q_h\asymp Z^{\ell_1}}q_h^{-1}\ll1\). If \(c\) is the current margin in \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.7}{(MF3.7)} and \(\eta\) bounds window errors, RE gives

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.9}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.9}

\[
H\le M-O+\eta,\qquad
T_d-S_0-B_0\le M-O+2z_a-z_0-c+5d+4\eta.
\qquad\text{(MF3.9)}
\]

For the second, apply \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B2.41}{(MF2.41)}, the first margin, and \(2V+3\log_Zq_h<5d+3\eta\).

Retention gives \(v+3b+e_\lambda\le T_d+\tau_{\rm ref}\), \(e_\lambda\ge-\eta\). The row branch yields \(E\le M+z_a+5\eta/3\le F-c+5\eta/3\). The column branch gives \(E\le O/2+T_d-S_0-B_0+5\eta/3+\tau_{\rm ref}\) if \(u=z_a\); otherwise \(u\ge v/2\) gives \(E\le O/2+(T_d-S_0-B_0)/2+z_a+7\eta/6+\tau_{\rm ref}/2\). Substitution and the respective margins give

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.10}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.10}

\[
E\le F-2c+5d+17\eta/3+\tau_{\rm ref},\qquad
E\le F-M/4-3c/4+5d/2+19\eta/6+\tau_{\rm ref}/2.
\qquad\text{(MF3.10)}
\]

These are \(<F\) for \(c\ge c_*/2\) and \(\eta,\tau_{\rm ref}\) and reflected divisor loss \(\le c_*/1000\). Summing labels costs \(Z^{V+o(1)}\), canceled by \(Z^{-V}\).

\textbf{Nonterminal block.} If \(V<d\), \(\ell_1\ge d\), reopen \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.8}{(MF3.8)}, combine its cube with \(h\) into \(b\), center \(\ell\), and put \(r=N-3\ell\). If \(V\ge d\), take \(b=1\), \(\ell=0\), \(r=N\). Joint Hilbert-space triangle and common-profile separation reduce to

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.11}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.11}

\par\noindent\resizebox{\linewidth}{!}{\begin{minipage}{\dimexpr\linewidth+67.6749pt\relax}\[
Z^{-r-2\ell-V}\sum_f w(f)\sum_k\Phi(q_k/Z^M)
 \left|\sum_{q_b\asymp Z^\ell}\beta(b,f)\chi_b(k)^3
  \sum_{n\ {\rm sf}}a_0(n)\nu(n)\rho(n)\chi_n(k)\chi_n(f)^4
        d_I(nb^3)W(q_n/Z^r)\right|^2,
\qquad\text{(MF3.11)}
\]\end{minipage}}\par

Here \(V+\ell\ge d\), \(\beta\) is divisor-bounded, independent of \(k,n\), and includes \((b,f)=1\); \(b,n\) may share primes. We return this to \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.6}{(MF3.6)} with shorter rows.

\textbf{First Poisson transformation.} In the square write

\[
\mathfrak B=\operatorname{rad}(b_1b_2),\qquad
n_i=A_iCu_i,\quad A_i=(n_i,\mathfrak B),\quad
C=(n_1/A_1,n_2/A_2).
\]

Then \(u_1,u_2\) are coprime squarefree and avoid \(C\mathfrak B\). At \(p\mid\mathfrak B\), put \(a_i=1_{p\mid A_i}\), \(\pi=v_p(b_1b_2)\bmod2\), \(t_p=a_1-a_2+3\pi\bmod6\), and \(R_1=\prod_{t_p\ne0}p\). Primitive character and residual mask are

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.12}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.12}

\[
\chi_{u_1}\overline{\chi_{u_2}}\prod_{t_p\ne0}\chi_p^{t_p},
\qquad\mathfrak m_1=u_1u_2R_1,
\qquad R_{\rm mask}=C\mathfrak B/R_1.
\qquad\text{(MF3.12)}
\]

Apply \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.5}{(MF3.5)}, with \(d_k\mid C\mathfrak B/R_1\) and frequency \(h\). Also denote the centers of \(A_i,C,R_1,d_k\) by \(A_i,B,R,\delta\). Actual conductor length is \(\log_Z(q_{n_1}q_{n_2}/(q_{A_1}q_{A_2}q_C^2))+\log_Zq_{R_1}\). The principal case forces \(u_1=u_2=1\), \(\pi=0\), \(a_1=a_2\) everywhere, hence \(n_1=n_2\) and square \(b_1b_2\). Counting even all cube pairs gives normalized \(O(Z^{M+o(1)})\), also covering restored principal nonzero frequencies with the same mask.

CRT, \(\gamma_1(u)\gamma_2(u)=\mu(u)\alpha(u)G(u)\) and reciprocity give inverse residual coefficients. Expand their joint fixed-ray factor \(G([u_1u_2^{-1}])R([u_1u_2^{-1}],[\prod p^{t_p}])\) before coprime extension. Set \(E=\prod_{\pi=1}p^{a_1+a_2}\), \(y=hf^2E\). The residual exponents \(4a_i+1_{t_p\ne0}(1\pm t_p)+\pi(a_1+a_2)\bmod6\) agree on both sides by this exhaustive table:

\begingroup\small
\begin{longtable}{@{}p{\linewidth}@{}}
\toprule
\endhead
\bottomrule
\endlastfoot
\textit{\(\pi\)}: 0; \enspace \textit{\(a_1\)}: 0; \enspace \textit{\(a_2\)}: 0; \enspace \textit{\(t_p\)}: 0; \enspace \textit{Residual exponent}: 0 \\
\addlinespace[3pt]
\textit{\(\pi\)}: 0; \enspace \textit{\(a_1\)}: 1; \enspace \textit{\(a_2\)}: 0; \enspace \textit{\(t_p\)}: 1; \enspace \textit{Residual exponent}: 0 \\
\addlinespace[3pt]
\textit{\(\pi\)}: 0; \enspace \textit{\(a_1\)}: 0; \enspace \textit{\(a_2\)}: 1; \enspace \textit{\(t_p\)}: 5; \enspace \textit{Residual exponent}: 0 \\
\addlinespace[3pt]
\textit{\(\pi\)}: 0; \enspace \textit{\(a_1\)}: 1; \enspace \textit{\(a_2\)}: 1; \enspace \textit{\(t_p\)}: 0; \enspace \textit{Residual exponent}: 4 \\
\addlinespace[3pt]
\textit{\(\pi\)}: 1; \enspace \textit{\(a_1\)}: 0; \enspace \textit{\(a_2\)}: 0; \enspace \textit{\(t_p\)}: 3; \enspace \textit{Residual exponent}: 4 \\
\addlinespace[3pt]
\textit{\(\pi\)}: 1; \enspace \textit{\(a_1\)}: 1; \enspace \textit{\(a_2\)}: 0; \enspace \textit{\(t_p\)}: 4; \enspace \textit{Residual exponent}: 4 \\
\addlinespace[3pt]
\textit{\(\pi\)}: 1; \enspace \textit{\(a_1\)}: 0; \enspace \textit{\(a_2\)}: 1; \enspace \textit{\(t_p\)}: 2; \enspace \textit{Residual exponent}: 4 \\
\addlinespace[3pt]
\textit{\(\pi\)}: 1; \enspace \textit{\(a_1\)}: 1; \enspace \textit{\(a_2\)}: 1; \enspace \textit{\(t_p\)}: 3; \enspace \textit{Residual exponent}: 4 \\
\end{longtable}
\endgroup

An exponent zero still means a puncture. Write \(b_1b_2=q^2s\), \(s\) squarefree, and put

\[
J_2=\prod_{\pi=0,\ a_1=a_2=1}p,
\quad J=sJ_2,\quad q=J_2q_0.
\]

Then \(J\) is squarefree and the residual factor is \(\chi_n(J)^4 1_{(n,\operatorname{rad}q_0)=1}\). For center \(j\) of \(J\), the identities

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.13}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.13}

\[
\log_Zq_{q_0}=\tfrac12\log_Z(q_{b_1}q_{b_2})
 -\tfrac12\log_Zq_s-\log_Zq_{J_2}\ge0,
\qquad
\log_Z\frac{q_{R_1}q_E}{q_{A_1}q_{A_2}}
=\log_Zq_s-2\log_Zq_{J_2}
\qquad\text{(MF3.13)}
\]

give the retained row enclosure

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.14}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.14}

\[
H_c=2r-2B-M+4\ell+2V+\delta-j,
\qquad H_+=H_c+12\eta+\tau,
\qquad 0<q_y\le Z^{H_+}.
\qquad\text{(MF3.14)}
\]

The actual argument is \(Z^Mq_h/(q_{d_k}q_{\mathfrak m_1})\); if \(\le Z^\tau\), \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.13}{(MF3.13)} gives \(\log_Zq_y\le H_c+12\eta+\tau\). Discard only this outer ball's complement by \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.4}{(MF3.4)}; \(H_+<0\) leaves no nonzero rows.

Extend the quotient-free expression to individually allowed \(u_1,u_2\), retaining full kernel, formal product conductor and outer ball. Insert \(1_{(u_1,u_2)=1}=\sum_{t'\mid(u_1,u_2)}\mu(t')\) and write \(u_i=t'x_i\), center \(t\). The complete Möbius sum removes artificial noncoprime conductors; squarefreeness retains \((x_i,t')=1\). With

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.15}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.15}

\[
s_i=r-A_i-B-t,\qquad
\varkappa_i=M-2r-2\ell-V-\delta+A_i+B-R/2,
\qquad\text{(MF3.15)}
\]

the separated inverse polynomial on side \(i\) is

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.16}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.16}

\[
P_i(y)=\sum_{x\ {\rm sf}}\mu(x)\nu_i(x)\rho(x)
 \overline{\chi_x(y)}\chi_x(CJ)^4\chi_x(d_k)
 \mathbf1_{(x,\operatorname{rad}(q_0t'))=1}d_{I_i}(x)W_i(q_x/Z^{s_i}).
\qquad\text{(MF3.16)}
\]

The ray character \(\nu_i\) is fixed. The \(f\) factor became \(\overline{\chi_x(f^2)}\), explaining row \(y\) and conjugation. Priority allocation to \((b_i,A_i,C,t';x_i)\) leaves original slot subsets; \(CJ,q_0,t'\) supply all earlier survival zeros.

The first common profile uses independent normalized norms

\[
(A_1,A_2,C,d_k,R_1,t',h,x_1,x_2).
\]

Apart from its larger cutoffs, its real root and kernel are

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.17}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.17}

\par\noindent\resizebox{\linewidth}{!}{\begin{minipage}{\dimexpr\linewidth+82.78163pt\relax}\[
y_d^{-1}y_R^{-1/2}y_t^{-1}(y_1y_2)^{-1/2}
 W(y_{A_1}y_Cy_ty_1)\overline{W(y_{A_2}y_Cy_ty_2)}
 K_1\!\left(A_1^{\rm ker}\frac{y_h}{y_dy_Ry_t^2y_1y_2}\right),
\quad A_1^{\rm ker}=Z^{M+h_1-\delta-R-2t-s_1-s_2},
\qquad\text{(MF3.17)}
\]\end{minipage}}\par

Here \(h_1\) centers bare frequency. Multiplying the profile by \(Z^{-9\eta/2}\) leaves outside side factors \(Z^{(\varkappa_i+9\eta/2)/2}\), exactly separating prefactor and kernel. Fresh supports satisfy \(|\log_Zq_{x_i}-s_i|\le4\eta\); profile bounds are uniform for \(A_1^{\rm ker}>0\).

Apply weighted Cauchy to the \textbf{whole} outer tuple before bounding weights. At fixed reconstruction and \(y\ne0\),

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.18}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.18}

\[
\sum_{f^2E\mid(y)}w(f)\ll d((y))^{C+1}\ll Z^{o(1)}.
\qquad\text{(MF3.18)}
\]

Thus \(f,h\) disappear together through \(h=y/(f^2E)\), retaining \(-V\) in \(\varkappa_i\). Only now smoothly majorize the positive row ball at \(Z^{H_+}\). Average \(J\), freeze \(q_0\). If \(r_{\rm diff}\) is the radical length of even-parity primes with \(a_1\ne a_2\), the table gives \(\log_Zq_{R_1}=\log_Zq_s+r_{\rm diff}\); hence

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.19}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.19}

\[
\log_Zq_{q_0}\le\ell+R/2-j+5\eta/2.
\qquad\text{(MF3.19)}
\]

Counting \(q_0\) and \(J\) costs \(Z^{\ell+R/2+7\eta/2+o(1)}\) once.

\textbf{Second Poisson.} On one first-Cauchy side expand \(|P_i(y)|^2\), write \(x_a=g'z_a\), \(g'=(x_1,x_2)\) with center \(g\), and apply \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.5}{(MF3.5)}: character \(\overline{\chi_{z_1}}\chi_{z_2}\), modulus \(z_1z_2\), mask \(g'\), divisor \(d_2\mid g'\) with center \(\theta\), frequency \(k''\). Put

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.20}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.20}

\par\noindent\resizebox{\linewidth}{!}{\begin{minipage}{\dimexpr\linewidth+50.12982pt\relax}\[
\lambda_c=H_c-\theta-(s_i-g),\qquad
M_c=2(s_i-g)-H_c+\delta+2\theta
=M-4\ell-2A_i-2t-2g+2\theta-2V+j,
\quad M'=M_c+\eta.
\qquad\text{(MF3.20)}
\]\end{minipage}}\par

Retain \(0<q_{d_kd_2k''}\le Z^{M'}\). Conductor error is \(10\eta\); two \(d_2\) and one \(d_k\) contribute \(3\eta\). Subtracting \(H_+=H_c+12\eta+\tau\) cancels the same \(\tau\), making the complement an actual-ratio tail under \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.4}{(MF3.4)}.

The principal case \(z_1=z_2=1\) has nominal total exponent

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.21}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.21}

\[
\varkappa_i+H_c+s_i+B+t+\ell+R/2=F-B-j.
\qquad\text{(MF3.21)}
\]

Prefactor, enclosure, diagonal window and outer counts add \(\le26\eta+\tau+o(1)\), within target, including restored principal nonzero frequencies with the same mask.

Use \(\mu(z)\gamma_{-1}(z)=a_0(z)\chi_z(-1)\overline{G(z)}\). The paired ray factor \(G([z_2z_1^{-1}])\) survives common-factor removal. Extend separate columns/full kernel off coprimality, insert the full Möbius sum \(z_a=v'n_a\), \(v'\) squarefree with center \(v\). Retain \((v',g'CJ)=(g',CJ)=(v',k'')=1\). Set

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.22}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.22}

\par\noindent\resizebox{\linewidth}{!}{\begin{minipage}{\dimexpr\linewidth+57.87354pt\relax}\[
\gamma=(q_0,t',r_g),\quad r_g=g'/d_2,\qquad
k_{\rm new}=d_kd_2k'',\qquad f_{\rm new}=JCd_2v',
\qquad\rho_\gamma(n)=\rho(n)\mathbf1_{(n,\operatorname{rad}(q_0t'r_g))=1}.
\qquad\text{(MF3.22)}
\]\end{minipage}}\par

Zeros make \(J,C,d_2,v'\) pairwise coprime squarefree, hence \(f_{\rm new}\) squarefree, and

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.23}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.23}

\[
\chi_n(d_k)\chi_n(k'')\overline{\chi_n(d_2)}
 \chi_n(CJ)^4\chi_n(v')^4
=\chi_n(k_{\rm new})\chi_n(f_{\rm new})^4.
\qquad\text{(MF3.23)}
\]

Use \(\overline{\chi_n(d_2)}=\chi_n(d_2)^5\) with zeros and \(a_0(v'n)=a_0(v')a_0(n)\chi_n(v')^4\). Expand \(G([n_2n_1^{-1}])\) to get the same fixed \(\nu'\) on both sides. Priority allocation leaves original slot subsets, with survival zeros supplied by \(f_{\rm new},\rho_\gamma\). Freeze only \(\gamma\); average all four label factors.

The formal child lengths and their actual enclosing windows are

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.24}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.24}

\[
\begin{aligned}
N_c&=r-A_i-B-t-g-v,& V_c&=B+\theta+v+j,\\
F_c&=N_c+V_c,& N'&=\max(0,N_c),\\
F'&=N'+V_c,&0\le\delta_N&=N'-N_c\le6\eta.
\end{aligned}
\qquad\text{(MF3.24)}
\]

Fresh column and label errors are \(6\eta,4\eta\). Omit empty windows; other clipping is bounded dilation. Put \(D_c=\ell+A_i+t+g-\theta+V\); then

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.25}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.25}

\[
\begin{aligned}
F_c&=F-D_c-2\ell+j,&M_c&=M-2D_c-2\ell+j,\\
F_c-M_c&=F-M+D_c,&
4F_c-3M_c&=4F-3M+2(A_i+t+g-\theta+V)+j.
\end{aligned}\qquad\text{(MF3.25)}
\]

Project fixed triples from the genuine reconstruction set \textbf{before} positive enlargement. Each has a source witness, giving \(g-\theta\ge-2\eta\), \(j\le2\ell+3\eta\), \(\log_Zq_{q_0}\le\ell+\eta\), hence \(Q'\le Q+\ell+t+g-\theta+4\eta\le Q+D_c+4\eta\). Therefore

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.26}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.26}

\par\noindent\resizebox{\linewidth}{!}{\begin{minipage}{\dimexpr\linewidth+9.220320000000001pt\relax}\[
F'-M'-Q'-z_0\ge c-5\eta,\quad
4F'-3M'-6z_0\ge c-7\eta,\quad
M-M'\ge2(V+\ell)-8\eta,\quad F'\le F+11\eta.
\qquad\text{(MF3.26)}
\]\end{minipage}}\par

These hold on structural triples, not every point of a containing ball.

\begin{sloppypar}
The second common profile uses \((g',d_2,v',k'',n_1,n_2)\), root \(y_d^{-1}y_v^{-1}(y_1y_2)^{-1/2}\), windows \(W_i(y_gy_vy_1)\overline{W_i(y_gy_vy_2)}\), and kernel \(K_2(A_2^{\rm ker}y_k/(y_dy_v^2y_1y_2))\), \(A_2^{\rm ker}=Z^{H_++h_2-\theta-2v-2N_c}\). Multiplying by \(Z^{-6\eta}\) leaves exact outside factor \(Z^{\lambda_c+18\eta+\tau}\). Fresh tests \(\omega_a(Z^{\delta_N}y)y^{\pm it_a}\) and density are independent of \(k_{\rm new},f_{\rm new},\gamma\). Retain both extracted squared magnitudes, priority masks, \(\mu(d_2)\mu(v')\) and frequency mask in signed outer weights. Fourier inversion restores terms exactly; then weighted Cauchy over all outer tuples permits bounding common zeros by one.
\end{sloppypar}

At fixed \(\gamma,f_{\rm new},k_{\rm new}\), with at most \(K_0\) original slots, split \(f_{\rm new}=JCd_2v'\), \(J=sJ_2\), reconstruct \(b_1b_2=q_0^2J_2^2s\), and choose \(A_i,d_k\). Multiplicity is at most

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.27}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.27}

\[
D(f_{\rm new})C(\gamma),\qquad
D(f)=d(f)^{9+4K_0},\quad
C(\gamma)=d(q_0)^{5+2K_0}d(t')^{2K_0}d(r_g)^{2K_0}.
\qquad\text{(MF3.27)}
\]

Indeed \(d_k\mid f_{\rm new}\operatorname{rad}q_0\), and extracted slot primes divide reconstructed factors, giving those divisor powers. The frequency is then determined; old \(f,h\) are absent. By \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.19}{(MF3.19)}, distinct triples have weighted count

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.28}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.28}

\[
\ll Z^{C_c+11\eta/2+o(1)},\qquad
C_c=\ell+R/2-j+t+g-\theta.
\qquad\text{(MF3.28)}
\]

Extract that count once and normalize its measure. The unnormalized squared child is \(Z^{F'}\mathcal E\); a child bound \(Z^{F'+\epsilon_{\rm child}}\) therefore contributes \(Z^{2F'+\epsilon_{\rm child}}\). The exact balance

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.29}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.29}

\[
\varkappa_i+\lambda_c+C_c+2F_c=F
\qquad\text{(MF3.29)}
\]

gives exponent \(F+40\eta+\tau+\pi+\epsilon_{\rm child}\): profiles and triple count contribute \(9\eta/2+18\eta+11\eta/2=28\eta\), clipping \(2\delta_N\le12\eta\).
