Here \(\pi\) combines freely chosen divisor, ray, dyadic and coefficient losses. Cauchy takes geometric means; neither exponent nor triple mass doubles.

\textbf{Closure.} Put \(D=\lceil(M_{\max}+2)/d\rceil+1\). At depth \(h\) use margin \(c_h=c_*-7h\eta\) and cap \(M\le M_{\max}-hd\). Choose

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.30}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.30}

\[
\eta\le\min\{d/16,c_*/(14D),\epsilon/(160D),c_*/1000\},
\qquad\tau,\pi\le\epsilon/(4D),\qquad
\tau\le c_*/1000,
\qquad\text{(MF3.30)}
\]

with the reflected losses above. By \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.26}{(MF3.26)}, rows decrease by \(\ge d\), margins stay \(\ge c_*/2\), and \(F\) remains bounded through depth \(D\); nonnegative row lengths preclude a strict call there. Allocate losses within \(\pi\), fix raw tails by \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.4}{(MF3.4)}, propagate finite smooth/height orders backwards, then take \(Z\) large for window errors. Path loss is \(<\epsilon\). Completion, principal counts and strict descent prove \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.7}{(MF3.7)}. \(\square\)

\hypertarget{the-marked-inverse-estimate-and-inverse-amplification}{}

\label{the-marked-inverse-estimate-and-inverse-amplification}

\hypertarget{the-marked-inverse-estimate-and-inverse-amplification-heading-2}{%
\subparagraph{7.2.3 The marked inverse estimate and inverse amplification}}

Let \(\varphi_u(n)=\nu(n)\chi_n(u)^{\varepsilon_\chi}\), with one common sign \(\varepsilon_\chi\in\{\pm1\}\), and put

\[
M_u(Z^r)=Z^{-r/2}\sum_n\mu(n)\varphi_u(n)W(q_n/Z^r),\qquad
Q_u=\prod_i\left(Z^{-z_i/2}\sum_{p\in\mathcal P_i}a_i(p)\varphi_u(p)\right).
\]

Lists are fixed disjoint, \(|a_i(p)|\le1\), \(q_p\asymp Z^{z_i}\); individual annular weights are absorbed at fixed supremum cost. Retain natural zeros. Length ranges and slot count are bounded/fixed; \(c_1,c_2>0\) are fixed.

\textbf{Theorem 7.3} (Marked inverse moment). \emph{\hypertarget{mf:inverse}{}\label{mf:inverse}The full-width inverse estimate is \hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.31}{}\label{mf:eq:B3.31}}

\[
r+2z\le m-c_1,\qquad2r+8z\le3m-c_2
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\sum_{0<q_u\ll Z^m}|M_u(Z^r)Q_u|^2\ll Z^{m+\epsilon}.
\qquad\text{(MF3.31)}
\]

\emph{The prime coefficients are arbitrary bounded row-independent coefficients, and no mesh is required.}

\emph{Proof.} Set \(P=\prod_i p_i\), \(j=(n,P)\) and fix its assigned slots. Write \(n=jn_0\), \(P=jP_0\), \(c=n_0P_0\); \(c\) is squarefree, \((c,j)=1\). The identities \(\mu(n_0)=\mu(c)\mu(P_0)\) and \(\varphi_u(n)\varphi_u(P)=\varphi_u(j)^2\varphi_u(c)\) retain zeros. For assigned length \(G\), put

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.32}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.32}

\[
z_0=z-G,\qquad D_0=r+z-2G,
\qquad Z^{-(r+z)/2}=Z^{-G}Z^{-D_0/2}.
\qquad\text{(MF3.32)}
\]

The \(O(Z^{G+o(1)})\) assigned tuples have triangle mass canceled by \(Z^{-G}\); \(\varphi_u(j)^2\) contracts. For fixed \(j\), the profile argument is \((q_j/Z^G)(q_c/Z^{D_0})/\prod_{i\,\mathrm{surviving}}(q_{p_i}/Z^{z_i})\). Separate it commonly before choosing survivors, absorb \(\mu(p_i)\) into coefficients, and conjugate the whole polynomial if needed. It remains to bound

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.33}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.33}

\[
R_0(u)=Z^{-D_0/2}\sum_{c\ {\rm sf}}\mu(c)\nu_0(c)
 \mathbf1_{(c,j)=1}\overline{\chi_c(u)}d_0(c)W_0(q_c/Z^{D_0}).
\qquad\text{(MF3.33)}
\]

The Fourier measure is uniform over the fixed \(q_j/Z^G\) interval.

Square in a smooth row ball, write \(c_i=Cz_i\), \((z_1,z_2)=1\), and apply \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.5}{(MF3.5)} with \(\overline{\chi_{z_1}}\chi_{z_2}\), \(d'\mid C\), frequency \(h'\). For centers \(B,\theta\) of \(C,d'\) put \(P_1=B-\theta\ge-2\eta\) and retain

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.34}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.34}

\[
M_c=2D_0-m-2P_1,\qquad M_{\rm init}=M_c+6\eta+\tau_{\rm init},
\qquad0<q_{d'h'}\le Z^{M_{\rm init}}.
\qquad\text{(MF3.34)}
\]

Conductor error is \(4\eta\) and two \(d'\) appearances add \(2\eta\), so the complement is an actual-ratio tail. Principal diagonal costs \(O(Z^{m+o(1)})\), including bounded nonempty negative-\(D_0\) windows.

As in the second canonical transform, Gauss conversion gives \(a_0(z)\nu_*(z)\overline{\chi_z(d')}\chi_z(h')\), with common \(\nu_*\) after expanding \(G([z_2z_1^{-1}])\). Insert full Möbius \(z_a=sn_a\), retaining \((C,j)=(s,Cj)=(s,h')=1\). Set

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.35}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.35}

\[
t=C/d',\qquad k=d'h',\qquad f=d's,\qquad
\rho_{t,j}(n)=\mathbf1_{(n,\operatorname{rad}(tj))=1}.
\qquad\text{(MF3.35)}
\]

The zero-preserving identity

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.36}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.36}

\[
a_0(sn)\nu_*(sn)\overline{\chi_{sn}(d')}\chi_{sn}(h')
=a_0(s)\nu_*(s)\overline{\chi_s(d')}\chi_s(h')
 a_0(n)\nu_*(n)\chi_n(k)\chi_n(f)^4
\qquad\text{(MF3.36)}
\]

holds for \((s,n)=1\) by \(\overline{\chi_n(d')}=\chi_n(d')^5\), including zeros. The squarefree \(f\) and priority allocation to \((t,d',s;n)\) preserve original slot subsets, with survival zeros supplied by \(\rho_{t,j}\) and \(\chi_n(f)^4\).

If \(v\) centers \(s\), the formal child parameters are

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.37}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.37}

\par\noindent\resizebox{\linewidth}{!}{\begin{minipage}{\dimexpr\linewidth+74.35098pt\relax}\[
N_c=D_0-B-v,\quad V_c=\theta+v,\quad F_c=D_0-P_1,
\quad N'=\max(0,N_c),\quad0\le\delta_N=N'-N_c\le3\eta,
\quad F'=F_c+\delta_N.
\qquad\text{(MF3.37)}
\]\end{minipage}}\par

For normalized \((C,d',s,h',n_1,n_2)\) use root \(y_d^{-1}y_s^{-1}(y_1y_2)^{-1/2}\), windows \(W_0(y_Cy_sy_1)\overline{W_0(y_Cy_sy_2)}\), kernel \(K_{\rm init}(A^{\rm ker}y_h/(y_dy_s^2y_1y_2))\), \(A^{\rm ker}=Z^{m+H-\theta-2(D_0-B)}\) (\(H\) the bare-frequency center). Multiplying the profile by \(Z^{-3\eta}\) leaves \(Z^{\varkappa_c+3\eta}\), \(\varkappa_c=m-2D_0+P_1\). Fresh tests are bounded clipped cutoffs times Fourier powers. Inversion restores the full kernel before Cauchy, retaining \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.34}{(MF3.34)}'s mask and all three zeros outside it.

At fixed \(t,f,k\), choose \(d'\mid f\); then \(s=f/d'\), \(C=td'\), \(h'=k/d'\). With at most \(K_0\) slots, multiplicity is \(\le d(f)^{1+2K_0}d(t)^{2K_0}\). Project distinct \(t\) once from the genuine outer set: \(\log_Zq_t\le P_1+2\eta\), giving weighted count \(Z^{P_1+2\eta+o(1)}\); extract it and normalize. The actual puncture has \(Q'\le P_1+G+3\eta\). Then

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.38}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.38}

\[
\begin{aligned}
F'-M_{\rm init}-Q'-z_0&\ge c_1-9\eta-\tau_{\rm init},\\
4F'-3M_{\rm init}-6z_0&\ge c_2-22\eta-3\tau_{\rm init},\\
\varkappa_c+P_1+2F_c&=m.
\end{aligned}\qquad\text{(MF3.38)}
\]

The formal differences are \(m-r-2z+2G\) and \(3m-2r-8z+10G+2P_1\). Choose \(\eta,\tau_{\rm init}\) small and apply canonical margin \(\min(c_1,c_2)/2\). The last identity and \(2\delta_N\le6\eta\) give exponent \(m+11\eta+\pi_{\rm init}+\epsilon_{\rm canonical}\). Allocate these within \(\epsilon\), then choose tails and backward seminorm orders as above. This proves \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.31}{(MF3.31)}, hence MI at \(m=1\). \(\square\)

\textbf{Unmarked consequence (UM).} With no slots, \(Z=H\), \(m=1\), \(r=\log_HD\), the condition \(H\ge D^{1+\vartheta}\) gives \(1-r\ge\vartheta/(1+\vartheta)\) and \(3-2r\ge1\); count bounded scales. Thus

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.39}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.39}

\textbf{(MF3.39)} is \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:3.2}{(95)}, with \(\varphi_u=\nu\chi_\bullet(u)^{\varepsilon_\chi}\), both signs and every element row, under \(H\ge D^{1+\vartheta}\).

All original zeros, finite-seminorm and polynomial-height control remain.

\hypertarget{the-enlarged-plain-moment-family}{}

\label{the-enlarged-plain-moment-family}

\hypertarget{the-enlarged-plain-moment-family-heading-2}{%
\subparagraph{7.2.4 The enlarged plain-moment family}}

Use \(\psi_k(n)=\tau(n)\chi_n(k)\) on \(0<q_k\ll Z^m\), where \(\tau\) combines a fixed ray character and displayed moving symbols. Their union \textbf{before} cancellations/exponent reduction has norm \(\le Z^q\); width is \(M=m+q\). A squarefree common mask \(q_R\le Z^B\) may depend on frozen labels but is fixed over the row sum and applies to every plain and prime factor. Canceled row-character primes retain natural zeros; they do not become freely chosen row masks.

For positive slots fix nonzero integral ray modulus \(\mathfrak M\), a subgroup containing global units, common annular profile \(W\), anchor \(\eta_0\), and ideal \(Q\subseteq\mathfrak M\). Require \(Q_0=Q\cap\mathfrak q_{\eta_0}\) to be nonzero, proper and contained in \((72)\). Each slot has \(\nu_i=\theta_i\overline{\eta_0}\) for a fixed-ray-quotient character \(\theta_i\), bounded real power \(\sigma_i\) and height \(v_i\):

\[
Q_{k,i}=Z^{-z_i/2}\sum_{p\in\mathcal P_i^{\rm can}}
 \psi_k(p)\nu_i(p)W(q_p/Z^{z_i})
 (q_p/Z^{z_i})^{-\sigma_i+iv_i}.
\]

Here \(\mathcal P_i^{\rm can}\) is the genuine canonical good-prime pool at \(Z^{z_i}\), retaining natural zeros and common puncture. MP uses this pool and common \(W\); it asserts neither arbitrary subsets, separate slot profiles nor a finite-character-expansion extension. MI permits arbitrary subsets. Physical scales are separated annuli.

Put \(z=\sum_i z_i\), \(A=n_1+n_2+z\). The two centrally normalized plains have independent profiles and heights with polynomial costs.

\textbf{Theorem 7.4} (Natural marked plain moment). \emph{\hypertarget{mf:plain}{}\label{mf:plain} The zero-slot assertion is the unconditional Theorem \protect\hyperlink{cf:mixed-plain-moment}{5.1}. For positive slots require \(0<z_i\le\eta_{\rm mesh}\), with mesh chosen below before the slot system. Absorb zero-width bounded factors into fixed arithmetic data. Assume}

\[
7/10\le\kappa\le1,\qquad \beta\ge51/100,\qquad
2\beta-1\le\kappa.
\]

\emph{The centrally normalized plain sums are}

\[
S_{k,i}(X)=X^{-1/2}\sum_{n}\psi_k(n)\mathbf1_{(n,R)=1}
 W_i(q_n/X),
\]

\emph{with all integral ideals \(n\) and the fixed bad-prime zeros understood. Then \hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.40}{}\label{mf:eq:B3.40}}

\[
\begin{array}{ll}
z=0:&\displaystyle
 \sum_{\psi_k^*\ne1}|S_k(Z^{n_1})S_k(Z^{n_2})|^2\ll Z^{M+\epsilon}
 \quad\text{for all bounded }n_1,n_2\ge0;\\[2mm]
z>0:&\displaystyle
 n_1+n_2+6\kappa z\le M
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 \sum_{k\ {\rm eligible}}|S_k(Z^{n_1})S_k(Z^{n_2})\prod_iQ_{k,i}|^2
 \ll Z^{M+\epsilon}.
\end{array}\qquad\text{(MF3.40)}
\]

\emph{A natural state has a whole-product presentation, complete natural zeros, fixed nonzero puncture and nonnegative smooth radial measure. Its width counts row-ball exponent plus displayed moving modulus before cancellation. Keep exactly nonzero rows not induced from \(\mathfrak Q_0=Q\cap\mathfrak q_{\eta_0}\): fixed-inducing means a primitive finite Hecke character of conductor dividing \(\mathfrak Q_0\) induces the actual row function with natural zeros. Deleting an extra puncture preserves this predicate, since additional zero Euler factors do not change the primitive inducing character. Initially,}

\par\noindent\resizebox{\linewidth}{!}{\begin{minipage}{\dimexpr\linewidth+7.89476pt\relax}\[
\operatorname{initialKeep}(\eta,\mathfrak Q_0)
=\{k\in\mathcal O:k\ne0,\ \text{the natural presentation of }n\mapsto\eta(n)\chi_n(k)
\text{ is not induced from }\mathfrak Q_0\}.
\]\end{minipage}}\par

\emph{The presentation retains fixed bad-prime zeros, primitive-conductor zeros, redundant moving zeros and row radical. Eligibility excludes every fixed-inducing presentation, not just principal characters or a chosen list.}

\emph{Quantifier order: for a requested loss and bounded width/puncture/length ranges choose one mesh, uniformly for \(\kappa\in[7/10,1]\), before arithmetic family, slot count, profiles or target height. After fixing a finite slot index type and common \(W\), choose finite seminorms and height order before fixed character/ideal; their constant and eventual threshold precede live subset, slot characters, lengths, powers, heights, radial state and plain profiles. Positive slots allow a common whole-product norm power; independent plain twists are controlled by seminorms. Zero slots retain independent heights, all bounded lengths and every nonprincipal inducing character, including fixed-conductor rows.}

\emph{Absorb fixed conductors in arbitrarily small width padding charged to the requested loss before the eventual threshold. Moving support remains width, never a profile seminorm.}

\emph{The physical initial state has scale \(U\), puncture one, fixed source \(\eta\) and width \(1+\delta\) once \(q_{\mathfrak q_\eta}\le U^\delta\). Its measure is \(1_{\operatorname{initialKeep}(\eta,\mathfrak Q_0)}(k)\Phi(q_k/U)\), \(\Re\Phi\ge0\), with the literal natural plains and slots above, and relative character}

\[
\nu_i(n)=\theta_i(n)\overline{\eta_0(n)},
\]

\emph{Lift \(\theta_i\) from \((\mathcal O/\mathfrak M)^\times/H\); both factors retain their zeros. The prime pool is the identity ray class, cut at \(\max(1,b)U^{z_i}\); \(W\) selects its exact annulus. Physically \(W=\overline{F.W}\), \(\theta_i=1\), real power \(33/50\), height \(-\Im z_i\). Fixed-inducing sixth-power-free rows have prescribed good-prime valuations modulo six, hence bounded norm and leave a growing lower annulus.}

\emph{The zero-slot endpoint restores fixed nonprincipal characters by masked lattice cancellation below. Width counts full moving support but excludes the puncture. For exceptional children fix a finite conjugation-stable \(S\)-supported group \(\Theta\) containing fixed phases, inverses and \(n\mapsto\chi_n(-1)\); it does not replace positive-slot eligibility. Taking \(m=1,q=0,n_1=n_2\) gives MP in the positive family and MP0 for all nonprincipal zero-slot rows.}

\emph{Proof.} \textbf{Extra masks.} A natural presentation has only primitive, redundant displayed, row and \(S\) zeros. Delete its additional fixed \(R\) mask to obtain \(\psi_k^0\). Multiplicativity gives

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.41}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.41}

\par\noindent\resizebox{\linewidth}{!}{\begin{minipage}{\dimexpr\linewidth+61.43738pt\relax}\[
S_{k,R}(X)=\sum_{d\mid R}\frac{\mu(d)\psi_k^0(d)}{\sqrt{q_d}}
 S_k^0(X/q_d),\qquad
Q_{k,i,R}=Q_{k,i}^0-Z^{-z_i/2}\sum_{p\mid R}\psi_k^0(p)\nu_i(p)W(q_p/Z^{z_i})(q_p/Z^{z_i})^{-\sigma_i+iv_i}.
\qquad\text{(MF3.41)}
\]\end{minipage}}\par

Total coefficient mass is \(Z^{o(1)}\): \(\prod_{p\mid R}(1-q_p^{-1/2})^{-1}\ll_\epsilon Z^\epsilon\), and a frozen slot has \(Z^{-z_i/2}\ll q_p^{-1/2}\). Apply both identities and Minkowski. The affine length \(A+(6\kappa-1)z\) decreases; if slots vanish the zero-slot row set contains the original eligible rows. Nonempty subunit plains clip by bounded dilation without exceeding old nonnegative lengths. Prove natural states uniformly, retaining this deletion at every child.

\textbf{Whole-factor reflection.} Use §\protect\hyperlink{cf:sec:D.3}{5.3}'s natural reflection, with primitive \(\psi_k^*\), conductor norm \(Q_k\), \(C_k=3Q_k/(2\pi)^2\) and redundant radical \(R_{0,k}\).

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.42}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.42}

\textbf{(MF3.42)} is \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:D.6}{(70)}, \(C_kq_{R_{0,k}}\ll Z^M\), with the same full-support width.

Unit and \(S\)-valuations modulo six give finite ray sectors. For nonprincipal inducing characters, the central primitive reflection has scale \(C_k/X\), unit root number and profile

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.43}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.43}

\textbf{(MF3.43)} is \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:D.7}{(71)}'s Mellin profile formula, \(\widehat{W^\sharp}(s)=\widehat W(1-s)\Gamma(s)/\Gamma(1-s)\).

The proof in §\protect\hyperlink{cf:sec:D.3}{5.3} substitutes the functional equation, sets \(s\mapsto1-s\) and shifts without an \(L\)-pole. It gives bounded Euler derivatives at zero, rapid decay at infinity, and finite-seminorm/polynomial-height control by Stirling and \(\widehat{Wy^{it}}(s)=\widehat W(s+it)\).

Deleting redundant factors before reflection and restoring geometrically gives exactly

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.44}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.44}

\[
S_k^0(X;W)=\varepsilon_k
 \sum_{d_0\mid R_{0,k}}\sum_{\operatorname{rad}(h_0)\mid R_{0,k}}
 \frac{\mu(d_0)\psi_k^*(d_0)\overline{\psi_k^*(h_0)}}{\sqrt{q_{d_0}q_{h_0}}}
 S_{\overline{\psi_k^0}}\!\left(\frac{C_kq_{d_0}}{Xq_{h_0}};W^\sharp\right).
\qquad\text{(MF3.44)}
\]

Its mass is \(Z^{o(1)}\). Lower annuli of \(W^\sharp\) have central coefficients \(O(2^{-j/2})\); upper annuli decay arbitrarily rapidly. Discard above relative scale \(Z^{\xi/2}\). A main scale \(Y<Z^{-\xi}\) contributes \(O_B(Y^{B-1/2})\) by counting and decay, for arbitrary fixed \(B\). Handle row-dependent coefficients by mass, then scale suprema: integrate function and log-scale derivative over \(O(\log Z)\) unit boxes, in two variables for two scales. The derivative is \(-W/2-yW'\), so common-profile bounds apply. Conjugate the entire reflected factor to restore orientation. By \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.42}{(MF3.42)}, retained scales satisfy

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.45}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.45}

\[
n_{\rm ref}\le M-n+\xi.
\qquad\text{(MF3.45)}
\]

Absorb fixed endpoints and boxes in the final threshold; negative right side gives an empty range. Reflecting plains longer than \(M/2\) reduces zero slots to

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.46}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.46}

\textbf{(MF3.46)} is the padded core \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:D.8}{(72)}, with \(2\xi\le\delta\): \(n_i\le M/2+\xi\) and \(A\le M+2\xi\le M+\delta\).

\textbf{Prime cost.} On eligible rows each slot remains nonprincipal after expanding the canonical identity-ray projector as the average of all fixed-quotient characters. Its product with \(\nu_i=\theta_i\overline{\eta_0}\) has conductor dividing \(Q_0\); a principal resulting row would therefore be induced from \(Q_0\). Multiplication by such a fixed character preserves eligibility by inverse multiplication. The finite average changes only constants; no arbitrary-pool/profile extension follows. Under \(2\beta-1\le\kappa\), move the smooth von Mangoldt contour to \(s_\kappa+e\), \(s_\kappa=(1+\kappa)/2\). There \(|L'/L|\ll_e\log(Q(3+|t|)^2)\): on the zero-free disk centered at \(2+it\) of radius \(2-s_\kappa-e/4\), the Euler branch \(g=\log L\) has \(g(2+it)=O(1)\) and \(\Re g\ll\log(Q(3+|t|)^2)\) by functional-equation strip growth. Borel--Carathéodory and then Cauchy's derivative bound on successively smaller disks containing \(s_\kappa+e+it\) prove the claim; all gaps are fixed multiples of \(e\). Prime powers of degree \(\ge2\) cost \(O(P^{1/2+\epsilon})\). Divide the annular weight by \(\log(Py)\); redundant zeros delete only \(O(\log Z)\) primes. Central normalization over the fixed slots gives

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.47}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.47}

\[
\left|\prod_iQ_{k,i}\right|^2
 \ll Z^{\kappa z+\epsilon_1}\,p_J(W)^b\langle\mathbf t\rangle^h.
\qquad\text{(MF3.47)}
\]

Contour displacement costs \(2ez\), depending on total length, not slot count. Heights translate rapidly decreasing Mellin transforms; required derivative/height orders do not affect the exponent.

\hypertarget{induction-order-and-the-centered-comparison}{}

\label{induction-order-and-the-centered-comparison}

\hypertarget{induction-order-and-the-centered-comparison-heading-2}{%
\subparagraph{7.2.5 Induction order and the centered comparison}}

Choose floor \(\rho>0\), step \(\sigma>0\), padding \(\delta\ll\min(\rho,\sigma)\) and bands of length \(\sigma/4\). Use the zero-slot theorem first, then prove positive bands increasingly; within each band prove \(A\le5M/6\) before centering. The zero-slot proof in §5 supplies all lengths by reflection and padded-floor excess \(\le\rho+\delta\). At \(M\le\rho\), positive slots follow from it and \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.47}{(MF3.47)}, since

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.48}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.48}

\[
z\le M/(6\kappa),\qquad\kappa z\le M/6.
\qquad\text{(MF3.48)}
\]

Its additional terminal excess is at most \(\rho/6\).

For \(A>5M/6\), put \(L=M/4\). If one plain is shorter than \(L\), reflect the other; otherwise compare lengths \(L,A-z-L\) with the same profiles, reflecting the larger comparison factor only to estimate the comparison. Since \(q_\kappa=6\kappa-1>2\) and \(z<(M/6)/q_\kappa<M/12\), both comparison lengths are \(\ge L\). Reflection gives

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.49}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.49}

\par\noindent\resizebox{\linewidth}{!}{\begin{minipage}{\dimexpr\linewidth+61.41724pt\relax}\[
A_{\rm comp}\le3M/2-A+2z+\xi,
\quad
\min\{5M/6-A_{\rm comp},\,M-A_{\rm comp}-q_\kappa z\}
\ge \mathfrak m(7/10)M-\xi,
\quad
\mathfrak m(\kappa)=\frac{2\kappa-1}{2(6\kappa-1)}.
\qquad\text{(MF3.49)}
\]\end{minipage}}\par

Indeed \(t=M-A<M/6\), \(z\le t/q_\kappa\) give margins \(\ge M/3-t(1+2/q_\kappa)-\xi\) and \(M/2-t(2+2/q_\kappa)-\xi\). Zero-slot comparison margin is \(\ge M/6-\xi\). Choose \(\xi<\rho\mathfrak m(7/10)/2\); each comparison then uses its already proved uncentered stage.

For both plains \(\ge L\), put \(X_i=Z^{n_i}\), \(Y_1=Z^L\), \(Y_2=X_1X_2/Y_1\). Subtract and retain the equal-product rectangles; after extracting \(b_i\), the coefficient is

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.50}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.50}

\textbf{(MF3.50)} is the exact rectangle difference \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:D.9}{(73)}, with \(D_b=\mathcal D_b\) and \(X_1X_2=Y_1Y_2\).

Use the same \(b_i\) even when one rectangle vanishes. The comparison is already bounded on eligible rows; enlarge the positive norm of the \textbf{whole difference} to a smooth ball of all rows. In the uncentered range enlarge the original squared norm. Never Poisson-sum an eligibility indicator.

The invariant coefficient carried through the transformations is

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.51}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.51}

\[
\prod_{i\in I'}\nu_i(p_i)W(q_{p_i}/Z^{z_i})(q_{p_i}/Z^{z_i})^{-\sigma_i+iv_i}\,
D_b(l_1,l_2)\,\tau_1(u)q_u^{it}\,\mathbf1_{(u,R)=1}\mathbf1_{s\mid u},
\qquad u=\Bigl(\prod_{i\in I'}p_i\Bigr)l_1l_2,
\qquad\text{(MF3.51)}
\]

Here \(s\) is fixed squarefree; uncentered inputs omit the second rectangle. The character, mask and norm power act on the entire product, preserving collisions by zero extension.

Use one aggregate tolerance. For fixed slot count \(N\), let \(H_N\) bound sums of absolute logarithmic endpoints of every slot/plain window and all finite later enlargements; set \(\theta_N=H_N/\log Z\). Every slot subset's total ratio error is \(\le\theta_N\). Extract plain powers using exact norms. Stage errors are \(O(\theta_N)\) with numerical coefficient independent of \(N\), not a loss per slot.

\hypertarget{first-poisson-from-plain-products-to-gauss-polynomials}{}

\label{first-poisson-from-plain-products-to-gauss-polynomials}

\hypertarget{first-poisson-from-plain-products-to-gauss-polynomials-heading-2}{%
\subparagraph{7.2.6 First Poisson: from plain products to Gauss polynomials}}

Set \(H=Z^m\), \(X=Z^A\). The zero-frequency argument of §\protect\hyperlink{cf:sec:D.4}{5.4} applies unchanged to full products: zero net sextic exponents exclude multiplicity-one primes, giving a powerful ideal of norm \(O(X^2)\), \(O(X^{1+\epsilon})\) choices and divisor-bounded slot/plain allocations. Multiplying central \(X^{-1}\) and row mean \(\le H\) gives \(O(Z^{m+o(1)})\).

For nonzero frequency write full products \(Ca,Db\) with complete common support, \((a,b)=(ab,CD)=1\). Fix each common prime's full plain valuations and slot occurrences, extract them, freeze supplying slots and puncture every residual factor. Apply the same allocation to both rectangles; retain any scalar zero before enlargement.

Let \(c,d,p\) be lengths of \(C,D,\operatorname{rad}(CD)\). Let \(r\) contain nonzero net exponents modulo six, length \(R\), primitive character \(\xi_r\); remaining common-mask divisor \(e\) has \(E\le p-R\). For allocated coefficients without the whole-product twist, set \(\tau_C(a)=\tau(a)\chi_a(er)\overline{\xi_r(a)}\) and similarly for \(\tau_D\). Poisson/CRT give, up to bounded frozen scalar,

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.52}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.52}

\[
\frac{H}{Xq_e\sqrt{q_r}}\sum_{h\ne0}\mathcal G_{\xi_r}(r,h)
\sum_{(a,b)=1}\frac{A_C(a)\overline{A_D(b)}}{\sqrt{q_aq_b}}
 \tau_C(a)\overline{\tau_D(b)}R(a,b)
 \mathcal G(a,h)\overline{\mathcal G(b,-h)}
 K_1\!\left(\frac{Hq_h}{q_eq_rq_aq_b}\right).
\qquad\text{(MF3.52)}
\]

Here \(\mathcal G\) is the normalized sum with local values \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:A.12}{(8)}, \(|\mathcal G_{\xi_r}|\le1\). As in §\protect\hyperlink{cf:sec:D.4}{5.4}, Poisson after \(k=ek'\) contributes \(H/(q_eq_rq_aq_b)\); Gauss sums restore \(\sqrt{q_rq_aq_b}\). CRT gives \(\chi_a(br)\overline{\chi_b(ar)}\overline{\xi_r(ab)}\), whose \(a,b\) ratio is \(R(a,b)\) by reciprocity. New moving primes puncture every residual factor even if exponents cancel; their support and frequency lengths are

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.53}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.53}

\textbf{(MF3.53)} combines \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:D.11}{(75)} and \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:D.12}{(76)}: \(\widetilde q=q+R+E\), \(K_0=2A-c-d+R+E-m\), with this chapter's full-product length \(A=n_1+n_2+z\).

The first kernel's nominal frequency scale is \(Z^{K_0}\).

For both Poisson transforms use one nonnegative norm-dyadic partition. In an outer sector retain whole weights meeting \(q_h\le T_{\rm sec}Z^{\xi/2}\), \(T_{\rm sec}\) the maximum nominal scale. For large \(Z\), upper length is \(K\le K_0+\delta_1\), \(0\le\delta_1<\xi\); discarded genuine arguments are \(\ge Z^{\xi/4}\), since full-product support costs \(e^{2H_N}\). Apply \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.4}{(MF3.4)} with raw bounds \(|\mathcal G|\le\sqrt{q_a}\), \(|F|\le q_uq_v\) and polynomial column/count cost, fixing that cost before decay order. Retained weights/full kernels are common to rectangles and independent of live columns.

On each dyad extend the separated Gauss bridge off coprimality using formal norms, original masks and bicharacter. Insert the complete \(\sum_{s\mid(a,b)}\mu(s)\) before absolute values; let \(s_0=\log_Zq_s\). Separate kernel/inverse roots in row and whole-product norms, adding an aggregate outer-ratio coordinate if needed. Each column receives one common norm power across its plains and rectangles. Then Cauchy and \(|\mathcal G_{\xi_r}|\le1\) give

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.54}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.54}

\textbf{(MF3.54)} is \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:D.13}{(77)} with \(\mathfrak g=\mathcal G\), this chapter's \(A_C,\tau_C'\) and dyadic \(h\ne0\) support. Its complete coefficient is \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.51}{(MF3.51)}.

The exchanged \(D\) norm has the same form. Outside exponent is \(m-A-R/2-E\); common-support and Möbius counts are \(Z^{p+s_0+o(1)}\). Count radicals first, then bounded powers via \(\#\{v:\operatorname{rad}v\mid c,q_v\le Z^T\}\le Z^{aT}\prod_{p\mid c}(1-q_p^{-a})^{-1}\) for small \(a>0\); allocations cost divisors.

It suffices to prove

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.55}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.55}

\textbf{(MF3.55)} is the target bound \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:D.14}{(78)}, here with loss \(\epsilon_G\): \(\mathcal N_C\ll Z^{A-c+\widetilde q+B_c-s_0+\epsilon_G}\), \(B_c=(3c-5d-R)_+/6\).

Its exchanged counterpart and outside factors have exponent \(M+\{B_c+B_d-(c+d-2p-R)\}/2\). The local comparison is

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.56}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.56}

\[
B_c+B_d\le c+d-2p-R.
\qquad\text{(MF3.56)}
\]

This is the primewise inequality proved after \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:D.15}{(79)}, with identical multiplicities and \(r_p=1_{6\nmid i-j}\); slot occurrences are already included in those multiplicities.

\hypertarget{enlarging-a-gauss-row-norm}{}

\label{enlarging-a-gauss-row-norm}

\hypertarget{enlarging-a-gauss-row-norm-heading-2}{%
\subparagraph{7.2.7 Enlarging a Gauss-row norm}}

Extract length \(w\) from \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.54}{(MF3.54)}, adding moving radical \(w_o\le w\) with squared scalar \(Z^{-w_o}\). Put \(a_0=A-c-w\); \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.55}{(MF3.55)} permits the unweighted norm exponent

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.57}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.57}

\[
\Lambda_c=a_0+\widetilde q+w_o+w+B_c-s_0+\epsilon_G.
\qquad\text{(MF3.57)}
\]

Define \(J=d-c+(K_0-K)-2w+w_o\), \(J_+=\max(J,0)\). With zero slots take \(w=w_o=0\), \(\ell=0\), and enlarge the row norm to a smooth ball of length \(K+g\), \(g=J_++\sigma\).

For positive slots choose \(\ell_* =\sigma/3\), mesh \(<\sigma/6\), and pool \(\mathcal P=\{p:Z^{\ell_*}/2<q_p\le Z^{\ell_*},p\notin S\}\). The fixed-field prime ideal theorem gives \(|\mathcal P|=Z^{\ell_*+o(1)}\). For large \(Z\) it avoids all live slots. For each \(h\ne0\), delete the \(O(\log Z)\) primes dividing \(h,s\) or frozen supports.

For \(p\nmid uh\), the prime-power Gauss identities \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:A.12}{(8)} and CRT give

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.58}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.58}

\[
\mathcal G(p^iu,h)=R(p,u)^i\chi_p(u)^{2i}\mathcal G(p^i,h)\mathcal G(u,h),
\qquad\mathcal G(u,hp^6)=\mathcal G(u,h).
\qquad\text{(MF3.58)}
\]

Replacing \(h\) by \(hp^6\) changes only valuations \(i=1,6,7\). Assign powers to the two plains (pool and slots are disjoint), puncturing the residual product and updating both rectangles. Jensen over eligible primes and these finite allocations bounds the original square by amplified square and extracted errors. Each output \(hp^6\) has boundedly many representations, since pool length is fixed positive and output length bounded; density is \(Z^{-\ell_*+o(1)}\). Take \(\ell=\ell_*\), \(g=J_++2\sigma\), enclosing amplified rows since \(6\ell_*=2\sigma\).

The extracted cases are exactly:

\begingroup\small
\begin{longtable}{@{}p{\linewidth}@{}}
\toprule
\endhead
\bottomrule
\endlastfoot
\textit{Column valuation \(i\)}: 1; \enspace \textit{Squared extraction coefficient}: \(q_p^{-1}\); \enspace \textit{\(w\)}: \(\ell_p\); \enspace \textit{\(w_o\)}: \(\ell_p\) \\
\addlinespace[3pt]
\textit{Column valuation \(i\)}: 6; \enspace \textit{Squared extraction coefficient}: \((1-q_p^{-1})^2\); \enspace \textit{\(w\)}: \(6\ell_p\); \enspace \textit{\(w_o\)}: 0 \\
\addlinespace[3pt]
\textit{Column valuation \(i\)}: 7; \enspace \textit{Squared extraction coefficient}: \(q_p^{-1}\); \enspace \textit{\(w\)}: \(7\ell_p\); \enspace \textit{\(w_o\)}: \(\ell_p\) \\
\end{longtable}
\endgroup

Here \(\ell_p=\log_Zq_p\in[\sigma/6,\sigma/3]\). At valuation six, \(\chi_p(u)^{12}\) is the existing common puncture; retain \(R(p,u)^6\). Valuations one/seven retain a moving character counted in \(w_o\). Freeze each error's \(p\), take \(\ell=0\), \(g=J_++\sigma\), and transform without re-amplifying. The normalized prime average has no extra count. Always

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.59}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.59}

\[
0\le w_o\le w\le7\sigma/3.
\qquad\text{(MF3.59)}
\]

Coefficient \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.51}{(MF3.51)}, multiplied by \(\mathcal G(u,h)\), retains its difference, puncture and \(s\)-divisibility.

Add zero only at the final smooth ball, never amplify it. By \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:A.12}{(8)}, \(\mathcal G(u,0)\) vanishes unless \(u\) is sixth-power, and is \(\le\sqrt{q_u}\). For nonempty upper bound \(Y=e^{H_N}Z^{a_0}\) and \(s\mid u\), \(q_s^6\le Y\). Counting \(O(Y^{1/6})\) columns gives normalized square \(Z^{-a_0}Y^{4/3+o(1)}\le Z^{a_0-s_0+2\theta_N+o(1)}\), within \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.57}{(MF3.57)}.

\hypertarget{second-transform-and-the-actual-children}{}

\label{second-transform-and-the-actual-children}

\hypertarget{second-transform-and-the-actual-children-heading-2}{%
\subparagraph{7.2.8 Second transform and the actual children}}

Restore live slot sums before squaring. If \(B(u)\) is the complete convolution coefficient, with \(s\mid u\), \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:A.14}{(10)} gives

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.60}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.60}

\[
\begin{aligned}
&Z^{-\ell}\sum_h\Phi_2(q_h/Z^{K+g})
 \left|Z^{-a_0/2}\sum_u B(u)\tau_1(u)q_u^{it}\mathcal G(u,h)\right|^2
\\
&\quad=Z^{K+g-\ell-a_0}\sum_{u,v}
 \frac{B(u)\overline{B(v)}\tau_1(u)\overline{\tau_1(v)}q_u^{it}q_v^{-it}}
      {\sqrt{q_uq_v}}
\\
&\qquad{}\times\sum_jF(u,v;j)K_2\!\left(\frac{Z^{K+g}q_j}{q_uq_v}\right).
\end{aligned}
\qquad\text{(MF3.60)}
\]

At \(j=0\), \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:A.15}{(11)} forces \(u=v\) and \(F(u,u;0)=\phi(u)\le q_u\). There are \(O(Z^{a_0-s_0+\theta_N})\) supported multiples of \(s\), giving exponent \(K+g-\ell-s_0+\theta_N+o(1)\) and nominal excess

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.61}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.61}

\[
(A-M)-d-(K_0-K)-w_o-B_c+g-\ell
\le A-M+5\sigma/3+\delta_1.
\qquad\text{(MF3.61)}
\]

If \(J\ge0\), \(-d-(K_0-K)-w_o+J_+=-c-2w\); otherwise it is \(\le\delta_1\). The largest increment is \(2\sigma-\ell_*=5\sigma/3\), a terminal loss, plus \(\delta\) in the zero-slot padded core.

Localize genuine nonzero \(j\) at nominal \(Z^{2a_0-K-g}\) by the whole-dyad rule before common extraction \(u=D_2a,v=E_2b\). Put

\par\noindent\resizebox{\linewidth}{!}{\begin{minipage}{\dimexpr\linewidth+13.74536pt\relax}\[
c_2=\log_Zq_{D_2},\quad d_2=\log_Zq_{E_2},\quad b_2=(c_2+d_2)/2,
\quad p_2=\log_Zq_{\operatorname{rad}(D_2E_2)},\quad G_c=(D_2,E_2),\quad g_2=\log_Zq_{G_c}.
\]\end{minipage}}\par

Apply the complete-support factorization

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.62}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.62}

\textbf{(MF3.62)} is \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:A.16}{(12)}, with \((D,E)=(D_2,E_2)\) and the same residual \(a,b,j\).

It holds only for \((a,b)=(ab,D_2E_2)=1\), allowing arbitrary powers and slot/plain coincidences. Extracted primes puncture residual factors; freeze supplying slots. A plain \(p^5\) and slot \(p\) therefore retain \(\chi_p(j)^6\), not one.

Equations \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:A.17}{(13)}--\protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:A.18}{(14)} force \(G_c\mid j\). At equal non-six-divisible multiplicities split divided frequency into units/nonunits, radical lengths \(t_2,V\) and nonunit radical \(V_{\rm id}\). Set \(j=G_cV_{\rm id}h'\). Extend \(1_{\rm part}F(D_2,E_2;G_cV_{\rm id}h')/Z^{g_2-t_2}\) by zero off its partition; it has modulus \(\le1\) by

\begingroup\small
\begin{longtable}{@{}p{\linewidth}@{}}
\toprule
\endhead
\bottomrule
\endlastfoot
\textit{Common multiplicities and divided frequency}: \(i=i,\ 6\nmid i\), unit; \enspace \textit{Bound}: \(P^{i-1}\) \\
\addlinespace[3pt]
\textit{Common multiplicities and divided frequency}: \(i=i,\ 6\nmid i\), nonunit; \enspace \textit{Bound}: \(P^i\) \\
\addlinespace[3pt]
\textit{Common multiplicities and divided frequency}: \(i=i,\ 6\mid i\), either; \enspace \textit{Bound}: \(P^i\) \\
\addlinespace[3pt]
\textit{Common multiplicities and divided frequency}: \(i>j_0,\ 6\mid j_0\), unit; \enspace \textit{Bound}: \(P^{j_0}\) \\
\end{longtable}
\endgroup

Other unequal cases vanish. In \(v=G_cV_{\rm id}\), reduce valuations modulo six: nonzero residues retain moving characters, zeros the existing puncture. Active primes lie in \(t_2+V\). Factor only \(\chi_n(v)\), preserving varying-row zeros of \(\chi_n(h')\). Thus

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.63}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.63}

\[
m'=2a_0-K-g-g_2-V,\quad q'=\widetilde q+w_o+t_2+V,
\qquad M'=m'+q'=M+J-g-g_2+t_2\le M-\sigma.
\qquad\text{(MF3.63)}
\]

The pulled-back retained weight has \(q_{h'}\le Z^{m'+\delta_2}\), \(0\le\delta_2<\xi\). Nonempty ranges have \(m'+\delta_2\ge0\); declare \(m'_{\rm act}=m'+\delta_2\), \(M'_{\rm act}=M'+\delta_2\). Extend weight and partition scalar by zero across this ball, keeping them signed.

The condition \(s\mid u,v\) forces \(s\) into the common radical, so labels cost \(Z^{p_2-s_0+o(1)}\). The ledger is

\begingroup\small
\begin{longtable}{@{}p{\linewidth}@{}}
\toprule
\endhead
\bottomrule
\endlastfoot
\textit{Factor}: Original squared central normalization; \enspace \textit{Exponent}: \(-a_0\) \\
\addlinespace[3pt]
\textit{Factor}: Poisson factor and inverse roots; \enspace \textit{Exponent}: \(K+g-a_0\) \\
\addlinespace[3pt]
\textit{Factor}: Amplifier density; \enspace \textit{Exponent}: \(-\ell\) \\
\addlinespace[3pt]
\textit{Factor}: Common-support count with fixed \(s\); \enspace \textit{Exponent}: \(p_2-s_0\) \\
\addlinespace[3pt]
\textit{Factor}: Common correlation; \enspace \textit{Exponent}: \(g_2-t_2\) \\
\addlinespace[3pt]
\textit{Factor}: Conversion to normalized residual products; \enspace \textit{Exponent}: \(a_0-b_2\) \\
\end{longtable}
\endgroup

Subtracting this sum from \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.57}{(MF3.57)} leaves the inner allowance

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.64}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.64}

\[
M'+\Delta,\qquad \Delta=b_2-p_2+w+B_c+\ell\ge w+\ell.
\qquad\text{(MF3.64)}
\]

The last inequality uses \(b_2\ge p_2\).

Extend \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.62}{(MF3.62)}'s right side to individually allowed pairs, retaining original common-support masks, formal full norms, roots, kernel and pulled-back row weight. Insert full \(\sum_{t\mid(a,b)}\mu(t)\); only this sum restores the genuine correlation. Its artificial common primes do not enter \(D_2,E_2\) or frequency restrictions. Separate in row/whole-product norms, then allocate primes of \(t\) by

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.65}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.65}

\[
\mathbf1_{p\mid\prod_i n_i}
=\sum_{\varnothing\ne J}(-1)^{|J|+1}\prod_{i\in J}\mathbf1_{p\mid n_i},
\qquad\text{(MF3.65)}
\]

Factors are the two plains and live slots. Selected plains become \(n_i=pl_i\) without residual puncture, dividing corresponding scales in both rectangles by \(q_p\). Selected slots freeze; selecting distinct primes in one prime slot vanishes. Quotients may retain \(p\).

Thus each child retains a whole-product character, common mask and norm power across plains/rectangles; live slots keep original \(\nu_i\) without Gauss factors. Sides may have different whole-product norm powers, but their fixed-inducing predicates agree: conjugation and multiplication by fixed-modulus characters preserve conductor divisibility, including unchanged redundant zeros. At each fixed-modulus stage enlarge \(\Theta\) to all primitive characters dividing that modulus plus the finitely many fixed ray twists; it remains finite. On an exceptional row inducing \(\vartheta\), freeze live slots to obtain

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.66}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.66}

\textbf{(MF3.66)} is the exceptional rectangle coefficient \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:D.27}{(91)}, with \(D_b=\mathcal D_b\), this child's \(\vartheta,t\), and its complete squarefree mask \(R_*\).

The polynomial-size \(R_*\) is common to both variables/rectangles. Retain extracted row scalars, including zeros, until positive or absolute estimation.

\textbf{Extraction normalization.} Put \(\alpha_1=a_0-c_2\), \(\alpha_2=a_0-d_2\). Let \(F_j\) be genuinely frozen entrance slots and \(I_j\) the live slots before final allocation. Their pre-allocation plain product \(T_j\) satisfies \(T_j\prod_{i\in I_j}Z^{z_i}=Z^{\alpha_j+e_j}\), \(e_j=\sum_{i\in F_j}\log_Z(q_{p_i}/Z^{z_i})\). Let \(d_{j,i}\) be final products selected in plain \(i\), \(J_j\subset I_j\) newly frozen slots, and put

\[
a_{j,i}=\log_Zq_{d_{j,i}},\quad
r_j=a_{j,1}+a_{j,2}+\sum_{i\in J_j}z_i,\quad
\omega_j=\sum_{i\in J_j}\log_Z(q_{p_i}/Z^{z_i}).
\]

Use lower-endpoint dyads \(Z^{t_-}\le q_t<2Z^{t_-}\). Every selected product is divisible by \(t\), so

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.67}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.67}

\[
r_j+\omega_j\ge t_-,\qquad |\omega_j|\le\theta_N,
\qquad |e_j+\omega_j|\le\theta_N.
\qquad\text{(MF3.67)}
\]

The last bound uses disjoint subsets of current entrance slots, without recounting ancestors. Raw child normalizer is \(Z^{-(\alpha_j-r_j)/2}\), extraction scalar \(Z^{-r_j/2}\), formal scale product \(Z^{\alpha_j+e_j-r_j}\). Signed raw rectangles may have negative individual lengths.

\textbf{Child eligibility.} Split by the same fixed-inducing predicate with complete source masks. On kept rows, \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.62}{(MF3.62)}--\protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.65}{(MF3.65)} give exactly the whole-product character in nonzero \(h'\) with pulled-back radial weight. Frozen zeros belong to its natural presentation; the extra common mask is row-fixed. Delete it by \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.41}{(MF3.41)}, obtaining a natural state of width \(M'_{\rm act}\) with precisely the non-fixed-inducing keep set. Each plain and surviving slot has this same character/mask, and Fourier density precedes live labels/rows. Hence the smaller-width theorem applies to the actual positive norm; complementary fixed-inducing rows remain for separate evaluation.

\textbf{Nonexceptional children.} Use Cauchy, then split centered children into rectangles; only now bound common weights/scalars by one and enlarge the ball. For pre-allocation plain lengths \(x_i\) and post-lengths \(y_i=x_i-a_{j,i}\), put \(\pi_0=\sum(-x_i)_+\), \(\pi=\sum(-y_i)_+\). Omit rectangles whose plain upper support is below the first ideal; otherwise \(0\le\pi_0\le\pi\le\theta_N\). The clipped reduction and total are

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.68}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.68}

\[
r_{{\rm clip},j}=r_j-(\pi-\pi_0)\ge0,\qquad
A_{{\rm clip},j}=\alpha_j+e_j-r_j+\pi,
\qquad\text{(MF3.68)}
\]

and its exact scalar relative to the pre-normalizer is \(Z^{(-r_{{\rm clip},j}+e_j+\pi_0)/2}\). For a paired divisor dyad, the count and both scalars satisfy

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.69}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.69}

\[
t_--\frac{r_{{\rm clip},1}+r_{{\rm clip},2}}2
 +\frac{e_1+\pi_{0,1}+e_2+\pi_{0,2}}2\le2\theta_N.
\qquad\text{(MF3.69)}
\]

Substitute \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.68}{(MF3.68)} and \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.67}{(MF3.67)}; there are at most four rectangle pairs. Do not assign a common clipped reduction to a signed difference.

With no surviving slot, \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.69}{(MF3.69)} and the smaller-width zero-slot bound fit \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.64}{(MF3.64)}, \(\Delta\ge0\). Otherwise side one's pre-allocation total is \(A'=A-c-w-c_2\), and

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.70}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.70}

\[
(A'-M')-(A-M)
=g+w-d-c_2-(K_0-K)-w_o+g_2-t_2
\le6(w+\ell)+\delta_1.
\qquad\text{(MF3.70)}
\]

Use \(g_2-t_2\le c_2\). In the amplified norm, \(g\le d+(K_0-K)+6\ell+\delta_1\); in errors, \(g\le d+(K_0-K)+\sigma+\delta_1\) and the extraction table gives \(w-w_o+\sigma\le6w\). Exchange \(c_2,d_2\) for the other side.

Delete extra masks. For each actual natural child with live slots put \(F_{\rm act}=(A_{\rm act}-M'_{\rm act}+(6\kappa-1)z_{\rm act})_+\). Equations \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.68}{(MF3.68)}--\protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.70}{(MF3.70)}, parent capacity and aggregate endpoint bound give

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.71}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.71}

\[
F_{\rm act}\le6(w+\ell)+\delta_1+2\theta_N.
\qquad\text{(MF3.71)}
\]

Use \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.47}{(MF3.47)} to remove whole slots until defect vanishes or no slot remains. A length-\(d\) slot releases \(6\kappa d\) capacity and costs \(\kappa d\) energy. A greedy prefix overshoots by at most one mesh, so

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.72}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.72}

\[
\kappa d_z\le F_{\rm act}/6+\kappa\eta_{\rm mesh}
\le\Delta+\delta_1/6+\theta_N/3+\eta_{\rm mesh}.
\qquad\text{(MF3.72)}
\]

If slots remain use the smaller-width positive theorem; otherwise use unrestricted zero-slot bounds. Equations \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.69}{(MF3.69)}, \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.72}{(MF3.72)} and \(M'_{\rm act}-M'=\delta_2\) give edge cost \(\le\delta_2+\delta_1/6+7\theta_N/3+\eta_{\rm mesh}\) plus its small-power share. Cauchy averages costs, including the single mesh.

\hypertarget{fixed-character-rows-and-rectangle-cancellation}{}

\label{fixed-character-rows-and-rectangle-cancellation}

\hypertarget{fixed-character-rows-and-rectangle-cancellation-heading-2}{%
\subparagraph{7.2.9 Fixed-character rows and rectangle cancellation}}

\textbf{Exceptional count.} Genuine second allocations avoid old moving support by original zeros, so no old character cancels \(G_cV_{\rm id}\) there. For nonunit common multiplicity-one primes of radical length \(v_1\), put \(f=2v_1\). Their fixed row valuation is two; unramified fixed-modulus induction forces \(v_p(h')\equiv4\pmod6\). More generally each good prime has a prescribed valuation class modulo six, zero outside frozen support. Units and \(S\)-valuations give finitely many sectors, with sixth powers free. Thus \((h')=h_0v^6\), \(h_0\) fixed sixth-power-free, \(q_{h_0}\ge Z^{4v_1}=Z^{2f}\). Counting gives \((m'_{\rm act}-2f)/6\); we use only

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.73}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.73}

\[
\#\{h'\text{ exceptional}:q_{h'}\ll Z^{m'_{\rm act}}\}
\ll Z^{(m'-f)/6+\delta_2/6+o(1)}.
\qquad\text{(MF3.73)}
\]

Below the forced initial norm the range is empty up to fixed endpoints. This counts principal and all other fixed-inducing rows, including positively added partition rows; no \(t_2\) saving is used.

After full Möbius allocation, side \(j\)'s normalized absolute volume is \(O(Z^{(\alpha_j-r_j)/2+e_j})\). Both extraction scalars and divisor count give paired volume \(\le Z^{a_0-b_2+2\theta_N+o(1)}\) by \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.67}{(MF3.67)}, since \(t_--r_1-r_2\le\omega_2\), \(r_1\ge0\). Including the exceptional count, its nominal excess is

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.74}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.74}

\[
\frac{m'-f}{6}+a_0-b_2-(M'+\Delta)
=A-\frac56M-F_1-F_2,
\qquad\text{(MF3.74)}
\]

where

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.75}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.75}

\[
\begin{aligned}
F_1&=c/6+5\{d+(K_0-K)\}/6+w/3+\widetilde q/6+w_o+B_c-5g/6+\ell,\\
F_2&=2b_2-5g_2/6-p_2+t_2+V/6+f/6.
\end{aligned}\qquad\text{(MF3.75)}
\]

These expressions obey

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.76}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.76}

\[
F_1\ge\frac23(c+w)-3\sigma-5\delta_1/6,
\qquad F_2\ge\frac23b_2.
\qquad\text{(MF3.76)}
\]

Initially take \(g=J_+\), \(\ell=0\), \(D_0=d+K_0-K\). For \(J\ge0\), \(F_1=c+2w+\widetilde q/6+w_o/6+B_c\). Otherwise \(\widetilde q\ge R\) gives \(B_c+\widetilde q/6\ge(3c-5D_0)_+/6-5\delta_1/6\), hence \(F_1\ge2(c+w)/3-w/3-5\delta_1/6\). Restoring \(g\le J_++2\sigma\), \(\ell\ge0\), \(w\le7\sigma/3\) costs \(<3\sigma\). For \(F_2\) use the exact local table of §\protect\hyperlink{cf:sec:D.6}{5.6}, with \(j=j_0\):

The four pairs \((F_2,b_2)\) are \((7i/6,i)\), \(((7i-5)/6+1_{i=1}/3,i)\), \((7i/6-1,i)\), and \((i+j_0/6-1,(i+j_0)/2)\) for, respectively, equal non-six-divisible/unit, equal non-six-divisible/nonunit, equal six-divisible, and unequal \(i>j_0\), \(6\mid j_0\)/unit cases.

Each first entry is \(\ge2/3\) of the second; at nonunit \(i=1\), \(f/6\) gives equality. Thus \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.76}{(MF3.76)} holds and uncentered \(A\le5M/6\) costs at most \(3\sigma+O(\xi+\theta_N)\), validating comparisons.

\textbf{Masked lattice cancellation.} Apply §\protect\hyperlink{cf:sec:D.1}{5.1} to fixed \(\vartheta\in\Theta\), polynomial squarefree \(R_*\), annular \(W_i\) and common real \(t\).

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.77}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.77}

\textbf{(MF3.77)} is \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:D.1}{(65)}, with the same \(L_i(X,t)\), \(c_{\vartheta,R_*}\) and \(I_i(t)\), loss \(\epsilon_1\) and \(\langle t\rangle=1+|t|\). It holds uniformly for \(X>0\).

Its proof there is fixed-lattice Poisson followed by complete mask inversion; in particular \(c_{\vartheta,R_*}=c_\vartheta\sum_{d\mid R_*}\mu(d)\vartheta(d)/q_d\). Primary/\(S\) restrictions are fixed lattice classes, and already excluded primes can be omitted. Thus it applies to every retained exceptional mask.

For \(U_i=X_i/q_{b_i}\), \(V_i=Y_i/q_{b_i}\), \(T=U_1U_2=V_1V_2\), the sum of \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.66}{(MF3.66)} is the difference of the two \(L_1L_2\) products. If all four scales are \(\ge Z^r\), \(r\ge0\), then

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.78}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.78}

\textbf{(MF3.78)} is the normalized rectangle bound \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:D.2}{(66)}, with these \(U_i,V_i,T\), loss \(\epsilon_1\) and height factor \(\langle t\rangle^{2J}\).

The same row, mask and norm power make main terms equal. Without a nonnegative lower scale use the all-scale volume \(O(T^{1/2})\).

For centered exceptional children, pre-allocation lower lengths are \(L-c-w-c_2\) and \(L-c-w-d_2\). Put \(r=(L-c-w-\min(c_2,d_2))_+\) and call the saving side one. Final extraction removes at most \(r_1\) from either plain: use \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.78}{(MF3.78)} if \(r_1<r\), otherwise volume. Preserve raw equal-product scales without clipping. Exactly,

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.79}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.79}

\[
t_--r_1-r_2-(r-r_1)_+
=(t_--r_2)-\max(r,r_1)\le\omega_2-r.
\qquad\text{(MF3.79)}
\]

Paired volume is therefore \(Z^{a_0-b_2-r+2\theta_N+o(1)}\), uniformly in live slots. Their labels never entered Fourier density; sum them now by volume with \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.67}{(MF3.67)}'s normalization.

Put \(v=c+w+\min(c_2,d_2)\). From \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.76}{(MF3.76)}, \(F_1+F_2\ge2v/3-3\sigma-5\delta_1/6\). The remaining centered exceptional deficit is bounded by

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.80}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.80}

\[
A-5M/6-2v/3-(L-v)_+\le(A-M)_+,
\qquad L=M/4.
\qquad\text{(MF3.80)}
\]

As in §\protect\hyperlink{cf:sec:D.6}{5.6}, the maximum occurs at \(v=L\) and equals \(A-M\). Positive slots have \(A\le M\), zero-slot padding \(A\le M+\delta\); exceptional terminal cost is \(\le3\sigma+\delta\), with reserved support/frequency errors.

\hypertarget{closure-and-uniform-common-mesh}{}

\label{closure-and-uniform-common-mesh}

\hypertarget{closure-and-uniform-common-mesh-heading-2}{%
\subparagraph{7.2.10 Closure and uniform common mesh}}

Strict nominal widths decrease by \(\sigma\). Actual enclosure/support corrections cost \(C_*\xi\) for numerical \(C_*\) independent of slot count: they involve one aggregate \(\theta_N\), two frequency enclosures, at most two plain clippings and finitely many aggregate lengths in \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.53}{(MF3.53)}--\protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.80}{(MF3.80)}. Ledgers are affine or positive-part affine; positive capacity varies by at most \(|\Delta M|+|\Delta A|+5|\Delta z|\), since \(6\kappa-1\le5\). Greedy removal charges one mesh.

Choose \(\rho,\sigma,\delta\) from the bounded real ranges and final \(\epsilon\), with \(\delta\ll\min(\rho,\sigma)\), so that

\[
T_{\rm term}:=\rho+\delta+\rho/6+3\sigma+5\sigma/3<\epsilon/4.
\]

Put \(D=2+\lceil2M_{\max}/\sigma\rceil\), and, still before specifying the fixed slot system, choose

\hypertarget{mf:eq:B3.81}{}

\label{mf:eq:B3.81}

\par\noindent\resizebox{\linewidth}{!}{\begin{minipage}{\dimexpr\linewidth+67.00496pt\relax}\[
\xi<\min\{\delta/2,\rho\mathfrak m(7/10)/2,\sigma/(4C_*),
                   \epsilon/(16C_*D)\},\qquad
\eta_{\rm mesh}<\min\{\sigma/6,\epsilon/(16C_*D)\},\qquad
\epsilon_0<\epsilon/(16C_*D).
\qquad\text{(MF3.81)}
\]\end{minipage}}\par

These preserve comparison margins, padding and width decrease \(\ge\sigma/2\). Each strict child leaves its band; depth is \(\le D-2\). Same-band comparisons use earlier uncentered stages, each plain reflects at most once before centering, and errors are not re-amplified. Thus the induction is acyclic.

Now fix arithmetic data, profiles and finite slot count, then \(H_N\) and boxes through depth \(D\). Eventually \(\theta_N\ll\xi\), tails satisfy whole-dyad inclusions and pool avoids slots. Allocate stage losses within \(\epsilon_0\): e.g.~at most \(N+2\) mask masses get \(\epsilon_0/(10(N+2))\) each; use global divisor bounds \(C_N^{\omega(v)},d_{N+2}(v)^C\ll_{N,C,a}q_v^a\) for one small \(a\). Prime displacement costs \(2ez\), independent of slot count. These affect constants, not the already chosen mesh.

With \(d\) strict calls remaining allow \(E_d=T_{\rm term}+(d+1)C_*(\eta_{\rm mesh}+\xi+\epsilon_0)\). Floor, diagonal and exception fit \(E_0\); \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.69}{(MF3.69)}--\protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.72}{(MF3.72)} bound a strict edge by its child plus one allowance, including comparisons/reflection masses. Cauchy averages and triangle takes maxima, so terminal loss occurs once. Equation \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.81}{(MF3.81)} gives \(E_{D-2}<\epsilon\), simultaneously for every surviving slot subset.

Choose raw tails and finite profile/height orders backwards, then one final threshold for tails, endpoints and logarithms. Child extra masks lie on frozen columns or a frozen amplifier prime, hence stay polynomial through finite depth. Exceptional masks also include row radicals and are covered uniformly by \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.77}{(MF3.77)}. All moving support, including canceled powers, remains explicit. This proves \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.40}{(MF3.40)}.

For completeness, fixed nonprincipal zero-slot rows are also restored directly. For a primitive nonprincipal \(\vartheta\) of the fixed modulus, orthogonality gives \(c_\vartheta=0\), hence \(c_{\vartheta,R_*}=0\) in \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.77}{(MF3.77)}. Each normalized nonempty \(X\ge1\) plain is \(O(Z^{\epsilon_1}X^{-1/2}\langle t\rangle^J)\); bounded subunit scales use volume. Prescribed valuations modulo six give \(O(Z^{m/6+\epsilon_1})\) rows by the exceptional parameterization without forced-prime savings. The fixed family is finite, so multiplying and choosing small \(\epsilon_1\) restores all such rows within \(Z^{M+\epsilon}\), with independent heights and common masks. Principal inducing characters remain excluded. \(\square\)

\hypertarget{the-two-incumbent-ranges-and-the-physical-interfaces}{}

\label{the-two-incumbent-ranges-and-the-physical-interfaces}

\hypertarget{the-two-incumbent-ranges-and-the-physical-interfaces-heading-2}{%
\paragraph{7.3 Natural moments and the physical interfaces}}

\textbf{Proposition 7.6} (Refined incumbent moment). \emph{\hypertarget{mf:plain-refined}{}\label{mf:plain-refined} The same natural moment, with each active \(0<z_i\le\eta_{\rm mesh}\), is valid for \(7/10\le\kappa\le3/4\), \(\beta\ge51/100\) and \(2\beta-1\le\kappa\), with its mesh chosen uniformly in this interval before the slot system, profiles and target height.}

\emph{Proof.} Restrict the same proof to \([7/10,3/4]\): \(6\kappa-1\le7/2\), margin \(\ge\mathfrak m(7/10)M>0\), removal ratio \(1/6\). Equation \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.81}{(MF3.81)} preserves the stated quantifier order. The mesh and all profile controls remain common on this closed interval. \(\square\)

\hypertarget{literal-initial-fields-and-positivity.}{}

\label{literal-initial-fields-and-positivity.}

Literal initial fields and positivity.

For fixed source \(\eta\), take scale \(U\), puncture one, width \(1+\delta\) once \(q_{\mathfrak q_\eta}\le U^\delta\), and keep set \(\operatorname{initialKeep}(\eta,\mathfrak Q_0)\). At \(X_1=X_2=U^m\), Theorem \protect\hyperlink{mf:plain}{7.4} gives

\par\noindent\resizebox{\linewidth}{!}{\begin{minipage}{\dimexpr\linewidth+10.05424pt\relax}\[
\sum_k\mathbf1_{\operatorname{initialKeep}(\eta,\mathfrak Q_0)}(k)
 \Re\Phi(q_k/U)\,|S_k(U^m)^2\prod_{i\in I}Q_{k,i}|^2
\ll \mathcal P_J(W_1,W_2)^{2}\mathcal D_J(\Phi)
 (1+|t_1|+|t_2|+\max_i|v_i|)^h U^{1+\delta+\epsilon}
\]\end{minipage}}\par

provided \(2m+6\kappa\sum_{i\in I}z_i\le1\) and \(0<z_i\le\eta_{\rm mesh}\). Here \(\mathcal P_J\) uses finitely many plain Euler seminorms (common slot profile fixed in the constant); \(\mathcal D_J\) controls the nonnegative radial Schwartz profile. Choose \(J,h\) before heights and live subset. Absorb \(\delta\) and local loss into the requested loss. This physical field is \(|S|^4|\prod Q_i|^2\).

Theorem \protect\hyperlink{mf:inverse}{7.3} admits literal relative-character slot coefficients, fixed ray Fourier coefficients and common character conjugation, all bounded and row-independent. Shared inverse primes are allocated by \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.32}{(MF3.32)}. At \(m=1\) the gates are \(r+2z<1\), \(2r+8z<3\). For finitely many profiles/derivatives take maximum orders/thresholds and union seminorms. All energies use nonnegative measures and squared moduli, so retained subfamilies decrease them. Signed Möbius coefficients remain until their full identity is restored.

\hypertarget{every-rescaled-completed-term.}{}

\label{every-rescaled-completed-term.}

Every rescaled completed term.

Theorem \protect\hyperlink{mf:marked-energy}{7.1} covers all original slot subsets. Priority allocation to frozen products preserves row-independent surviving coefficients, retaining exact central scalars and tuple counts outside. For physical rescaling by subset width \(d\),

\[
M'=M-2d,\qquad z'=\ell-d,\qquad N'=1+\ell-d.
\]

If \(M+\ell\le1\), then \(M'+2z'\le N'\) for every such subset. The energy exponent is therefore \(M'+(1+3\ell-2M+d)_+/4+\epsilon\). After the physical Cauchy square root, its exact compensator and assigned tuple mass give the additional exponent

\[
-d+\frac18(1+3\ell-2M+d)_+.
\]

Its slopes are \(-1,-7/8\), so the maximum is \(d=0\); this pays every rescaled term with its actual outside coefficient. Individual energies need not be \(\le Z^{M'}\).

\textbf{Lemma 7.7} (Actual finite source rows). \emph{\hypertarget{mf:finite-source-field}{}\label{mf:finite-source-field} Let \(\mathcal R\) be a finite row family with an injective map \(\iota:\mathcal R\longrightarrow\mathcal O\). Suppose that, for each \(u\in\mathcal R\), its displayed character \(\chi_u\) agrees at every ideal with the natural function \(n\mapsto\eta(n)\chi_n(\iota(u))\), including the fixed bad-prime zero mask. Suppose also that \(\iota(u)\in\operatorname{initialKeep}(\eta,\mathfrak Q_0)\) and \(q_{\iota(u)}\le U\). Choose a fixed smooth radial majorant \(\Phi\ge0\) with \(\Phi\ge1\) on \([0,1]\). Then the unweighted finite-row plain times marked-prime energy is at most the initial-state positive energy in Theorem~\protect\hyperlink{mf:plain}{7.4}. It consequently obeys its bound \(CU^{1+\delta+\epsilon}\) and the stated polynomial height control whenever \(2m+6\kappa z\le1\).}

\emph{Proof.} The literal character identity identifies all plain and relative-slot coefficients. Injectivity reindexes without multiplicity; the radial majorant and keep indicator dominate on the image. Enlarge only the resulting nonnegative squared-modulus sum. For profiles with at most two logarithmic derivatives, the product rule for \(W(y)y^{-\sigma+it}\) gives finite derivatives of \(W\) times polynomials in bounded \(\sigma\) and height \(t\). Combine with the moment's height polynomial. Degree and constant precede live rows, subset, ray characters and heights, proving the stated finite field and uniformity. \(\square\)

\hypertarget{ov:section}{}

\label{ov:section}

\hypertarget{overlapping-prime-lists-in-the-inverse-moment}{%
\subsubsection{8 Overlapping prime lists in the inverse moment}\label{overlapping-prime-lists-in-the-inverse-moment}}

This coefficient-specific extension precedes affine arguments and is endpoint-independent.

\textbf{Lemma 8.1} (Overlap adapter for the inverse moment). \emph{\hypertarget{ov:inverse-overlap}{}\label{ov:inverse-overlap} The estimate of Theorem~\protect\hyperlink{mf:inverse}{7.3} remains valid for a fixed finite number of possibly overlapping or repeated prime lists with the same bounded annular coefficients. Both admission inequalities may use the full sum of the original slot widths.}

\emph{Proof.} Partition finite prime tuples by exact equality pattern. Freeze each block of \(k\ge2\) equal primes before the row norm. Its intersecting annuli have comparable \(D_i\), giving central mass

\[
\sum_{\mathfrak p\in\bigcap_i\mathcal P_i}
       \prod_{i=1}^k D_i^{-1/2}|b_i(\mathfrak p)|
 \ll (\min_iD_i)\prod_{i=1}^k D_i^{-1/2}
 \ll D^{1-k/2}\ll1.
\]

Constants depend only on fixed slot count and profile suprema. Keep \(\varphi_u(\mathfrak p)^k\) until taking the positive norm, then contract by its modulus \(\le1\) with zeros retained. The inverse polynomial is unchanged and may share that prime.

Delete frozen primes from singleton lists. For \(K\) singletons, color their union independently with \(K\) colors. Each distinct ordered tuple receives prescribed slot colors with probability \(K^{-K}\); repeated tuples never do. Its polynomial is therefore \(K^K\) times the average over products on disjoint, row-independent colored lists. Apply Theorem \protect\hyperlink{mf:inverse}{7.3}, then Minkowski/Jensen, costing \(K^{2K}\) in energy. For \(K=0\) use UM. Deleting slots decreases both admission expressions.

Triangle over bounded-mass frozen choices and finitely many equality patterns proves the claim. This coloring extension applies only to MI; MP retains its canonical-pool contract and overlap allocation. \(\square\)

\hypertarget{init:section}{}

\label{init:section}

\hypertarget{the-zero-supremum-and-the-natural-moment-range}{%
\subsubsection{9 The zero supremum and the natural moment range}\label{the-zero-supremum-and-the-natural-moment-range}}

Let \(\mathcal H\) be the presented finite-order Eisenstein Hecke family with prescribed Euler factors and natural zeros. Its actual zero supremum is

\[
\beta=\sup\left(\{\tfrac12\}\cup
 \{\Re\rho:\chi\in\mathcal H,\ \Re\rho>0,\
 L_K(\rho,\chi)=0,\ (\chi,\rho)\ne(1,1)\}\right).
\]

Absolute convergence of the Euler product in \(\Re s>1\) gives \(1/2\le\beta\le1\). The principal pole at one is excluded throughout.

\textbf{Lemma 9.1} (Primitive targets approaching the supremum). \emph{\hypertarget{init:primitive-supremum}{}\label{init:primitive-supremum} The same supremum is obtained from primitive targets. If \(\varepsilon>0\) and \(\beta-\varepsilon\ge1/2\), there is a primitive target with a zero \(\rho\) satisfying \(\Re\rho>\beta-\varepsilon\).}

\emph{Proof.} A presentation induced by \(\chi^*\) has \(L_K(s,\chi^*)\) times finitely many \(1-\chi^*(\mathfrak p)(N\mathfrak p)^{-s}\). These are one when the value is zero and otherwise vanish only on \(\Re s=0\). Primitive and presented positive-half-plane zeros coincide. Supremum approximation then supplies the asserted primitive zero; no attainment is needed. \(\square\)

\textbf{Lemma 9.3} (A common-reserve Mellin contradiction). \emph{\hypertarget{init:mellin-return}{}\label{init:mellin-return} Let \(1/2\le\theta<\beta\), and suppose \(k_0\) is real. Suppose there are common numbers \(\omega,\sigma_0>0\) with \(\omega<\beta-\theta\) such that each primitive target has a probe \(J_\eta\) and a function \(H_\eta\), holomorphic on \(\Re s>\theta\), satisfying \hypertarget{init:signal}{}\label{init:signal}}

\[
|H_\eta(s)-1|\le\frac12,
 \qquad
 f_\eta(Z)=\frac1{2\pi i}\int_{(c)}
 Z^{s-k_0}e^{(s-5/6)^2}
 \frac{H_\eta(s)}{L_K(s,\eta)}\,ds,
 \qquad\text{(I.3)}
\]

\emph{on an absolute line \(c>1\), with the stated vertical convergence. Assume that \(f_\eta\) decays faster than every power at zero and that, for large \(Z\), \hypertarget{init:two-probe-bounds}{}\label{init:two-probe-bounds}}

\[
J_\eta(Z)\ll_\eta Z^{\theta-k_0+\omega},\qquad
 J_\eta(Z)-f_\eta(Z)\ll_\eta Z^{\beta-k_0-\sigma_0}.
 \qquad\text{(I.4)}
\]

\emph{Then these hypotheses contradict the definition of \(\beta\).}

\emph{Proof.} Put \(m=\min(\beta-\theta-\omega,\sigma_0)>0\). The two bounds on the same probe give \(f_\eta(Z)\ll_\eta Z^{\beta-k_0-m}\), so

\[
F_\eta(s)=\int_0^\infty f_\eta(Z)Z^{k_0-s}\frac{dZ}{Z}
\]

is locally uniformly holomorphic for \(\Re s>\beta-m\): use this bound at infinity and arbitrary decay at zero, also after logarithmic differentiation. Absolute-line Fourier--Mellin inversion identifies it with the Gaussian quotient in \protect\hyperlink{init:signal}{(I.3)}; meromorphic identity continues throughout this connected half-plane.

Since \(\beta-m\ge\theta+\omega>\theta\), the numerator is holomorphic and nonzero (\(|H_\eta|\ge1/2\)). Any denominator zero, of any multiplicity, would give an impossible pole. Euler deletions change no such zero by Lemma \protect\hyperlink{init:primitive-supremum}{9.1}; the principal pole creates no reciprocal pole.

Thus every primitive target is zero-free for \(\Re s>\beta-m\), with common \(m\) despite target-dependent constants. Lemma \protect\hyperlink{init:primitive-supremum}{9.1} at \(\varepsilon=m/2\) supplies a forbidden zero, since \(\beta-m/2>\theta\ge1/2\). \(\square\)

\hypertarget{sr:chapter}{}

\label{sr:chapter}

\hypertarget{the-analytic-reduction-of-the-literal-initial-source}{%
\subsubsection{10 The analytic reduction of the literal initial source}\label{the-analytic-reduction-of-the-literal-initial-source}}

We carry the literal marked coefficient through the two Poisson formulas of Chapters 1 and 7, preserving actual pools, allocations and normalizers. Later source classes are restrictions of these expressions.

\hypertarget{sr:coefficient}{}

\label{sr:coefficient}

\hypertarget{the-coefficient-and-the-reference-comparison}{%
\paragraph{10.1 The coefficient and the reference comparison}\label{the-coefficient-and-the-reference-comparison}}

Fix \(U>1\), \(H_0=U^M\), a finite slot set \(I\), \(P_i=U^{w_i}\) and

\[
b_i(\mathfrak p)=\nu_i(\mathfrak p)W(q_{\mathfrak p}/P_i)
       (q_{\mathfrak p}/P_i)^{-\sigma_i+iv_i}.
\]

Each relative \(\nu_i\) retains both defining zero sets. Pools contain all support primes in the identity ray class and may overlap. Actual affine input has \(\sigma_i=33/50\), \(\operatorname{supp}W\subset[1,2]\). Put \(\chi_z(n)=\eta(n)\chi_n(m^6Az)\) with original zeros, and

\[
L_z(p,X;t)=\sum_{n\ne0}\chi_z(n)q_n^{it}p(q_n/X),\qquad
 V=X_1X_2\prod_{i\in I}P_i.
\]

The energy to reduce is

\hypertarget{sr:energy}{}

\label{sr:energy}

\[
\qquad\text{(115)}
 E_X=\frac1V\sum_{\substack{z\ne0\\\operatorname{keep}(z)}}
 \Phi(q_z/H_0)
 \left|L_z(p_1,X_1;t)L_z(p_2,X_2;t)
       \prod_i\sum_{\mathfrak p\in\mathcal P_i}
         b_i(\mathfrak p)\chi_z(\mathfrak p)q_{\mathfrak p}^{it}\right|^2 .
\]

Use real nonnegative \(\Phi\) (or \(\Re\Phi\) in the complex-profile convention). Keep the incumbent's actual natural predicate and fixed conductor ideal dividing \((72)\); later zero bins do not replace it.

Assume \(0<M_{\min}\le M\le M_{\max}\) and

\hypertarget{sr:initial-scales}{}

\label{sr:initial-scales}

\[
\qquad\text{(116)}
 .41M\le n_i:=\log_UX_i\le .44M,\qquad
 w_i\ge0,\qquad z_I:=\sum_iw_i\le M/25.
\]

Choose the reference rectangle

\hypertarget{sr:comparison}{}

\label{sr:comparison}

\[
\qquad\text{(117)}
 Y_1=U^{M/4},\quad Y_2=X_1X_2/Y_1,\qquad
 D(l_1,l_2)=p_1(q_{l_1}/X_1)p_2(q_{l_2}/X_2)
          -p_1(q_{l_1}/Y_1)p_2(q_{l_2}/Y_2).
\]

The rectangles share product, whole character, mask, slots and norm power. Keep their difference until positive child norms form.

For actual-pool tuples \(\mathbf l=(p_i,l_1,l_2)\) on the union of plain annuli, put \(J(\mathbf l)=(\prod p_i)l_1l_2\). With common puncture \(R\) and squarefree seed \(s\), define

\hypertarget{sr:beta}{}

\label{sr:beta}

\[
\qquad\text{(118)}
 \beta_{R,s}(J)=
 \sum_{\substack{\mathbf l\\J(\mathbf l)=J}}
 \left(\prod_i b_i(p_i)\right)D(l_1,l_2)
 \mathbf1_{(J,R)=1}\mathbf1_{s\mid J}.
\]

Initially \(R=s=1\). Zeros remain zero; later masks/divisors are actual extractions, not new conditions on plain quotients.

\textbf{Lemma 10.1} (The original comparison). \emph{\hypertarget{sr:comparison-bound}{}\label{sr:comparison-bound} Suppose \(\beta\ge51/100\) and \(2\beta-1\le3/4\). For every \(\epsilon>0\) one common positive slot mesh gives}

\[
E_Y\ll\mathcal D(\Phi)\mathcal H_J
       (1+|t|+\max_i|v_i|)^J U^{M+\epsilon},
 \qquad
 \mathcal D(\Phi)=16(1+\pi)^2\max_{0\le j\le2}\sup_x|x|^j|\Phi(x)|.
\]

\emph{The mesh precedes the fixed dictionary; subsequent orders/constants/threshold cover all surviving subsets, four plain derivative pairs and \protect\hyperlink{sr:initial-scales}{(116)}.}

\emph{Proof.} Comparison lengths are \(M/4,n_1+n_2-M/4\). Reflect the second whole factor if \(>M/2\). By \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.42}{(MF3.42)}, conductor times redundant radical is \(O(U^M)\), hence retained reflected length \(\le M/2+\xi\). Then

\[
M/4+(M/2+\xi)+(9/2)z_I\le .93M+\xi<M
\]

For \(\xi<.07M_{\min}\) with loss reserve, apply Theorem \protect\hyperlink{mf:plain}{7.4} at \(\kappa=3/4\), or unrestricted zero-slot bounds. Capacity is applied after reflection.

Use the exact natural reflection \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.44}{(MF3.44)}: it retains all holes, has \(U^{o(1)}\) coefficient mass, and after whole-factor conjugation restores orientation without changing relative slots. Its profile has bounded zero-end Euler derivatives, rapid upper decay and summable central annuli; empty annuli vanish and retained subunit scales dilate boundedly.

Apply common-profile/Sobolev control to rowwise scales and Stirling to heights. After fixing the dictionary, take finite maxima over subsets and four profile pairs, before live labels/heights. \(\square\)

\hypertarget{sr:first}{}

\label{sr:first}

\hypertarget{the-first-exact-source}{%
\paragraph{10.2 The first exact source}\label{the-first-exact-source}}

Let \(E_D\) be the energy of the whole difference in \protect\hyperlink{sr:comparison}{(117)}. Positivity gives

\hypertarget{sr:positive-comparison}{}

\label{sr:positive-comparison}

\[
\qquad\text{(119)}
 E_X\le2E_D+2E_Y.
\]

Enlarge only \(E_D\) to all rows; never transform the keep predicate.

Fix nonnegative smooth \(\Psi\) supported in \([-1,b_\Phi+1]\), \(\Psi\ge1\) on \([0,b_\Phi]\), so \(0\le\Phi\le\mathcal D(\Phi)\Psi\) for \(x\ge0\). Before live subsets/labels, expand \protect\hyperlink{sr:beta}{(118)} in the full lattice square and extract complete common support \(Ca,Db\), \((a,b)=(ab,CD)=1\). Define

\[
c=\log_Uq_C,\ d=\log_Uq_D,\ p=\log_Uq_{\operatorname{rad}(CD)},\
 A_0=\log_UV.
\]

Let \(r\) contain nonzero net common sextic exponents, length \(R_1\), primitive character \(\xi_r\). Expand the remaining mask by \(e\mid\operatorname{rad}(CD)/r\), length \(E_1\), so \(R_1+E_1\le p\). For actual allocated convolutions \(A_C,A_D\), nonzero frequency gives

\hypertarget{sr:first-poisson}{}

\label{sr:first-poisson}

\[
\qquad\text{(120)}
 \begin{aligned}
 \frac{H_0}{Vq_e\sqrt{q_r}}\sum_{h\ne0}\mathfrak g_{\xi_r}(r,h)
 \sum_{(a,b)=1}&\frac{A_C(a)\overline{A_D(b)}}{\sqrt{q_aq_b}}
 \tau_C(a)\overline{\tau_D(b)}R(a,b)\\
 &\times\mathfrak g(a,h)\overline{\mathfrak g(b,-h)}
 \widehat\Psi\!\left(\frac{H_0q_h}{q_eq_rq_aq_b}\right).
 \end{aligned}
\]

One orientation is \(\tau_C(a)=\tau(a)\chi_a(er)\overline{\xi_r(a)}\), \(\tau_D(b)=\tau(b)\chi_b(er)\xi_r(b)\). Keep in \(A_C\) the exact frozen CRT scalar, \(\mu(e)\) and fixed character values, including zeros. Convention-independently, if \(f_{C,D,e}(x)\) is the actual active common character times both residual zero-extended characters at \(ex\), define

\[
\mathcal T_{C,D,e}(h)=\sum_{x\bmod rab}
            f_{C,D,e}(x)e(hx/(rab)).
\]

Poisson multiplies it by \(H_0/(q_eq_rq_aq_b)\); CRT defines all displayed factors simultaneously, preserving unit phases. Three Gauss sums restore \(\sqrt{q_rq_aq_b}\) and central normalization gives \(1/V\). The cross factor \(\chi_a(br)\overline{\chi_b(ar)}\overline{\xi_r(ab)}\) gives \(R(a,b)\) by reciprocity. This proves the formula with nonunit zeros; changing orientation conjugates the whole character.

On retained smooth frequency dyads define \(\mathcal B_q\) as \(V\) times the normalized block \protect\hyperlink{sr:first-poisson}{(120)}, removing its \(1/V\) once. Include all common labels, inactive subsets and dyadic coordinates. Its normalized contribution is \(\mathcal B_q/V\) and physical mass is

\hypertarget{sr:physical-mass}{}

\label{sr:physical-mass}

\[
\qquad\text{(121)}
 \mathcal M_{\rm phys}=\sum_q|\mathcal B_q|
   =\left|\sum_q\zeta_q\mathcal B_q\right|,\qquad
 \zeta_q=\begin{cases}|\mathcal B_q|/\mathcal B_q,&\mathcal B_q\ne0,\\
 0,&\mathcal B_q=0.\end{cases}
\]

First labels thus stay in one joint sum, without an extra count.

The powerful zero-frequency argument of §\protect\hyperlink{first-poisson-from-plain-products-to-gauss-polynomials}{7.2.6}, now with full product norm \(V\), gives \(O(H_0U^\epsilon\mathcal H_J)\) after divisor allocations and central \(1/V\), retaining every mask.

The nominal first frequency length is

\hypertarget{sr:first-scale}{}

\label{sr:first-scale}

\textbf{(122)} is \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:D.12}{(76)} under \((A,R,E,m)=(A_0,R_1,E_1,M)\): \(K_0=2A_0-c-d+R_1+E_1-M\).

Retain whole dyads through each outer box's maximum scale with enclosure \(\delta_1<\xi\). Discarded genuine arguments are \(\ge U^{\xi/4}\) after fixed support errors. Use \(|\mathfrak g(a,h)|\le\sqrt{q_a}\) and polynomial counts, fixing raw cost before Schwartz order. For every fixed \(L>0\),

\hypertarget{sr:first-remainder}{}

\label{sr:first-remainder}

\[
\qquad\text{(123)}
 E_D\le\mathcal D(\Phi)\{\mathcal M_{\rm phys}/V+
 C\mathcal H_JH_0(U^\epsilon+U^{-L})\}.
\]

\textbf{Proposition 10.2} (Initial energy reduced to its actual source). \emph{\hypertarget{sr:initial-reduction}{}\label{sr:initial-reduction} Under Lemma~\protect\hyperlink{sr:comparison-bound}{10.1}, one \(\Psi\), positive slot mesh, finite profile orders and \(J\) give \hypertarget{sr:initial-inequality}{}\label{sr:initial-inequality}}

\[
\qquad\text{(124)}
 E_X\le2\mathcal D(\Phi)\mathcal M_{\rm phys}/V+
 C\mathcal D(\Phi)\mathcal H_J(1+|t|+\max_i|v_i|)^J U^{M+\epsilon}.
\]

\emph{After fixing arithmetic and dictionary, thresholds are uniform over live subsets, natural masks and initial scales. Descendants need satisfy no affine condition.}

\emph{Proof.} Combine \protect\hyperlink{sr:positive-comparison}{(119)}, \protect\hyperlink{sr:first-remainder}{(123)} and Lemma 10.1. Initial supports eventually give \(V,H_0^{-1}\le U^{M_{\max}+1}\), the needed coarse caps. Relative norm twists/derivatives have polynomial seminorm cost. Take finite maxima over subsets/anchors; allocate \(\epsilon/2\) to comparison/remainder and absorb logarithms in the rest. \(\square\)

\hypertarget{sr:kernel-budget}{}

\label{sr:kernel-budget}

\hypertarget{complete-kernels-and-allocation-normalizers}{%
\paragraph{10.3 Complete kernels and allocation normalizers}\label{complete-kernels-and-allocation-normalizers}}

On a retained dyad the nonseparated kernel contains

\[
\omega_K(q_h/U^K)\widehat\Psi(H_0q_h/(q_eq_rq_aq_b))
                  (q_aq_b)^{-1/2}.
\]

Multiply by common log-row and whole-column cutoffs, shared by both rectangles. For the resulting compact logarithmic kernel,

\hypertarget{sr:full-kernel}{}

\label{sr:full-kernel}

\[
\qquad\text{(125)}
 \mathcal K(\mathbf x)=\int_{\mathbb R^d}\widehat{\mathcal K}(\mathbf u)
       e^{2\pi i\mathbf u\cdot\mathbf x}\,d\mathbf u,\qquad
 \int(1+\|\mathbf u\|)^J|\widehat{\mathcal K}(\mathbf u)|\,d\mathbf u<\infty.
\]

Bounded Euler derivatives of \(\widehat\Psi\) and integration by parts of order \(>J+d\) give live-column-independent seminorm control. Separate the second full kernel/roots in the same order.

Extend \protect\hyperlink{sr:first-poisson}{(120)} off coprimality using separate Gauss sums, fixed bicharacter and original masks. Insert complete \(\sum_{s\mid(a,b)}\mu(s)\) before factorwise estimates; artificial primes gain no common-support restrictions. Expand the ray factor and apply joint Cauchy after \protect\hyperlink{sr:full-kernel}{(125)}, giving

\hypertarget{sr:gauss-column}{}

\label{sr:gauss-column}

\[
\qquad\text{(126)}
 \sum_h\omega_K(q_h/U^K)
 \left|U^{-(A_0-c)/2}\sum_{s\mid a}
       A_C(a)\tau'_C(a)q_a^{it}\mathfrak g(a,h)\right|^2,
\]

and similarly on the right. Set

\[
B_c=(3c-5d-R_1)_+/6,\qquad B_d=(3d-5c-R_1)_+/6,
\]

\hypertarget{sr:target-exponents}{}

\label{sr:target-exponents}

\[
\qquad\text{(127)}
 S_C=p+A_0-c+R_1+E_1+B_c,\quad
 S_D=p+A_0-d+R_1+E_1+B_d,\quad
 O_0=M-A_0-R_1/2-E_1.
\]

By \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.56}{(MF3.56)}, \(O_0+(S_C+S_D)/2\le M\). A fixed divisor width \(s_0\) leaves column allowance \(A_0-c+R_1+E_1+B_c-s_0\); one common-label count \(p+s_0\) gives \(S_C\). Keep that count inside joint weights.

Let \(j\) index actual first columns, divisors and ray characters in a common box, with chosen common ideal \(C_j\). An allocation \(B=(B_i,B_1,B_2)\) extracts each factor's complete \(C_j\)-part, \(\prod_iB_iB_1B_2=C_j\). Freeze supplying slots; survivors keep coefficients. Divide both rectangle scales of each plain by the same \(q_{B_k}\) and puncture all residual factors at complete support, even if exponents cancel.

Let \(F_B\) be the actual frozen coefficient with profiles, \(M_B=\prod_{i\,\mathrm{frozen}}M_i\) its bound. Retain \(F_B=0\) before enlargement. For slot support bound \(b\) and ambient count \(N\), allocation Cauchy gives

\hypertarget{sr:allocation-weight}{}

\label{sr:allocation-weight}

\[
\qquad\text{(128)}
 \lambda_{j,B}=
 \begin{cases}
 |\rho_j|^2(\max(1,b))^N
       \dfrac{\#\operatorname{Alloc}(C_j)}{q_{C_j}}M_B^2,&F_B\ne0,\\
 0,&F_B=0.
 \end{cases}
\]

Let \(I_B\) be the surviving slots. The actual child volume and exact central scalar are

\[
V_{j,B}=\frac{X_1X_2}{q_{B_1}q_{B_2}}\prod_{i\in I_B}P_i,\qquad
 \frac{V_{j,B}}V=\frac1{q_{C_j}}\prod_{i\notin I_B}\frac{q_{p_i}}{P_i}.
\]

Factoring \(F_B\), the original normalized sum is exactly \(F_B\sqrt{V_{j,B}/V}\) times the \(V_{j,B}^{-1/2}\)-normalized child. Since \(q_{p_i}/P_i\le b\), its squared scalar is \(\le(\max(1,b))^NM_B^2/q_{C_j}\), yielding \protect\hyperlink{sr:allocation-weight}{(128)}. Actual child volume divides its energy; the first allocation weight stays undivided.

The scalar \(\rho_j\) retains the first Poisson coefficient. For original complex block scalar \(a_j\), including \protect\hyperlink{sr:physical-mass}{(121)}'s phase, an asymmetric split is

\[
\rho_{C,j}=a_jU^{-O_0-S_C/2},\qquad \rho_{D,j}=U^{-S_D/2}.
\]

Five geometry coordinates vary by \(\le\log_U2\) per box; the positive-part allowance varies by at most \(3/2\) times this width. Cross-label Cauchy costs \(U^{5\log_U2}\), a constant, after freezing common coordinates---not the square of the label count.

For extracted datum \(s\), let \(\beta_s\) be \protect\hyperlink{sr:beta}{(118)}'s residual coefficient without \(F_B\), \(\tau_s\) its whole character and \(V_s\) its actual volume. Define

\hypertarget{sr:normalized-gauss}{}

\label{sr:normalized-gauss}

\[
\qquad\text{(129)}
 G(s;\Omega,L)=\frac1{V_s}\sum_{h\in\mathcal O}\Omega(q_h/L)
 \left|\sum_u\beta_s(u)\tau_s(u)q_u^{it}\mathfrak g(u,h)\right|^2 .
\]

The side budget is \(\mathcal L=\sum_{j,B}\lambda_{j,B}G(s_{j,B};\Omega,L_j)\), with fixed annular majorant on \([1/8,2]\), including all columns/allocations.

The column window has an independent Mellin coordinate: left/conjugate-right raw heights are \(t+2\pi(u-v)\), \(t+2\pi(u+v)\). Window densities have common finite weighted \(L^1\) norms by integration by parts; Minkowski integrates the polynomial-height \(\mathcal L\) bound through both full windows. Bounded row-profile parameters and partition scalars contract the joint norm.

\hypertarget{sr:amplifier}{}

\label{sr:amplifier}

\hypertarget{amplification-with-its-exact-local-errors}{%
\paragraph{10.4 Corrected MAIN amplification}\label{amplification-with-its-exact-local-errors}}

Set \(M=1\). For each selected source cell, use its exact rational
parameters from (S4) for the seed closure and (NF4) for the later closure.
Thus the earlier LOW face \((B_0,\Lambda_0)\), the new face \((B,\Lambda)\),
its slope \(q\), cell endpoint \(b\), width reserve \(e\) (\(e=\mu\) in (S4)), and return reserve \(r\) define the amplifier by
\[
 t=B-\Lambda(b-e),\qquad
 a=\frac{q\{b+(t-B_0)/\Lambda_0\}+r}
         {1-6qB_0/\Lambda_0},\qquad \sigma=3a.
\]
The rational gates in those certificates give a positive denominator and
\hypertarget{sr:amp-range}{}\label{sr:amp-range}
\[
 0<a<1/75.\qquad\text{(130)}
\]
There are finitely many selected cells. Choose the common slot mesh below
half their minimum positive \(a\), and keep that cell's amplifier fixed
across rectangles, derivatives and descendants.

Use the literal prime pool

\[
\mathcal P=\{\mathfrak p:U^a/2<q_{\mathfrak p}\le U^a,\
 \mathfrak p\notin S_{\rm bad},\
 \mathfrak p\text{ in the prescribed identity ray class}\}.
\]

\begin{sloppypar}
Fixed-extension Chebotarev in the abelian ray-class extension \(L/K\) gives \(\pi_{\{1\}}(x)\sim|\operatorname{Gal}(L/K)|^{-1}\operatorname{Li}(x)\); subtract at \(x,x/2\). A primary reference is Thorner--Zaman, \emph{A unified and improved Chebotarev density theorem}, Algebra Number Theory 13 (2019), 1039--1068, arXiv:1803.02823v3, Theorem 1.1. For fixed \(L\), a possible exceptional real zero is \(<1\) and contributes \(o(x/\log x)\). Thus \(\#\mathcal P=U^{a+o(1)}\ge U^{a-\ell}\) for each fixed \(\ell>0\), uniformly over finite cells, with ray-dependent threshold and no new zero-free assumption. Eventually the pool avoids all slots. 
\end{sloppypar}

Use the corrected whole coefficient \(\widetilde b\) of (CA4),
