retaining the squarefree ordinary-product projector and the same common
puncture. The local Gauss identity (8) gives (CA3); averaging its exact
pool identity gives
\hypertarget{sr:amp-identity}{}\label{sr:amp-identity}
\[
 F_b(h)=\frac1{\#\mathcal P}
       \sum_{p\in\mathcal P}F_{\widetilde b}(p^6h).
 \qquad\text{(131)}
\]
This is (CA5), applied before the positive norm to the complete centered
coefficient, with all masks, natural zeros and profile heights retained.

The actual retained dyad has scale \(K\le K_0+\delta\). The identity

\[
K+(d+K_0-K-c)_++2\sigma
    =\max(K+2\sigma,d+K_0-c+2\sigma)
\]

gives the MAIN radius
\hypertarget{sr:main-radius}{}\label{sr:main-radius}
\[
 L_{\rm main}=U^{K_0+(d-c)_++6a+\delta+r}.
 \qquad\text{(134)}
\]

Reserve \(r>0\), \(U^r\ge2\), pays smooth-ball doubling. For sector constant one, \(\delta=\xi/2+\log_U128\). These derive from the original retained annulus. Negative exponents still define positive radii; test actual emptiness and never count a nonempty bounded range below one.

\textbf{Proposition 10.3} (MAIN amplification of the actual affine source).
\emph{\hypertarget{sr:affine-amplification}{}\label{sr:affine-amplification}
For the actual nonnegative weights (128), put
\(\mathcal M(s)=G_{\widetilde b}(s;\Phi_{\rm ball},L_{\rm main})\),
using the corrected whole coefficient in every transformed row. Then}
\hypertarget{sr:amplified-budget}{}\label{sr:amplified-budget}
\[
 \mathcal L\le\frac{56}{\#\mathcal P}
 \frac{15/2+6a}{a/2}\sum_{j,B}\lambda_{j,B}\mathcal M(s_{j,B}).
 \qquad\text{(136)}
\]
The threshold precedes live labels and weights, orientations and heights.

\emph{Proof.} Apply Jensen to (131), before any source estimate.
Push its terms through \((h,p)\mapsto p^6h\). If \(q_p\ge U^{a/2}\) and the output norm is at most
\(U^{C+6a}\), their product divides that output, so there are at most
\((C+6a)/(a/2)\) preimages. Formula (134) gives the radius majorant.
Support \([1/8,2]\) meets at most seven dyadic bands. The seven-band factor is at most the conservative constant 56.
The full pool average in (131) is independent of frequency.

Initially \(V\le U^2\), \(H_0^{-1}\le1\), and actual columns/pools have norms \(\le U^3\) eventually. Retained scales satisfy

\[
8L_j\le U^{3\max(2,0)+1/2+1}=U^{15/2}\qquad(0\le\xi\le1).
\]

Nominal scale is \(\le V^3H_0^{-1}\), then \(U^{\xi/2}\) and factor \(\le32\) absorbed by \(U\). Thus \(C=15/2\) works uniformly. Padded zero columns justify support admission only and add no source mass.

Multiply by \(\lambda_{j,B}\ge0\) and sum, taking one threshold over the finite selected cells and live subsets. The same pool and factor \(56/\#\mathcal P\) occur once outside the joint sum. \(\square\)

\hypertarget{sr:second}{}

\label{sr:second}

\hypertarget{second-transform-and-normalized-source-classes}{%
\paragraph{10.5 Second transform and normalized source classes}\label{second-transform-and-normalized-source-classes}}

Terms retain the whole corrected Gauss source of (131). MAIN has
nominal density \(U^{-a+o(1)}\) from the prime average. Put
\[
 a_0=A_0-c,\qquad J=d-c+(K_0-K),\qquad g=J_++6a.
\]
Carry radius padding unchanged and restore live slots before squaring.
For complete \(B(u)\),

\hypertarget{sr:second-poisson}{}

\label{sr:second-poisson}

\textbf{(137)} is \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.60}{(MF3.60)} under \(Z=U\), \(\mathcal G=\mathfrak g\), \(\tau_1=\tau\), \(K_2=\widehat\Phi_2\), with the same full \(B(u)\), central \(U^{-a_0/2}\) and density \(U^{-\ell}\).

Take \(\ell=a\) after multiplying both sides by nominal main density, keeping prime-count loss separate. The identity includes exact central powers and roots.

The genuine \(j=0\) diagonal is \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:A.15}{(11)}. The separate artificial zero Gauss row is supported on sixth powers, added only at final enlargement and never amplified. Its count retains \(-s_0\) when \(s\mid u\). Their terminal bounds are \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.57}{(MF3.57)}--\protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.61}{(MF3.61)}.

For \(j\ne0\), retain whole genuine dyads before extracting complete \(u=C_2a,v=D_2b\). Put

\[
c_2=\log_Uq_{C_2},\quad d_2=\log_Uq_{D_2},\quad \bar b_2=(c_2+d_2)/2,
 \quad p_2=\log_Uq_{\operatorname{rad}(C_2D_2)},\quad
 g_2=\log_Uq_{(C_2,D_2)}.
\]

Apply \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:A.16}{(12)} only on \((a,b)=(ab,C_2D_2)=1\). At equal non-sixfold multiplicities split divided frequency into units/nonunits, widths \(t_2,V_2\), latter radical \(V_{\rm id}\). Then \protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:A.17}{(13)}--\protect\hyperlink{cf:eq:A.18}{(14)} give

\[
j=(C_2,D_2)V_{\rm id}h',\qquad
 |F(C_2,D_2;j)|\le U^{g_2-t_2}.
\]

Extend the partition scalar by zero before enlargement. All extracted primes puncture residual factors; plain fifth power plus slot prime retains zero-extended sixth power.

The nominal child widths are

\hypertarget{sr:child-width}{}

\label{sr:child-width}

\begin{equation*}
\begin{aligned}
 m'&=2a_0-K-g-g_2-V_2,\\
 q'&=R_1+E_1+t_2+V_2,\\
 M'&=m'+q'=M+J-g-g_2+t_2.
\end{aligned}\tag{138}
\end{equation*}

For the actual main ball, writing all its radius padding as \(\omega\), this is

\hypertarget{sr:main-child-width}{}

\label{sr:main-child-width}

\[
\qquad\text{(139)}
 M'=M-(c-d)_+-6a-\omega-g_2+t_2.
\]

Actual row width is \(m'+\delta_2\), \(0\le\delta_2<\xi\), and natural width \(M'+\delta_2\) retains \(q'\) despite cancellations. An upper norm below one is empty; otherwise use its nonnegative width. The fixed unit remains in the literal \(h'\) character.

The first projector divides both common parts; second support counts cost \(U^{p_2-s_0+o(1)}\). The full ledger is

\[
(K+g-a_0)-\ell+(p_2-s_0)+(g_2-t_2)+(a_0-\bar b_2).
\]

Subtract it from the individual first allowance, with the MAIN normalization just displayed. The exact inner allowance is

\hypertarget{sr:inner-allowance}{}

\label{sr:inner-allowance}

\[
\qquad\text{(140)}
 M'+\bar b_2-p_2+B_c+\ell .
\]

The MAIN allowance includes density \(a\) once. Here \(\bar b_2\) is paired average; after positive Cauchy, HIGH uses actual chosen-side \(c_2\) or \(d_2\) and corresponding frozen slots. Estimate sides separately before restoring the pair.

Remove residual coprimality by its complete Möbius sum and allocate each of its primes with

\[
\mathbf1_{p\mid\prod n_i}
   =\sum_{\varnothing\ne J}(-1)^{|J|+1}
                    \prod_{i\in J}\mathbf1_{p\mid n_i}.
\]

Selected plains are \(pl\) with unrestricted \(l\) and identical rectangle division; selected slots freeze, with distinct primes in one slot giving zero. Artificial primes gain no genuine frequency restrictions.

The extraction check is \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.67}{(MF3.67)}--\protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.69}{(MF3.69)}: for pre-total \(\alpha_j\), extracted central length \(r_j\), frozen ratio error \(e_j\) and pre/post negative lengths \(\pi_{0,j},\pi_j\), nonempty rectangles satisfy

\[
|e_j|\le\theta_N,\quad 0\le\pi_{0,j}\le\pi_j\le\theta_N,\quad
 r_{{\rm clip},j}=r_j-(\pi_j-\pi_{0,j})\ge0.
\]

On a lower-width-\(t_-\) divisor dyad, divisibility gives \(r_j+\omega_j\ge t_-\); disjoint entrance-slot subsets give \(|e_j+\omega_j|\le\theta_N\). Hence

\hypertarget{sr:extraction-payment}{}

\label{sr:extraction-payment}

\textbf{(141)} is \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.69}{(MF3.69)}, with these same actual extraction and clipping parameters.

It pays divisor count and both central scalars. Form positive norms before splitting/clipping; at most four rectangle pairs occur.

Define classes by restricting the exact second sum: zero frequency, discarded whole dyads, fixed-inducing rows and natural nonexceptional rows. Only the last receives buffered zero bins from the child's finite whole-character dictionary and conjugates. Threshold-crossing bins are LOW; higher bins HIGH. Keep pulled-back weights, units, both whole-product Mellin powers, natural holes and both projector allocations. Only then bound the completed positive norms.

This reduces MAIN to LOW, HIGH, fixed-inducing, diagonal and tail sources.
The global source proof applies the earlier global face to LOW, the actual
marked row count to HIGH, and (CA13) to centered terminals.

Fixed-inducing rows retain predicate, forced valuations, masks and positive physical-radius factor. After freezing slots, their plains have one character, puncture, norm power and \(D\). Equal products cancel leading lattice terms even after extraction, exactly as \protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.73}{(MF3.73)}--\protect\hyperlink{mf:eq:B3.80}{(MF3.80)}. Apply affine terminal reserves to this source; no enlarged zero predicate removes it.

\hypertarget{sr:uniformity}{}

\label{sr:uniformity}

\hypertarget{uniform-analytic-closure}{%
\paragraph{10.6 Uniform analytic closure}\label{uniform-analytic-closure}}

Choose the selected cell, mesh, strict scalar reserves and support padding before live scales, mesh before dictionary. Then choose one \(H_N\) over subsets/four profile pairs, \(\theta_N=H_N/\log U\), without per-slot error. Choose seminorm/height orders backwards through kernels and functional equations before labels/heights.

Integrate retained heights against \protect\hyperlink{sr:full-kernel}{(125)}'s full densities. For external cutoffs, fix height degree before tail order dominating it and raw counts. Discard genuine Poisson tails before separation using arguments \(\ge U^{\xi/4}\), \(|\mathfrak g|\le\sqrt{q_u}\), \(|F|\le q_uq_v\), obtaining arbitrary decay for independent profile heights.

Contract \protect\hyperlink{sr:physical-mass}{(121)}, \protect\hyperlink{sr:allocation-weight}{(128)}, \protect\hyperlink{sr:amplified-budget}{(136)}, \protect\hyperlink{sr:inner-allowance}{(140)} in order. This pays first labels and amplifier density once, preserving all zeros/masks, and completes the literal-energy bridge to MAIN LOW and HIGH.

\hypertarget{s60:chapter}{}

\label{s60:chapter}

\hypertarget{ma:definitions}{}

\label{ma:definitions}

\hypertarget{the-literal-row-and-its-normalization}{%
\paragraph{12.1 The literal row and its normalization}\label{the-literal-row-and-its-normalization}}

Use \(K=\mathbb Q(\sqrt{-3})\), \(\mathcal O=\mathbb Z[\omega]\), the fixed bad set and natural zero-extended sextic ideal character \(\chi_z\). Before scale, fix \(\eta,\eta_0\), ray modulus \(\mathfrak q\) and a unit-quotient subgroup containing all global units. For ray character \(\vartheta\) with Hecke lift \(\eta_\vartheta\), retain actual relative coefficient \(\nu_\vartheta=\eta_\vartheta\overline{\eta_0}\) with both zero sets. Let \(\mathcal R\) be the identity class.

For an element \(m\), an anchor \(A\), and a row \(z\), put

\[
c_z(I)=\eta(I)\chi_{m^6Az}(I),\qquad
 L_z(W,X;t)=\sum_{I\ne0}c_z(I)q_I^{it}W(q_I/X).
\]

All ideal characters mean their actual coefficients. For finite slots \(\mathcal J\), put \(P_j=U^{w_j}\) and

\hypertarget{ma:pools}{}

\label{ma:pools} \hypertarget{ma:slot}{}\label{ma:slot}

\[
\begin{aligned}
 \mathcal P_j&=\{\mathfrak p:\mathfrak p\text{ prime},
                    q_{\mathfrak p}\le2P_j,\ \mathfrak p\in\mathcal R\},
                                                        \qquad\text{(158)}\\
 b_j(I)&=\nu_{\vartheta_j}(I)
 W(q_I/P_j)(q_I/P_j)^{-\sigma_j+iv_j},\\
 A_j(z)&=P_j^{-1/2}\sum_{\mathfrak p\in\mathcal P_j}
                  b_j(\mathfrak p)c_z(\mathfrak p)q_{\mathfrak p}^{it}.
                                                        \qquad\text{(159)}
\end{aligned}
\]

Here \(\operatorname{supp}W\subset[1,2]\), so smaller pool primes contribute zero. Pools may overlap; retain fixed masks, relative coefficients and phases. The global source proof applies its exact collision decomposition to the full original marked pools.

For two annular profiles \(p_1,p_2\), the normalized positive row is

\hypertarget{ma:positive-row}{}

\label{ma:positive-row}

\[
\qquad\text{(160)}
 \mathscr R_z=(X_1X_2)^{-1/2}
       L_z(p_1,X_1;t)L_z(p_2,X_2;t)\prod_{j\in\mathcal J}A_j(z).
\]

Total central normalization is \((X_1X_2\prod_jP_j)^{-1/2}\), without prime-count or logarithmic factors.

In the initial energy \(m=m_{\rm bad}\) and \(A=1\). Its row restriction is

\[
\operatorname{keep}_{\eta,Q}(z):\quad z\ne0\quad\hbox{and}\quad
 z\text{ is not a fixed-inducing row for }(\eta,Q,m_{\rm bad},1).
\]

The exceptional family is the incumbent's fixed-inducing family. Put \(Q'=\operatorname{internalQ}(Q,\eta_0)\), retaining the relative anchor's fixed holes, and

\hypertarget{ma:energy}{}

\label{ma:energy}

\[
\qquad\text{(161)}
 \mathcal E_\eta(\Phi;p_1,p_2)
  =\sum_{z\in\mathcal O:\operatorname{keep}_{\eta,Q'}(z)}
          |\mathscr R_z|^2\Re\Phi(q_z/U).
\]

Independent plain heights are included by the unit-modulus norm powers in the common profile controls of Section 1.3; the single displayed \(t\) abbreviates that family. Retain all six units, sixth-power repetitions and root-relative holes.

Assume upper-supported Schwartz \(\Phi\), \(\Re\Phi\ge0\), \(\operatorname{supp}\Phi\subset(-\infty,b_\Phi]\), allowing positivity at zero. Let

\[
\mathcal D(\Phi)=16(1+\pi)^2
       \max_{0\le a\le2}\sup_x |x|^a|\Phi(x)|.
\]

For finite \(\mathcal F\subset\mathbb N^2\), \(\mathcal S_{\mathcal F}(p)\) is the product of the two maximum Schwartz-seminorm controls. Its square has the energy's quartic profile homogeneity; both supports lie in \([1/4,9/4]\).

\hypertarget{opening-the-source-without-erasing-its-coefficients}{}

\label{opening-the-source-without-erasing-its-coefficients}

\hypertarget{opening-the-source-without-erasing-its-coefficients-heading-2}{%
\paragraph{12.2 Opening the source without erasing its coefficients}}

The product in~\protect\hyperlink{ma:positive-row}{(160)} has a finite coefficient expansion

\[
a(I)=\sum_{I_1I_2\prod_j\mathfrak p_j=I}
       p_1(q_{I_1}/X_1)p_2(q_{I_2}/X_2)
       \prod_j b_j(\mathfrak p_j),
\]

Keep \(\eta(I)q_I^{it}\) outside \(a(I)\). Open its square into actual pairs \(I,J\); at common primes put \(i=v_{\mathfrak p}(I)\), \(j=v_{\mathfrak p}(J)\) and

\hypertarget{ma:net}{}

\label{ma:net}

\[
\qquad\text{(164)}
 k_{\mathfrak p}=(i+5j)\bmod6.
\]

For \(i,j>0\), split common primes into active \(k_{\mathfrak p}\ne0\) and principal \(k_{\mathfrak p}=0\), giving

\hypertarget{ma:common-mask}{}

\label{ma:common-mask}

\[
\qquad\text{(165)}
 \chi_z(I_{\rm com})\overline{\chi_z(J_{\rm com})}
 =\mathbf1_{(z,\mathfrak r_{\rm pr})=1}
       \prod_{\mathfrak p\ \mathrm{active}}\chi_{\mathfrak p}(z)^{k_{\mathfrak p}}.
\]

Exponent zero retains its principal character's zero at the prime.

Let \(a,b\) generate coprime residual ideals, \(r\) the active radical, and \(\rho=\prod\chi_{\mathfrak p}^{k_{\mathfrak p}}\). Expand the principal mask over \(e\mid\mathfrak r_{\rm pr}\); set \(K_e=K/q_e\). For the product \(f\) of both residual finite characters, \(\rho\) and fixed masks, choose a factoring modulus \(M_f\) and define

\[
G_f(h)=\sum_{x\bmod M_f}f(x)e_K(hx/M_f).
\]

With the Fourier convention of the arithmetic chapter, additive Poisson gives

\hypertarget{ma:first-transform}{}

\label{ma:first-transform}

\[
\qquad\text{(166)}
 \sum_z\chi_z(I)\overline{\chi_z(J)}\Phi(q_z/K)
 =\sum_{e\mid\mathfrak r_{\rm pr}}\mu(e)f(e)
       \frac{K_e}{q_{M_f}}\sum_hG_f(h)
                  \widehat\Phi(K_eq_h/q_{M_f}).
\]

\emph{Proof.} Substitute \(z=ex\), then apply additive Poisson in each class modulo \(M_f\). Fixed coefficients multiply both sides unchanged; \(f(e)=0\) when \(e\) shares an active/residual prime. Retain every frequency, including nonunits. This exact identity precedes estimates and applies to the multi-slot convolution.

Canonical Gauss factorization and sextic reciprocity give residual columns

\hypertarget{ma:first-columns}{}

\label{ma:first-columns}

\[
\qquad\text{(167)}
 \ell_e(n)=\chi_{er}((n))\rho(n),\qquad
 r_e(n)=\chi_{er}((n))\overline{\rho(n)}.
\]

The fixed phase \(\mathfrak R(a,b)\) has ray-character expansion mass \(\le256\); the separated product is

\[
\rho(e)\mathfrak R(a,b)\ell_e(a)\overline{r_e(b)}.
\]

Retain masks at \(er\), the fixed conductor and residual ideals. The reverse orientation exchanges columns and conjugates whole coefficients.

Apply the same finite transform to the allocated canonical residual columns. Their common ideals \(C_2,D_2\) have equal support; put

\[
g_2=\prod_{\mathfrak p\mid C_2D_2}
                  \varpi_{\mathfrak p}^{\min(v_{\mathfrak p}C_2,
                                                   v_{\mathfrak p}D_2)},
\]

Correlation vanishes unless frequency is \(g_2h\). Expand principal factors over subsets \(\mathcal U\) of common support, retaining holes at \(g_2\) and \(\prod_{\mathfrak p\in\mathcal U}\mathfrak p\). Local orthogonality saves at least \((\log_Uq_{C_2}+\log_Uq_{D_2})/3\) on nonzero support. Retain completed Gauss coefficients and the generator discrepancy as one of six unit labels.

The actual joint row is

\hypertarget{ma:joint-row}{}

\label{ma:joint-row}

\[
\qquad\text{(168)}
 w=u_0\epsilon\,e\,n_\pm\,g_2\,v_{\mathcal U}h.
\]

Here \(u_0\) is the parent anchor, \(\epsilon\) a unit, \(e\) the inactive projector, \(n_\pm\) the uncancelled numerator below, and \(g_2v_{\mathcal U}h\) the second frequency. Pre-partition valuations determine \(\mathcal U\). The whole row character is a fixed finite ray character times \(\chi_w\) with original moving puncture; reverse orientation conjugates it wholly. Zero selection uses that whole character.

\hypertarget{ma:allocations}{}

\label{ma:allocations}

\hypertarget{the-two-actual-allocations}{%
\paragraph{12.3 The two actual allocations}\label{the-two-actual-allocations}}

For tuple indices \(\mathcal J\sqcup\{1,2\}\), extraction of \(C\) uniquely splits coordinates \(B_iV_i\) with \(\prod B_i=C\), \(B_i\) supported on \(C\) and \(V_i\) outside it. A prime slot is live (\(B_j=1\)) or wholly frozen (\(V_j=1\)). Its frozen coefficient is

\hypertarget{ma:frozen}{}

\label{ma:frozen}

\[
\qquad\text{(169)}
 F_B(C,R)=\mathbf1_{(C,R)=1}
       \prod_{j:B_j\ne1}\nu_j(B_j)W_j(q_{B_j}/P_j).
\]

Absorb fixed-annulus powers into \(W_j\). Substitution dilates the plain scales to \(X_i/q_{B_i}\) and punctures at \(RC\); summing admissible allocations recovers the exact coefficient, including dependent slots.

\textbf{Lemma 12.2} (Width conserved by two allocations). \emph{\hypertarget{ma:width}{}\label{ma:width} Suppose there are at most \(N_0\) slots, the fixed annular lower endpoint is \(a_0>0\), and both successive frozen coefficients are nonzero. Let \(C_1,C_2\) be the ideals extracted in the two allocations. For every \(\zeta_w>0\), if \(w_{\min}>0\) and \(N_0w_{\min}\le\zeta_w/2\), then, for all sufficiently large \(U\), \hypertarget{ma:width-conservation}{}\label{ma:width-conservation}}

\[
\qquad\text{(170)}
 s-\zeta_w\le
      \log_Uq_{C_1}+\log_Uq_{C_2}
       +\sum_{\substack{j\text{ live after both allocations}\\w_j\ge w_{\min}}}w_j.
\]

\emph{The scale threshold is uniform in both allocations and their characters and heights.}

\emph{Proof.} Nonzero \protect\hyperlink{ma:frozen}{(169)} gives \(q_{B_j}\ge a_0P_j\), while their product divides \(q_C\). Thus frozen width is \(\le\log_Uq_C-N_0\log_U\min(1,a_0)\). Apply twice, identifying live slots with original indices. The total support loss \(-2N_0\log_U\min(1,a_0)\) is eventually \(\le\zeta_w/2\); remaining tiny widths cost \(\le N_0w_{\min}\le\zeta_w/2\). Remove zero allocations before maximization so the support implication applies. \(\square\)

If final projector extraction replaces plain scales \(X_i\) by \(X_i/U^{r_i}\) and formally removes logarithmic volume \(\Delta\), then

\hypertarget{ma:clipping}{}

\label{ma:clipping}

\par\noindent\resizebox{\linewidth}{!}{\begin{minipage}{\dimexpr\linewidth+75.99155pt\relax}\[
\qquad\text{(171)}
 \sum_{i=1}^2\max(0,\log_UX_i-r_i)
       +\sum_{j\ \mathrm{live}}w_j
 =\sum_{i=1}^2\max(0,\log_UX_i)+\sum_jw_j
       -\left[\Delta+\sum_i(-\log_UX_i)_+
                       -\sum_i(r_i-\log_UX_i)_+\right].
\]\end{minipage}}\par

Apply \(x_+=x+(-x)_+\) to raw volume conservation. The bracket is the actual clipped reduction, nonnegative on nonzero support; retain it even when a short plain scale is replaced by one.

\hypertarget{ma:numerator}{}

\label{ma:numerator}

\hypertarget{the-actual-first-numerator}{%
\paragraph{12.4 The actual first numerator}\label{the-actual-first-numerator}}

At active primes, reciprocity in \protect\hyperlink{ma:first-columns}{(167)} gives powers \(1+k_{\mathfrak p}\) or \(7-k_{\mathfrak p}\). Replace sixth powers by their coprimality masks and define

\[
e_+(i,j)=
 \begin{cases}0,&k=0\text{ or }k=5,\\1+k,&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}
 \qquad
 e_-(i,j)=
 \begin{cases}0,&k=0\text{ or }k=1,\\7-k,&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}
 \quad k=(i+5j)\bmod6.
\]

Put \(n_\pm=\prod_{\mathfrak p}\varpi_{\mathfrak p}^{e_\pm(i,j)}\). Retain the unit, projector \(e\), and phases \(\prod\mathfrak R(\varpi_{\mathfrak p},n)^k\) or \(\prod\mathfrak R(\varpi_{\mathfrak p},n)^{6-k}\); finite ray expansion makes these fixed characters, without extra moving width.

\textbf{Lemma 12.3} (Valuation budget). \emph{\hypertarget{ma:valuation}{}\label{ma:valuation} For every \(i,j\ge1\), \(4e_\pm(i,j)\le5(i+j)\). Hence, if \(C,D\) are the actual first common ideals, \hypertarget{ma:numerator-budget}{}\label{ma:numerator-budget}}

\[
\qquad\text{(172)}
 q_{(n_\pm)}^4\le(q_Cq_D)^5.
\]

\emph{Writing \(L_c=\log_Uq_{(n_\pm)}\), \(c=\log_Uq_C\), \(d=\log_Uq_D\), \(R\) for the active radical width and \(R_0\) for its cancelled part, \hypertarget{ma:coupled-gates}{}\label{ma:coupled-gates}}

\[
\qquad\text{(173)}
 4L_c\le5(c+d),\qquad L_c\le5(R-R_0),\qquad
 0\le R\le\min(c,d),\quad R_0\ge0.
\]

\emph{Proof.} Since \(e_\pm\le5\), only \(i+j<4\) needs checking: \((i,j)=(1,1),(1,2),(2,1)\) give \((e_+,e_-)=(0,0),(0,2),(2,0)\). Multiply \(4e_\pm\le5(i+j)\) over common primes; their parts divide \(C,D\), even without equal support. Surviving exponents are \(\le5\) and supported on the active radical minus its cancelled part, proving \protect\hyperlink{ma:coupled-gates}{(173)}. Both orientations are included without directional divisibility. \(\square\)


\paragraph{Multiplicity of actual joint rows.}
Fix a polynomial norm bound \(U^{B_0}\) on all four extracted common
ideals and on \((w)M_0\), with \(w\ne0\) as in (168), and missing
first radical \(NM_0\le U^m\). The number of actual labels giving
that row is \(O_{\epsilon,B_0}(U^{m+\epsilon})\).

Every first common prime occurs in the inactive projector, the numerator
\(n_\pm\), or \(M_0\), hence in \((w)M_0\). The second common
support is visible through \(g_2\mid w\); its valuations fix the
unit/nonunit partition, then (168) uniquely recovers the remaining
frequency. Thus, after \(M_0\) is fixed, four polynomially bounded
ideals supported on \((w)M_0\) determine all labels up to fixed unit
and ray choices.

For completeness, if \(NH\le U^B\), Rankin's bound gives, for fixed
\(s>0\), at most
\(U^{B_0s}\prod_{\mathfrak p\mid H}(1-(N\mathfrak p)^{-s})^{-1}\)
ideals of norm at most \(U^{B_0}\) supported on \(H\).
For every fixed \(\delta>0\), sufficiently large primes satisfy
\(-\log(1-(N\mathfrak p)^{-s})\le\delta\log N\mathfrak p\);
the remaining finite prime product is constant. The displayed product
is therefore \(O_{s,\delta}(U^{B\delta})\). Choose \(s\) and
\(\delta\) small enough for the four ideals together to cost
\(U^\epsilon\). Ideal counting gives \(O(U^m)\) choices for
\(M_0\). This proves the claimed multiplicity, with all valuations,
units, masks and both frequency partitions retained.
\hypertarget{the-compensated-probe-its-detector-and-the-return-to-ordinary-characters}{%
\subsubsection{14 The compensated probe, its detector, and the return to ordinary characters}\label{the-compensated-probe-its-detector-and-the-return-to-ordinary-characters}}

Use finite-order, trivial-infinite-type ideal \(L\)-functions over \(K=\mathbb Q(\sqrt{-3})\), with every integral modulus and finite conductor. Let \(\beta\) be the supremum of \(1/2\) and positive-half-plane zero real parts, excluding the principal pole. Primitive reduction below preserves this supremum; attainment is not assumed.

One compensated probe serves every stage: Collet's one-slot \(29/33\) construction and the later disjoint-slot signed inclusion--exclusion. Levy's weighted numerator changes its nonprincipal Poisson estimate while retaining the principal signal.

\hypertarget{dr:probe}{}

\label{dr:probe}

\hypertarget{the-actual-arithmetic-coefficients-and-the-two-representations}{%
\paragraph{14.1 The actual arithmetic coefficients and the two representations}\label{the-actual-arithmetic-coefficients-and-the-two-representations}}

Use the arithmetic chapter's primary generators, sextic symbols, angular \(\alpha\), ray phases \(G,R\), normalized Gauss sums \(\gamma_j\) and additive measure. All residue-character powers, including zero/sixth, vanish at nonunits; rows keep six units. Ideal columns avoid fixed finite \(S\), containing primes over six, primitive target conductor, ray moduli and a sufficiently large small-prime cutoff, chosen before scale/tuples/rows.

Let \(b_*\) generate the product of the prime ideals in \(S\). Choose a primitive residue character \(\xi\bmod b_*\), quadratic at odd residue characteristic and cubic at the prime of norm four. Put

\[
\tau=q_{b_*}^{-1/2}g_\xi(b_*,1),\qquad
 \Xi(A)=\xi(A)\chi_A(b_*),\qquad |\tau|=1.
\]

The finite identities proved in the arithmetic chapter give

\[
\gamma_2(c)^3=\mu(c)\alpha(c),\quad
 \gamma_1(c)\gamma_2(c)=\mu(c)\alpha(c)G(c),\quad
 \chi_b(a)=R(a,b)\chi_a(b)\quad ((a,b)=1).
\]

These fix coefficient phases. Set

\[
V_G(r)=\frac{e^{-(\log r)^2/4}}{2\sqrt\pi},\qquad
 \widehat V_G(t)=e^{t^2},
\]

and choose nonnegative, nonzero, smooth annular \(W_0,W_1\). For \(A=cn^3\), where \(c\) is squarefree and \(c,n\) may share primes, define

\hypertarget{dr:T}{}

\label{dr:T} \hypertarget{dr:I}{}\label{dr:I}

\par\noindent\resizebox{\linewidth}{!}{\begin{minipage}{\dimexpr\linewidth+43.33473pt\relax}\[
\begin{aligned}
 T_{m,a,\eta}(t)
 &=\sum_{c\ {\rm sf},n}
 \gamma_2(c)\overline{\alpha(A)G(A)}\eta(A)\Xi(A)^{-1}
 R(A,a)\chi_A(m)q_c^{-1/2-t}q_n^{-1-3t},\qquad\text{(187)}\\
 I_\eta(X,Y,Z)
 &=\frac1Y\sum_a
 \frac{W_1(q_a/Y)\chi_a(b_*)}
 {\tau\xi(a)\sqrt{q_{b_*}q_aX}}
 \sum_m\xi(m)q_a^{-1/2}g_{\chi_a}(a,-m)
 W_0\left(\frac{q_m}{q_{b_*}q_aX}\right)
 \frac1{2\pi i}\int_{(4)}Z^te^{t^2}T_{m,a,\eta}(t)\,dt.
 \qquad\text{(188)}
\end{aligned}
\]\end{minipage}}\par

The \(a\) columns include prime powers and full zero-extended imprimitive Gauss sums. Annuli make \(a,m\ne0\) finite; all other sums/integrals converge absolutely. Define \(I_{\eta;D}\) by inserting \(\mathbf1_{D\mid cn^3}\) on the whole theta index.

Choose finitely many distinct positive \(\ell_i\), \(P_i=Z^{\ell_i}\), and common smooth nonnegative nonzero \(w\) supported in \((1,2)\). Each slot uses all primes outside \(S\); distinct lengths make supports eventually disjoint. They may be as fine as any prescribed positive mesh with prescribed total \(\ell=\sum_i\ell_i\). Write \(w_i=w\) to index slots, \(p_J=\prod_{i\in J}p_i\), and define

\hypertarget{dr:comp}{}

\label{dr:comp}

\[
I_\eta^{\rm comp}(Z)=
 \sum_{(p_i)}\prod_iw_i(q_{p_i}/P_i)
 \sum_{J\subseteq\{1,\ldots,r_{\rm slot}\}}
 (-1)^{|J|}q_{p_J}^{-3/2}\overline{\eta(p_{J^c})}
 I_{\eta;p_{J^c}}(X/q_{p_J},Y/q_{p_J},Zq_{p_{J^c}}).
 \qquad\text{(189)}
\]

Weights are row-independent and the coefficient is \(\overline\eta\). One slot gives divisibility minus rescaled subtraction; retain all collisions with \(c,n\) and later rows.

For the physical representation, expand \(\overline G\) on its fixed ray group, partition \(a\) into classes \(\sigma\) for \(R(A,a)\), set \(Q_0=q_{b_*}XY\) and take \(\Omega=1\) on the product annuli. Mellin inversion of \(W_0\) expresses \protect\hyperlink{dr:I}{(188)} as a finite sum of

\hypertarget{dr:physical}{}

\label{dr:physical}

\[
\frac{Q_0^{-1/2}}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb R}\widehat W_0(iv)
 \sum_{m\ne0}\Omega(q_m/Q_0)\xi(m)(q_m/Q_0)^{-iv}
 A_{m,\sigma,v}(Y)B_{m,\sigma}(Z)\,dv,
 \qquad\text{(190)}
\]

where \(B\) is a completed theta sum, with precisely the coefficients

\[
\mathcal C_V(N;m,\nu)=\sum_{c\ {\rm sf},n}
 \frac{\overline{\alpha(cn^3)}\gamma_2(c)\nu(cn^3)\chi_{cn^3}(m)}
 {\sqrt{q_c}\,q_n}V(q_cq_n^3/N),
\]

and

\hypertarget{dr:A}{}

\label{dr:A}

\[
A_{m,\sigma,v}(Y)=Y^{-3/2}\sum_{a\in\sigma}
 \frac{\chi_a(b_*)}{\tau\xi(a)}W_1(q_a/Y)
 (q_a/Y)^{-1+iv}g_{\chi_a}(a,-m).
 \qquad\text{(191)}
\]

The identity \(q_m/(q_{b_*}q_aX)=(q_m/Q_0)/(q_a/Y)\) gives \(Y^{-3/2}\).

Let \(\widetilde W_0(|y|^2)\) be the planar Fourier transform of \(W_0(|z|^2)\) and \(\mathscr M(z)=\int_0^\infty\widetilde W_0(r)r^{z-1}\,dr\), holomorphic for \(\Re z>0\) with arbitrary vertical decay on compact positive substrips. Poisson gives

\hypertarget{dr:Poisson}{}

\label{dr:Poisson}

\[
\begin{aligned}
 I_\eta(X,Y,Z)=\frac1{(2\pi i)^3}
 \int_{(3)}\int_{(3)}\int_{(2)}
 &X^{1/2-z}Z^{s+z-1}Y^{w-1}e^{(s+z-1)^2}
 \mathscr M(z)\widehat W_1(w)\\[-2mm]
 &\quad\times\sum_u^{(6)}q_u^{-z}\overline{\xi(u)}
 \frac{\zeta_K^S(6z)L_K^S(w,\psi_u)}
 {L_K^S(s,\eta\overline{\psi_u})}H_{\eta,u}(s,w,z)
 \,dz\,dw\,ds.\qquad\text{(192)}
\end{aligned}
\]

Its finite convolution is

\[
\mathcal F(a,A,H)=\sum_{\substack{d\bmod a\\H\equiv b_*Ad\bmod a}}
 \chi_a(d)g_{\xi\chi_A}
 \left(b_*A,\frac{H-b_*Ad}{a}\right).
\]

Expanding both Gauss sums in the full transform modulo \(ab_*A\) gives \(q_a^{1/2}\mathcal F\). Planar dilation \(X/q_A\) leaves physical factor \(X^{1/2}/(\sqrt{q_{b_*}}q_A)\). The primitive factor at \(S\) kills frequencies meeting \(S\), including zero. Write others uniquely \(H=ur^6\), with primary \(r\) and the unit retained in \(u\); summing \(r\) gives \(\zeta_K^S(6z)\) without a factor six. Mellin inversion and \(s=t+1-z\) yield \protect\hyperlink{dr:Poisson}{(192)}. On its initial lines a majorant is

\[
C_S\sum_{u,c,n,a,r}q_u^{-2}q_c^{-5/2}q_n^{-7}q_a^{-2}q_r^{-12},
\]

times rapidly decreasing heights, justifying absolute interchange. CRT gives \(g_{\xi\chi_A}(b_*A,K)=\xi(A)\chi_A(b_*)g_\xi(b_*,K)g_{\chi_A}(A,K)\); \(\Xi(A)^{-1}\) and \(\chi_a(b_*)/(\tau\xi(a))\) cancel fixed-prime factors, leaving \(\overline{\xi(u)}\). The displayed phase identities cancel cross-prime terms, including shared primes of \(a,A\).

\hypertarget{dr:local}{}

\label{dr:local}

\hypertarget{the-local-table-including-all-shared-primes}{%
\paragraph{14.2 The local table, including all shared primes}\label{the-local-table-including-all-shared-primes}}

The complete local table for \protect\hyperlink{dr:Poisson}{(192)} and numerator averaging uses, at \(p\notin S\),

\[
\begin{gathered}
 Q=q_p,\quad j=v_p(u)\in\{0,\ldots,5\},\quad
 \rho=\chi_p(u/p^j),\quad
 \mathfrak a=\overline{\alpha(p)}^3\eta(p)^3,\quad
 \mathfrak b=\overline{\alpha(p)}^2\eta(p)^2\overline{\chi_p(4)},
 \quad \mathfrak b^3=\mathfrak a^2,\\
 V=Q^{-6z},\quad R_p=\mathfrak a^2Q^{4-6s-6z},\quad
 W^\circ=\rho Q^{-w},\quad W=\chi_p(u)Q^{-w},\quad
 D=\eta(p)\overline{\chi_p(u)}Q^{-s}.
\end{gathered}
\]

Thus \(D=W=0\) for \(j>0\). Set

\hypertarget{dr:Euler-table}{}

\label{dr:Euler-table}

\[
\begin{aligned}
 P_p^*&=\frac1{1-R_p}
 \left\{\frac{R_p(1-Q^{-1})-\eta(p)(Q-1)Q^{-s-w}
 V^{\mathbf1_{j\le1}}}{1-V}+J_j\right\},\\
 P_p&=(1-V)^{-1}+\mathbf1_{j=0}\frac{W^\circ}{1-W^\circ}+P_p^*,
 \qquad H_p=\frac{P_p(1-V)(1-W)}{1-D},
 \quad H_{\eta,u}=\prod_{p\notin S}H_p,
 \qquad\text{(193)}
\end{aligned}
\]

where

\[
\begin{array}{c|l}
 j&J_j\\ \hline
0&-\eta(p)\rho^{-1}Q^{-s}+W^\circ R_p\\
1&\eta(p)Q^{-s-w}\\
2&\mathfrak a\rho^{-3}Q^{3/2-3s}\\
3&-\eta(p)\mathfrak b\rho^{-2}Q^{2-3s-w}
                  +\mathfrak b^2\rho^{-4}Q^{2-4s}\\
4&-\eta(p)\mathfrak a\rho^{-3}Q^{5/2-4s-w}\\
5&-\mathfrak a^2Q^{3-6s}.
\end{array}
\]

For \(t=e+3l\), \(e\in\{0,1\}\), the local convolution is

\[
C_p(t,k,j')=\sum_{\substack{d\bmod p^k\\p^{j'}\equiv p^td\bmod p^k}}
 \chi_p(d)^k g_{\chi_p^t}
 \left(p^t,\frac{p^{j'}-p^td}{p^k}\right).
\]

Lifting a prime Gauss sum gives

\[
g_{\chi_p^h}(p^t,K)=
 \begin{cases}Q^{t-1}g_{\chi_p^h}(p,K/p^{t-1}),&p^{t-1}\mid K,\\
 0,&p^{t-1}\nmid K.
 \end{cases}
\]

For \(t=0\) it is one at \(k=0\), \(\mathbf1_{j'=0}\) at \(k>0\). For \(t>0,k=0\) it is the source Gauss sum; for \(k=1,j'\ge1\) it is \(\chi_p(-1)^{-1}g_{\chi_p}(p,1)g_{\chi_p^{t-1}}(p^t,p^{j'-1})\), while \(j'=0\) has no solution. For \(k\ge2\) the last lift \(d=d_0+p^{k-1}h\) sums a nontrivial additive character and vanishes. Surviving \(t>0\) valuations are

\[
\begin{array}{c|cc}
 t&k=0&k=1\\ \hline
 6r+6&j'\ge t-1&j'=t\\
 6r+1&j'=t-1&j'\ge t\\
 6r+3&j'=t-1&j'=t\\
 6r+4&j'=t-1&j'=t.
\end{array}
\]

After CRT phase cancellation, with \(j'=j+6m\) and \(\omega_p=\chi_p(-1)\), the summand is

\[
(-1)^e\gamma_1(p)^{-e}\eta(p)^e
 (\mathfrak a\overline{\gamma_3(p)})^l
 \rho^{k-t}\omega_p^{tk-el-l(l-1)/2}C_p(t,k,j+6m)
 Q^{-(s+1/2)e-(1+3s)l-wk-6zm}.
\]

The first two strict tails sum to \(R_p(1-Q^{-1})/((1-R_p)(1-V))\) and \(-\eta(p)(Q-1)Q^{-s-w}V^{\mathbf1_{j\le1}}/((1-R_p)(1-V))\). Finite Gauss identities give equality terms \(J_j/(1-R_p)\); \(t=0\) gives the initial terms of \(P_p\). This covers every valuation.

Put \(c_p=\overline{\eta(p)}Q^s\), \(h_p=Q^{-w}\), and

\hypertarget{dr:G}{}

\label{dr:G}

\[
G_p=\frac{(c_p-h_p)(1-V)(1-W)P_p^*-h_p(1-VW)}{1-D}.
 \qquad\text{(194)}
\]

The local effect of the two cases in \protect\hyperlink{dr:comp}{(189)} is the identity

\[
\overline{\eta(p)}Q^{s+z-1}P_p^*-Q^{z-w-1}P_p
 =\frac{(1-D)Q^{z-1}G_p}{(1-V)(1-W)}.
\]

Consequently its exact Poisson correction is

\hypertarget{dr:Hcomp}{}

\label{dr:Hcomp}

\[
\mathcal H_{\eta,u,Z}=
 \sum_{(p_i)}\prod_iw_i(Q_i/P_i)Q_i^{z-1}G_{p_i}
 \prod_{p\notin S\cup\{p_i\}}H_p.
 \qquad\text{(195)}
\]

For \(p\nmid u\) put \(v=\chi_p(u)\), \(E_p=P_p^*+D\). Substituting \(c_pD=v^{-1}\) gives the quotient-free identity

\hypertarget{dr:Delta}{}

\label{dr:Delta}

\[
G_p+v^{-1}H_p=\frac{(1-V)(1-W)}{1-D}
 \left[(v^{-1}-h_p)\left(\frac V{1-V}-D\right)
 +(c_p+v^{-1}-h_p)E_p\right].\qquad\text{(196)}
\]

Define \(\Delta_p=G_p+\overline{\psi_u(p)}H_p\) also at row primes. Expanding \(G_p=-\overline{\psi_u(p)}H_p+\Delta_p\) gives all main/error choices in \protect\hyperlink{dr:Hcomp}{(195)}, with main slot exactly \(-\sum_pw_i(Q/P_i)Q^{z-1}\overline{\psi_u(p)}\), including zero character values.

The local table also proves normal convergence and \(H_{\eta,u}\ll q_u^\epsilon\) on compact real boxes in

\hypertarget{dr:box}{}

\label{dr:box}

\[
\Re s\ge51/100,\quad\Re z\ge17/50,\quad
 \Re w\ge-1/100,\quad\Re(s+w)\ge1+\epsilon_1.
 \qquad\text{(197)}
\]

Unramified leading defects \(Q^{-s-w}\) and \(Q^{4-6s-6z}\) are \(\le Q^{-1-\epsilon_1}\) and \(Q^{-11/10}\); all others have summable margins. Row-prime defects are negative powers, giving \(q_u^\epsilon\) after a fixed cutoff; \(1-W\) cancels. For \(u=1\) also allow \(\Re s\ge87/100\), \(\Re w\ge3/4\), \(\Re z\ge1/8\). Substituting the six local terms in \protect\hyperlink{dr:Hcomp}{(195)} gives \(\ll q_u^\epsilon Z^{(17/50)\ell}\) on \((\beta+e,1/2,17/50)\) and \(\ll q_u^\epsilon Z^{v\ell}\) on \((2,2,v)\), \(v>2\): selected off-row factors are bounded; the first line's exceptional \(O(Q^{1/2})\) occurs only at divisor-many primes. Intervening fixed boxes have fixed polynomial height/scale bounds.

\hypertarget{dr:Gram}{}

\label{dr:Gram}

\hypertarget{the-signed-gram-calculation-and-the-physical-bound}{%
\paragraph{14.3 The signed Gram calculation and the physical bound}\label{the-signed-gram-calculation-and-the-physical-bound}}

\textbf{Lemma 14.1} (Signed physical Gram estimate). \emph{\hypertarget{dr:Gram-lemma}{}\label{dr:Gram-lemma} For \protect\hyperlink{dr:A}{(191)}, with \(P_a=Y^2/Q_0\ge1\),}

\[
\sum_{q_m\ll Q_0}|A_{m,\sigma,v}(Y)|^2
 \ll (1+|v|)^J\frac{Q_0}{Y}
       \left(1+P_a^{1/6}+\frac{P_a^2}{Y}\right)Z^\epsilon.
\]

\emph{This assertion uses the displayed fixed ray coefficients.}

\emph{Proof.} Expand the signed square. Arithmetic Fourier correlation has \(F(u,v;0)=\mathbf1_{u=v}\phi(u)\) and, for \(u=Cn_1,v=Cn_2\), \((n_1,n_2)=1\),

\[
F(Cn_1,Cn_2;Ck)=\chi_{n_1}(k)\overline{\chi_{n_2}(-k)}
 R(n_1,n_2)L_C(n_1,n_2;k),\qquad |L_C|\le q_C.
\]

Row Poisson contributes \(Q_0/(q_uq_v)\), the Gauss pair \(q_uq_vF\), giving prefactor \(Q_0/Y^3\); zero frequency costs \(O(Q_0/Y)\).

For nonzero frequencies extend the coefficient to residual pairs, setting it zero when a \(C\)-prime divides both, then insert full Möbius coprimality. On divisor \(d\), write \(n_i=dm_i\); natural zeros force \((d,Ck)=1\). The extension cancels in the complete divisor sum. After finite ray separation the period norm is \(\ll q_Cq_k\); its complete mean vanishes unless the sixth-free part of \(k\) is supported on \(C,S\), since every other prime gives a nonprincipal complete character sum.

On \(q_C\asymp C_0,q_d\asymp D_0,q_Cq_k\asymp RP_a\), put \(N=Y/(C_0D_0)\). Two-column lattice Poisson bounds zero-mean sums by

\[
(1+|v|)^J R^{-B}C_0N^2
           \min\{1,(RP_a/N)^A\}.
\]

Below period use counting; above period integrate all nonzero dual frequencies by parts. There are \(O(D_0RP_a)\) choices of \(C,d,k\), and the remaining dyads use

\[
\sum_{i,j\ge0}2^{-i-j}\min\{1,(2^{i+j}/\Lambda)^A\}
 \ll_A\frac{1+\log(2+\Lambda)}\Lambda,\quad
 \Lambda=Y/(RP_a),\quad A>1.
\]

Group by \(i+j=n\) and split \((n+1)2^{-n}\) at \(2^n=\Lambda\). Including \(Q_0/Y^3\) gives \((Q_0/Y)P_a^2/Y\); large \(B\) sums \(R\).

For nonzero mean, write \(k=er^6\) with sixth-free \(e\) supported on \(C,S\). There are divisor-many \(e\) and \(O(Z^\epsilon(RP_a/C_0)^{1/6})\) roots. Absolute column volume gives \((Q_0/Y)P_a^{1/6}\) with summable weights \(C_0^{-1/6+\epsilon}D_0^{-1+\epsilon}\). Add zero frequency; finitely many profile derivatives give \((1+|v|)^J\). \(\square\)

We apply the completed marked energy of Lemma~\protect\hyperlink{mf:marked-energy}{7.1} with its following exact interface. For the displayed \(\mathcal C_V\), product-form row-independent prime marks of total width \(z\), completed length \(Z^N\), and row range \(Z^M\),

\hypertarget{dr:marked-energy-interface}{}

\label{dr:marked-energy-interface}

\[
M+2z\le N\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 \sum_{0<q_m\ll Z^M}|\mathcal T(m)|^2
 \ll Z^{M+(N+2z-2M)_+/4+\epsilon}.
 \qquad\text{(198)}
\]

Lemma \protect\hyperlink{mf:marked-energy}{7.1} includes both collision masks, all cusps, joint profiles and full kernel tails for these multiple slots.

Let \(X=Z^x,Y=Z^y,M=x+y\) and assume

\[
0<\ell<x\le y,\qquad M+\ell\le1,\qquad y-2x+\ell<0.
\]

Cauchy in \protect\hyperlink{dr:physical}{(190)}, the Gram lemma and \protect\hyperlink{dr:marked-energy-interface}{(198)} give

\hypertarget{dr:physical-bound}{}

\label{dr:physical-bound}

\[
|I_\eta^{\rm comp}(Z)|
 \ll Z^{x/2+(y-x)/12+(1+3\ell-2M)_+/8+\epsilon}.
 \qquad\text{(199)}
\]

For subset \(J\) of width \(d\), the rescaled row/mark/completed exponents are \(M'=M-2d\), \(z'=\ell-d\), \(N'=1+\ell-d\), satisfying \(M'+2z'\le N'\). Outside factor, tuple mass and energy square root add

\[
-d+\frac{(1+3\ell-2M+d)_+}{8}.
\]

Its slopes \(-1,-7/8\) make \(d=0\) maximal. The Gram condition \(P_a^2/(Y/q_{p_J})\ll1\) follows from \(y-2x+\ell<0\). Integrate common Mellin profiles and sum finite rays to obtain \protect\hyperlink{dr:physical-bound}{(199)} for every subset.

\hypertarget{dr:signal}{}

\label{dr:signal}

\hypertarget{the-unique-principal-signal-and-its-nonvanishing-numerator}{%
\paragraph{14.4 The unique principal signal and its nonvanishing numerator}\label{the-unique-principal-signal-and-its-nonvanishing-numerator}}

Only \(u=1\) is principal in \protect\hyperlink{dr:Poisson}{(192)}: a nonunit sixth-free row has a good prime of valuation \(1,\ldots,5\), hence nontrivial tame Kummer inertia. For unit \(u\ne1\), CRT gives primary \(n\equiv-2-3\omega\pmod{36\mathcal O}\) and \(n\equiv1\) at other fixed primes. Then \(q_n\equiv7\pmod{36}\) and \(\chi_n(u)=u^{(q_n-1)/6}=u\ne1\). Keep all five units in the remainder.

Let \(\rho_S=\operatorname{Res}_{s=1}\zeta_K^S(s)>0\). At \(w=1,z=1/6\) the table simplifies, with \(t=Q^{-1}\), to

\hypertarget{dr:principal-local}{}

\label{dr:principal-local}

\[
H_{\eta,p}(s)=\frac{1-t^2}{1-R_p}
 \left(1+\frac{t(D-R_p)}{1-D}\right),\qquad
 D=\eta(p)Q^{-s},\quad
 R_p=\overline{\alpha(p)}^6\eta(p)^6Q^{3-6s}.
 \qquad\text{(200)}
\]

In particular, for any fixed \(b>2/3\),

\[
|H_{\eta,p}(s)-1|\ll_b
 Q^{-2}+Q^{-1-b}+Q^{3-6b}+Q^{2-6b}\qquad(\Re s\ge b).
\]

These powers are prime summable. Enlarge the fixed cutoff until their majorant sum is \(\le\log(3/2)\); normal convergence and \(\prod(1+a_p)\le e^{\sum a_p}\) give

\hypertarget{dr:H-nonzero}{}

\label{dr:H-nonzero}

\[
H_\eta(s)=\prod_{p\notin S}H_{\eta,p}(s),\qquad
 |H_\eta(s)-1|\le\tfrac12\quad (\Re s\ge b).
 \qquad\text{(201)}
\]

Thus \(H_\eta\) is holomorphic and nonzero uniformly in height; \(b=87/100\) suffices for all established endpoints here.

The positive residue scalar is

\[
c_S=\frac{\rho_S^2}{6}\widehat W_1(1)\mathscr M(1/6)>0.
\]

Indeed, Fourier transformation of the radial Riesz kernel, first written as a positive integral of Gaussians, gives for \(0<\sigma<1\)

\[
\mathscr M(\sigma)=\left(\frac{2\pi}{\sqrt3}\right)^{1-2\sigma}
 \frac{\Gamma(\sigma)}{\Gamma(1-\sigma)}
 \int_0^\infty W_0(r)r^{-\sigma}\,dr>0.
\]

Annular support/Schwartz decay justify interchange; \(1/6\) in \(c_S\) is the residue scaling of \(\zeta_K^S(6z)\).

On this residue locus \protect\hyperlink{dr:Delta}{(196)} gives \(G_p/H_p=-1+O(Q^{-b})\). Define the real nonzero normalizer

\[
A(Z)=(-1)^{r_{\rm slot}}Z^{-\ell/6}
       \prod_i\sum_pw_i(Q/P_i)Q^{-5/6}.
\]

The fixed-field prime ideal theorem gives \(|A(Z)|\asymp(\log Z)^{-r_{\rm slot}}\). Multiplying over fixed disjoint slots gives

\hypertarget{dr:prime-residue}{}

\label{dr:prime-residue}

\[
\mathcal H_{\eta,1,Z}(s,1,1/6)
 =H_\eta(s)Z^{\ell/6}A(Z)(1+O(Z^{-\mu_P})),
 \quad 0<\mu_P<b\min_i\ell_i,\quad\Re s\ge b.
 \qquad\text{(202)}
\]

Put

\hypertarget{dr:J-f}{}

\label{dr:J-f}

\par\noindent\resizebox{\linewidth}{!}{\begin{minipage}{\dimexpr\linewidth+16.83815pt\relax}\[
h=1+\ell-x,\quad k=\tfrac56-\tfrac x3-\tfrac\ell6,
 \qquad J_\eta(Z)=\frac{I_\eta^{\rm comp}(Z)}{c_S A(Z)},
 \quad
 f_\eta(Z)=\frac1{2\pi i}\int_{(2)}Z^{s-k}
 e^{(s-5/6)^2}\frac{H_\eta(s)}{L_K^S(s,\eta)}\,ds.
 \qquad\text{(203)}
\]\end{minipage}}\par

The residue power is \(X^{1/3}Z^{s-5/6}Z^{\ell/6}=Z^{s-k}\). Equation \protect\hyperlink{dr:prime-residue}{(202)} therefore identifies \(f_\eta\) up to power-small error: the compensator gives \(Z^{\ell/6}\) and \(A(Z)\) has logarithmic size.

\hypertarget{dr:detection}{}

\label{dr:detection}

\hypertarget{buffered-zero-bins-and-two-witnesses-at-the-same-zero}{%
\paragraph{14.5 Buffered zero bins and two witnesses at the same zero}\label{buffered-zero-bins-and-two-witnesses-at-the-same-zero}}

For fixed finite-ray \(\nu\), let \(\varphi_u=\nu\psi_u^{\pm1}\) with its natural zeros, and set

\[
M_u(D;W)=D^{-1/2}\sum_n\mu(n)\varphi_u(n)W(q_n/D),\qquad
 S_u(N;W)=N^{-1/2}\sum_n\varphi_u(n)W(q_n/N).
\]

Preceding moments have common annular controls for real powers, scale derivatives and independent polynomial heights. In particular,

\hypertarget{dr:unmarked}{}

\label{dr:unmarked}

\[
\sum_{q_u\asymp U}^{(6)}|M_u(U^r;W)|^2
 \ll U^{\max\{1,(1+5r)/6\}+\epsilon},\qquad
 \sum_{q_u\asymp U}^{(6),\varphi_u^*\ne1}|S_u(U^m;W)|^4
 \ll U^{1+\epsilon}.
 \qquad\text{(204)}
\]

The long inverse exponent follows from Lemma \protect\hyperlink{cf:inverse-moment}{4.1} by the exact amplification

\[
M_u(D;W)=\sum_{d\mid\operatorname{rad}(a)}
 \mu(d)\varphi_u(d)q_d^{-1/2}M_{ua^6}(D/q_d;W).
\]

This separates squarefree column primes dividing \(a\), retaining shared-prime zeros, with coefficient mass \(q_a^\epsilon\). Log-scale differentiation replaces \(W\) by \(-W/2-yW'\). Sobolev and the inverse moment bound \(\sup_{0<E\le D}\) on balls \(H\ge D^{1+\vartheta}\). Average over primary \(q_a\le(H/U)^{1/6}\) for \(H=\max(2U,D^{1+\vartheta})\); valuations modulo six make \((u,a)\mapsto ua^6\) injective. Its \(\gg_S(H/U)^{1/6}\) preimages give \(U^{1/6}H^{5/6}(UD)^\epsilon\), hence \protect\hyperlink{dr:unmarked}{(204)}. Row \(u=1\) gives smooth Möbius sums \(\ll D^{11/12+\epsilon}\); Mellin inversion with a test nonzero at a proposed zero yields preliminary \(\beta\le11/12\), before \(7/8\) or marked plain moments.

Fix finite conjugation-stable \(\Theta\) containing all target numerator/denominator/ray/phase twists. For nonunit sixth-free \(u\), \(\mathcal X(u)=\{\nu\psi_u^{\pm1}:\nu\in\Theta\}\) is nonprincipal of conductor \(O_S(q_u)\) with redundant zeros. Put \(a_*=51/100\), small \(e>0\), \(I=\lceil1/e\rceil+2\). Let \(B_j(u)\) maximize \(a_*\) and zero real parts of \(\mathcal X(u)\) up to height \(3^jT\). Some \(i<I\) has \(B_{i+1}-B_i\le e\); round \(B_i\) down on the \(e\) mesh:

\hypertarget{dr:bins}{}

\label{dr:bins}

\[
a\le B_i<a+e,\qquad B_{i+1}<a+2e.\qquad\text{(205)}
\]

Labels \((i,a)\) are finite. Above the floor there is one actual zero \(\rho=\sigma+i\gamma\), \(a\le\sigma<a+e\), \(|\gamma|\le3^iT\), supplying both witnesses. Put \(\delta=2a-1\).

We record the analytic consequence of the buffer. The ordinary Hecke functional equation in this normalization is

\[
\Lambda(s,\varphi)=(3q_{\mathfrak f})^{s/2}(2\pi)^{-s}
 \Gamma(s)L_K(s,\varphi),\qquad
 \Lambda(s,\varphi)=\epsilon_\varphi\Lambda(1-s,\bar\varphi),
 \quad |\epsilon_\varphi|=1.
\]

Euler convergence and the functional equation give polynomial conductor/height growth. Continue the Euler logarithm on a zero-free disk about \(2+it\). Borel--Carathéodory bounds it by \(O_e(\log(2q_{\mathfrak f}(3+|t|)^2))\) on a smaller disk; Euler convergence bounds it on radius \(49/100\). Three circles with another buffer reduces the logarithm to a power \(<1\). Exponentiating either sign gives

\hypertarget{dr:buffered}{}

\label{dr:buffered}

\[
|L_K(s,\varphi)|+|L_K(s,\varphi)^{-1}|
 \ll_{e,\epsilon}\{2q_{\mathfrak f}(3+|t|)^2\}^{\epsilon}.
 \qquad\text{(206)}
\]

Use radii \(2-a-2e,2-a-4e,2-a-6e\); Cauchy gives logarithmic derivatives. For principal characters apply this to \((s-1)\zeta_K(s)/(s+1)\); to the right of \(\beta+e\) it holds at all heights. Deleted factors cost \(U^\epsilon\) on fixed positive half-planes and \(U^{v+\epsilon}\) on \(\Re s\ge-v\), by summing \(O(q_p^{-\sigma})\) beyond a fixed cutoff and the divisor bound.

Mellin inversion and reflection now give

\hypertarget{dr:saturation-upper}{}

\label{dr:saturation-upper}

\[
|M_u(U^r)|^2\ll U^{\delta r+\epsilon_0},\qquad
 |S_u(U^m)|^2\ll U^{\delta\min(m,1-m)+\epsilon_0}.
 \qquad\text{(207)}
\]

For prime tests apply \(-L'/L\), divide by \(\log q_p\) and subtract prime powers costing \(O(P^{1/2+\epsilon})\) before normalization:

\hypertarget{dr:prime-upper}{}

\label{dr:prime-upper}

\[
\left|P^{-1/2}\sum_p\overline{\psi_u(p)}w(q_p/P)
 (q_p/P)^{it}\right|
 \ll P^{\delta/2+O(e)+\epsilon}U^\epsilon(1+T)^A.
 \qquad\text{(208)}
\]

The buffer \protect\hyperlink{dr:bins}{(205)} covers the witness neighborhood; distinct norm twists keep independent heights.

\textbf{Lemma 14.2} (Same-zero witnesses). \emph{\hypertarget{dr:same-zero}{}\label{dr:same-zero} Fix \(t_0\in[1,3/2]\) for a nonfloor bin before selecting its witnesses. Each row has a presentation, a real power \(\sigma\in[a,a+e)\), a common height and dyadic lengths \(U^r,U^m\) such that}

\[
r\le t_0+o(1),\quad r+m\ge t_0-o(1),\quad
 0\le m\le\tfrac12+O(\epsilon_0),
\]

\[
|M_u(U^r)|^2\gg U^{\delta r-O(\epsilon_0)},\qquad
 |S_u(U^m)|^2\gg U^{\delta m-O(\epsilon_0)}.
\]

\emph{After fixing presentations and dyads, the base profiles are common.}

\emph{Proof.} Choose \(V_\le=1\) on \([0,1]\), zero on \([2,\infty)\), and put \(C_\varphi(s)=\sum_l\mu(l)\varphi(l)V_\le(q_l/U^{t_0})q_l^{-s}\). At the actual zero \(\rho\) shift

\[
\frac1{2\pi i}\int_{(2)}U^{40z}\Gamma(z)
 L_K^S(\rho+z,\varphi)C_\varphi(\rho+z)\,dz
\]

to \(\Re z=-1/4\). The zero residue vanishes; polynomial growth, gamma decay and small height exponent make the new integral \(o(1)\), since \(U^{-10}\) dominates ideal counting. On the absolute line the unit contributes \(1+o(1)\) and \(\mu*1=\mathbf1_1\) cancels \(1<q_{lk}\le U^{t_0}\). The remaining sum has magnitude \(1+o(1)\) and product norm \(\gg U^{t_0}\). Truncate smoothly at \(U^{41}\) with error \(o(1)\), then dyadically partition and Fourier-separate the product weight to select one pair/frequency with

\[
|M_u(U^r)S_u(U^m)|^2
 \gg U^{\delta(r+m)-O(\epsilon_0)}.
\]

Both factors inherit the same character, real power and height from the convolution at \(\rho\). Equation \protect\hyperlink{dr:saturation-upper}{(207)} and \(\delta\ge1/50\) force \(m\le1/2+O(\epsilon_0)\); division by each upper bound gives both lower bounds. The inverse cutoff \(V_\le(U^ry/U^{t_0})\) has uniform annular transition derivatives. Dyads and rapidly decaying separating-frequency tails fit the loss. \(\square\)

\hypertarget{dr:count}{}

\label{dr:count}

\hypertarget{the-weighted-numerator-and-its-conductor-allocation}{%
\paragraph{14.7 The weighted numerator and its conductor allocation}\label{the-weighted-numerator-and-its-conductor-allocation}}

For each main/error term of \protect\hyperlink{dr:Hcomp}{(195)}, define its actual numerator by full \(w\) inversion:

\hypertarget{dr:N}{}

\label{dr:N}

\[
\mathcal N_u(Y;s,z)=\frac1{2\pi i}\int_{(3)}Y^{w-1}
 \widehat W_1(w)L_K^S(w,\psi_u)
 \mathcal H^{\rm term}_{\eta,u,Z}(s,w,z)\,dw.
 \qquad\text{(216)}
\]

Bound the \(w\)-independent denominator afterwards. The following coefficient calculation admits a plain moment.

Write \(t=Q^{-w}\). Away from the row let \(v=\chi_p(u)\) and \(d_p=\eta(p)v^{-1}Q^{-s}\). Direct expansion of \protect\hyperlink{dr:Euler-table}{(193)} gives \(H_p=H_{p,0}+H_{p,1}t+H_{p,2}t^2\), where

\hypertarget{dr:Hcoeff}{}

\label{dr:Hcoeff}

\[
\begin{aligned}
 H_{p,0}&=\frac{1-R_p/Q-d_p(1-V)}{(1-R_p)(1-d_p)},\\
 H_{p,1}&=\frac{\eta(p)Q^{-s}(1-QV)-vV+vR_p/Q}
 {(1-R_p)(1-d_p)},\qquad\text{(217)}\\
 H_{p,2}&=\frac{v\eta(p)(Q-1)Q^{-s}V-v^2R_p(1-V)}
 {(1-R_p)(1-d_p)}.
\end{aligned}
\]

On \(\Re s=a+16e,\Re z=z_0=17/50\) these imply

\hypertarget{dr:Hcoeff-bound}{}

\label{dr:Hcoeff-bound}

\[
|H_{p,0}-1|+\sum_{k=1}^2|H_{p,k}|Q^{k(a-1)}
 \ll Q^{-1-\epsilon_e}.
 \qquad\text{(218)}
\]

The largest linear exponent is \(-1-16e\); other candidates \(a-1-6z_0,2-4a-6z_0,4-6a-6z_0,-a-6z_0\) are \(<-1\) for \(a\ge51/100\). For quotient-free error estimates write \(E_p=E_0+E_1t\) with

\[
E_0=\frac{R_p}{1-R_p}\left(\frac{1-Q^{-1}}{1-V}-d_p\right),\qquad
 E_1=\frac{-\eta(p)(Q-1)Q^{-s}V/(1-V)+vR_p}{1-R_p}.
\]

Insertion in \protect\hyperlink{dr:Delta}{(196)} proves

\hypertarget{dr:Delta-bound}{}

\label{dr:Delta-bound}

\[
\sum_{k=0}^3|\Delta_{p,k}|Q^{k(a-1)}\ll Q^{-a}.
 \qquad\text{(219)}
\]

The constant is \(\ll Q^{-a}+Q^{-6z_0}+Q^{4-5a-6z_0}\ll Q^{-a}\). Each additional \(t\) weighs \(Q^{a-1}<1\), and \(1-6z_0<-1\).

At a row prime \(p^j\Vert u\), \(1\le j\le5\), write \(J_j=A_j+B_jt\). For \(j\ge2\) the exact coefficients are

\hypertarget{dr:rowcoeff}{}

\label{dr:rowcoeff}

\par\noindent\resizebox{\linewidth}{!}{\begin{minipage}{\dimexpr\linewidth+6.42113pt\relax}\[
\begin{aligned}
 H_0&=\frac{1-R_p/Q+(1-V)A_j}{1-R_p},&
 H_1&=\frac{-\eta(p)(Q-1)Q^{-s}+(1-V)B_j}{1-R_p},\\
 G_0&=\frac{c_p\{R_p(1-Q^{-1})+(1-V)A_j\}}{1-R_p},&
 G_1&=\frac{-Q+R_p/Q+(1-V)(c_pB_j-A_j)}{1-R_p},
 \quad G_2=-H_1.\qquad\text{(220)}
\end{aligned}
\]\end{minipage}}\par

For \(j=1\) replace these by

\par\noindent\resizebox{\linewidth}{!}{\begin{minipage}{\dimexpr\linewidth+5.77538pt\relax}\[
H_0=\frac{1-R_p/Q}{1-R_p},\quad
 H_1=\frac{\eta(p)Q^{-s}(1-QV)}{1-R_p},\quad
 G_0=\frac{c_pR_p(1-Q^{-1})}{1-R_p},\quad
 G_1=\frac{R_p/Q-QV}{1-R_p},\quad G_2=-H_1.
\]\end{minipage}}\par

At row primes \(\Delta_p=G_p\). The primitive conductor's good-prime exponent is one. Allocate \(\min(k,j-1)\) powers of shift \(Q^k\) to \(h_r\mid\Lambda_u=q_u/q_{\operatorname{rad}(u)}\), the rest to \(h_0\). With \(\delta=2a-1\),

\hypertarget{dr:rowmass}{}

\label{dr:rowmass}

\[
\sum_k|H_{p,k}|Q^{-k/2}Q^{\delta(k-j+1)_+/2}\le1+CQ^{-c_e},
 \quad
 \sum_k|\Delta_{p,k}|Q^{-(k+1)/2}Q^{\delta(k-j+1)_+/2}\ll1.
 \qquad\text{(221)}
\]

For \(j\ge2\), \(G_1\asymp Q\) cancels against \(Q^{-1}\) and its shift lies in \(\Lambda_u\). At \(j=2\), quadratic error is \(Q^{1-a}Q^{-3/2}Q^{\delta/2}=Q^{-1}\). Remaining nontail constant/linear exponents are \(-\delta\) at \(j=2\); \(-3\delta/2,-\delta\) at \(j=3\); \(-3\delta/2\) at \(j=4\); \(-5\delta/2\) at \(j=5\). The explicit \(j=1\) terms give negative powers. At unramified error slots, removing normalizer \(P_i^{z_0-1/2}\) leaves

\[
P_i^{-1/2}\sum_{q_p\asymp P_i}q_p^{-a}\ll P_i^{1/2-a}\le1.
\]

Products of these inequalities and divisor bounds at the row primes prove the complete mass bound

\hypertarget{dr:mass}{}

\label{dr:mass}

\[
\sum_{h_0,h_r}|c_u(h_0,h_r)|h_0^{\delta/2}\ll U^\epsilon.
 \qquad\text{(222)}
\]

Include all local main/error choices after normalizers, monomial shifts and fixed-prime restoration in \(c_u\); keep moving coefficients out of seminorms.

Inverting monomial shift \(h=h_0h_r\) gives \(Y^{-1/2}h^{-1/2}S_{\psi_u}(Y/h)\). Expand fixed Euler deletions, reflect primitively and restore original zeros with bounded fixed Euler mass, only shortening scales. Central amplitude is preserved, giving

\[
\epsilon_u S_{\overline{\psi_u}}
 \left(\mathfrak c_u h/Y;W_1^\vee\right),\quad
 \mathfrak c_u=\frac{3q_{\mathfrak f_u}}{(2\pi)^2}
 \asymp_S q_{\operatorname{rad}(u)},\quad
 \widehat W_1^\vee(v)=\widehat W_1(1-v)\frac{\Gamma(v)}{\Gamma(1-v)}.
\]

Since \(q_{\operatorname{rad}(u)}h_r\le q_u\), dual scale is \(\ll_S(U/Y)h_0\). Annular support forces \(h\le CY\), keeping lengths bounded. Move the reflected Mellin line just left of zero across its simple gamma pole: the profile is bounded at zero and rapidly decreasing at infinity, with summable normalized annuli. Varying conductor changes scale only.

For \(m=\log_UY>1/2\), \(h_0\asymp U^t\), reflected length is \(\le U^{1-m+t+o(1)}\). Take the common-profile scale supremum before the fourth moment. Hölder with count \(U^R\) gives \(U^{1/4+3R/4}\), selected primes give \(U^{-qz(t)/2}\) after fourth root, and \protect\hyperlink{dr:mass}{(222)} gives \(U^{-\delta t/2}\). Thus old capacities

\[
z_O(t)=\max\{0,\min(1,2m-1-2t)/(6\kappa)\},
 \qquad z_A=\frac{2\min(m,1)-1}{6\kappa},
\]

gives

\hypertarget{dr:old-numerator}{}

\label{dr:old-numerator}

\[
\sum_{u\text{ in class}}|\mathcal N_u|
 \ll Y^{-1/2}Z^{\ell(z_0-1/2)+\ell q}
 U^{1/4+3R/4-qz_A/2+\epsilon}(1+T)^A.
 \qquad\text{(223)}
\]

Indeed \(z_A-z_O(t)\le t/(3\kappa)\) and \(-\delta t/2+qt/(6\kappa)\le0\) for \(q\le\delta/2\), \(\kappa\ge7/10\). Use marked moments for positive capacities and unmarked for zero. Bounded-at-zero profiles assign subunit scales length zero.

\hypertarget{dr:stage60}{}

\label{dr:stage60}

\hypertarget{height-order-mellin-continuation-and-the-global-contradiction}{%
\paragraph{14.12 Height order, Mellin continuation, and the global contradiction}\label{height-order-mellin-continuation-and-the-global-contradiction}}

\textbf{Theorem 14.12} (Generic common-reserve return).
\emph{Fix \(87/100<\theta<1\), a real normalization \(k\), and physical
geometry with \(M=1-\ell\), \(0<\ell<1/5\), and
\(k+x/2+(y-x)/12=11/12-\ell/4=\theta\).
Suppose that the complete source and conductor estimates, with one mesh
and all reserves chosen before the primitive target, give (237) with a
common \(\sigma>0\), and that the physical bound (199) gives
\(J_\eta(Z)\ll_{\eta,\epsilon}Z^{\theta-k+\epsilon}\) for every
\(\epsilon>0\). Suppose also that the signal is (203), with the absolute
vertical convergence stated there, and that (201) gives a holomorphic
nonzero numerator on \(\Re s>87/100\). Then \(\beta\le\theta\).}

\emph{Proof.} Suppose \(\mu=\beta-\theta>0\).
Before the target, choose a fraction of feedback margin, small row extension \(\zeta\), moment losses, capacity decrements and amplitude mesh. Obtain a common moment mesh; fix positive distinct slots fine enough for every \(d\ge d_0\), with total \(\ell\), fixing \(\mu_P>0\). Choose contour/detector buffers below remaining margins and \(y/4,h/24,\mu_P,1/20\).

Then fix target, dictionary, \(S\) and finite Sobolev/smooth/moment orders. Maximize degrees and choose \(\tau_\eta>0\), \(T=3Z^{\tau_\eta}\), paying all heights and small relative to \(d_0,\ell_i\) for witnesses/prime derivatives. Independent external coordinates are

\[
\Im(s+z),\qquad\Im z,\qquad\Im w.
\]

The Gaussian decays in the first coordinate and Mellin weights arbitrarily in the other two; allocate each part of the zero-free height buffer. Joining reciprocals stay buffered or global. Before tail-dependent separation, correction is \(\ll Z^B U^\epsilon\) with fixed \(B\), so omitted/joining pieces are \(O_N(Z^{B'}T^{-N})\) with \(B'\) fixed before \(N\). Choose \(N\) last, preserving mesh, slots and real margins. The numerator's absolute-line \(w\) inversion and reflected-profile tails were paid before moments; its height remains independent of witnesses.

The common source-to-probe remainder hypothesis is

\hypertarget{dr:remainder}{}

\label{dr:remainder}

\[
J_\eta(Z)-f_\eta(Z)\ll_\eta Z^{\beta-k-\sigma}.
 \qquad\text{(237)}
\]

The geometry has \(M=1-\ell\) and \(\ell<1/5\), so \protect\hyperlink{dr:physical-bound}{(199)}, divided by its logarithmic normalizer, gives

\[
J_\eta(Z)\ll_{\eta,\epsilon}Z^{\theta-k+\epsilon},\qquad
 k+x/2+(y-x)/12=11/12-\ell/4=\theta.
\]

Take \(\epsilon<\mu/4\), then common \(0<\Delta<\min(\mu/4,\sigma/2)\) with \(\beta-\Delta>87/100\). Thus \(f_\eta\ll_\eta Z^{\beta-k-\Delta}\) at infinity. Shifting \protect\hyperlink{dr:J-f}{(203)} to any \(A>2\), absolute Euler convergence/Gaussian decay give \(f_\eta=O_{\eta,A}(Z^{A-k})\) at zero. Hence

\hypertarget{dr:Mellin}{}

\label{dr:Mellin}

\[
\mathfrak F_\eta(s)=\int_0^\infty f_\eta(Z)Z^{-s+k}\,\frac{dZ}{Z}
 \qquad\text{(238)}
\]

is holomorphic on \(\Re s>\beta-\Delta\), since endpoint bounds remain integrable with every fixed logarithmic power. Fourier inversion in \(\log Z\) on a line right of one gives

\hypertarget{dr:Mellin-identity}{}

\label{dr:Mellin-identity}

\[
\mathfrak F_\eta(s)=e^{(s-5/6)^2}
                      \frac{H_\eta(s)}{L_K^S(s,\eta)}.
 \qquad\text{(239)}
\]

Gaussian/endpoint bounds make both Fourier functions integrable. Meromorphic identity extends \protect\hyperlink{dr:Mellin-identity}{(239)}; by \protect\hyperlink{dr:H-nonzero}{(201)} every denominator zero, of any multiplicity, would be a pole, contradicting holomorphy.

Common \(\Delta\) precedes every primitive target. Supremum approximation supplies a zero with real part \(>\beta-\Delta/2\); its arbitrarily large finite conductor/height affects constants, cutoff and \(\tau_\eta\), but not the gap. This contradiction proves \(\beta\le\theta\). \(\square\)

Apply this theorem separately with the geometry and complete analytic estimates of the first return and the final return. The value of \(\theta\) and the source premises are fixed separately in each application.

\hypertarget{dr:ordinary-transfer}{}

\label{dr:ordinary-transfer}

\hypertarget{primitive-reduction-and-every-presented-dirichlet-character}{%
\paragraph{14.13 Primitive reduction and every presented Dirichlet character}\label{primitive-reduction-and-every-presented-dirichlet-character}}

\textbf{Theorem 14.13} (Ordinary transfer).
\emph{Let \(87/100<\theta<1\). If the full finite-order Hecke family
has \(\beta\le\theta\), then every presented Dirichlet character is
nonvanishing for \(\Re s>\theta\), with its principal pole excluded.}

\emph{Proof.} Let a Hecke character in modulus \(\mathfrak m\) be induced by the primitive \(\eta^*\) of conductor \(\mathfrak f\mid\mathfrak m\). Absolute Euler products on \(\Re s>1\) give

\[
L_{\mathfrak m}(s,\eta)=L(s,\eta^*)
 \prod_{\substack{p\mid\mathfrak m\\p\nmid\mathfrak f}}
                 (1-\eta^*(p)q_p^{-s}).
\]

Continuation extends this identity away from the principal pole. Each deleted factor is nonzero for \(\Re s>0\) since \(|\eta^*(p)q_p^{-s}|<1\). Thus primitive reduction and fixed-\(S\) restoration preserve zeros. Reflected numerator bounds \protect\hyperlink{dr:rowmass}{(221)}--\protect\hyperlink{dr:mass}{(222)} still retain repeated row powers/holes.

For any \(q\ge1\) and complex Dirichlet \(\chi\bmod q\), norm pullback \(\eta_\chi(\mathfrak a)=\chi(N\mathfrak a)\) has finite order in modulus \(q\mathcal O\) and is trivial on units. Let \(\chi_{-3}\) be the field character and present \(\chi\chi_{-3}\) at a common modulus. Euler factors give

\hypertarget{dr:norm-lift}{}

\label{dr:norm-lift}

\[
L_K(s,\eta_\chi)=L(s,\chi)L(s,\chi\chi_{-3}).
 \qquad\text{(240)}
\]

Split primes give two norm-\(p\) factors with \(\chi_{-3}(p)=1\); inert primes give a norm-\(p^2\) factor equal to the product with signs \(\pm\). Prime three gives one factor and \(\chi_{-3}(3)=0\). At \(p\mid q\) all corresponding factors are deleted. This proves \protect\hyperlink{dr:norm-lift}{(240)} absolutely, then by continuation.

For \(\theta<\Re s<1\), both Dirichlet factors are holomorphic, so a zero contradicts Hecke nonvanishing. For \(\Re s\ge1\) use ordinary Dirichlet nonvanishing, excepting the principal pole; treat nonprincipal \(s=1\) directly even if its companion is principal. Thus every presented modulus and finite height satisfy

\[
\Re s>\theta,\qquad
 \neg(\chi=1\ \hbox{and}\ s=1)
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad L(s,\chi)\ne0.
\]

Modulus-one principal gives zeta. The conclusion uses the strict half-plane; it asserts nothing on its boundary. \(\square\)
